# RabbleBound - the record

> **Everything in this file is history, not instructions.** It exists because the reasoning behind
> a past decision is the most expensive thing to reconstruct and the cheapest thing to keep - but
> nothing here is a task, and many of its numbers were true only on the day they were written.
>
> **Changelog rows are a historical record: the numbers inside them describe what a build did at
> the time and are deliberately not updated.** [`01_GAME_CONCEPT.md`](01_GAME_CONCEPT.md) and the
> running build hold what is true now. **When the record and the build disagree, the build wins.**
>
> *Split out of [`00_PLAN_AND_BACKLOG.md`](00_PLAN_AND_BACKLOG.md) on 2026-07-31 at the user's
> request - the working file keeps only what a session can act on; everything shipped or
> superseded lands here. When an active entry is built: move its full text here, leave its row in
> the Done table there.*

**Contents:** [the build log](#the-build-log--one-row-per-sub-build) ·
[built backlog entries in full](archive/CHANGELOG_BUILT_ENTRY_TEXT.md) ·
[the outside review's P0 list](#codex-packet--status) ·
[playtest snapshots](#snapshots-kept-for-the-reasoning) ·
[the original phase plan](#the-original-phase-plan)

| 8f.200 | **#172 - ✎ TEXT: EVERY WORD ON EVERY SCREEN IS EDITABLE IN DEV.MODE, AND THE EDIT IS A MESSAGE, NOT A PATCH.** *(User, 2026-08-16: "add a tool in dev mod, so if i togled it on i can eddit text on any screen. I have options: 1) Delete 2) Change + save 3) Add note to AI (to update in future). The ideas - so i can do fast update in the file on my laptop (it could work only for offline version) and then you check it, add your updates where needed and then push. Make it simple and easy to work from the same interface in deb mode".)* **A fifth rung on the tester stack** (top:224, the 30px pitch), gated on `TEST.on` like LINT and WIPE, disarmed by DEV.MODE going off under it - the same authority-hands-back rule as AUTO, because an armed interceptor with no visible switch would eat every click on the page. ⚑ **THE LEDGER IS THE DELIVERABLE AND THE LIVE CHANGE IS ONLY A PREVIEW.** Nearly every word on screen is painted out of the string tables by a renderer that repaints on the next tick, so a DOM patch cannot survive and does not try to: what is SAVED (`gt_tedit_v1`) is `{when, screen, op, orig, to, note, where, ctx}` - the exact original text, which is greppable in THIS file, plus a breadcrumb anchored on the first id up the tree as a hint, never a selector to re-apply by. Proved in the drive: a preview of a menu label evaporated when the TEST toggle redrew the front door, and the ledger kept the record - that IS the design. **⇩ EXPORT** wraps the ledger in JSON with its own how-to-apply line; DOWNLOAD works on the laptop's file:// and localhost builds, **⧉ COPY and the visible textarea exist because the published artifact's sandbox eats programmatic downloads** - the same wall that created `ask()`. ⚑ **ONE CAPTURE LISTENER ON `document` AND THE GAME NEVER SEES THE CLICK**: every game handler is an onclick property, so capture runs first and `stopImmediatePropagation` starves them all; `mousedown` is swallowed too or the map pan would still act under the pick. The chrome that may never be eaten is one exemption list (`TE_EXEMPT`): the tool itself, the tester stack, ♪/?/☰ - **and `#askWrap`, found by reasoning before it shipped: CLEAR confirms through `ask()`, and a dialog whose buttons are intercepted is a dialog that cannot be answered while armed.** The price - ask() prose is not editable in place - is written at the list. ⚑ **THE UNIT OF AN EDIT IS THE TEXT NODE UNDER THE CURSOR** (`caretRangeFromPoint`), not the element: a road door is one button holding a label, a price line and a glyph, and an edit to the label must not carry the receipt with it. Element fallback when the point sits in none. `gt_tedit_v1` joins `gt_test` in the WIPE HISTORY keep-list: the developer's working material is not the player's history. ⛔ **AND THE FILE'S OLDEST TRAP FIRED ON THIS ENTRY, INSIDE THE VERY COMMENT WARNING ABOUT SCALE VARS**: the first draft named two CSS var families as star-after-slash, which is a comment terminator - the sequence #98's `#cssTail` note says never to write - and it silently ate the `#teditBtn` base rule. `__cssTail` stayed true (the parser recovered at the next brace), the DOM checks stayed green (they read `classList`, not computed style), and the button quietly sat in static flow at top-left, visible to EVERYBODY with no dev mode. Caught only by the real-viewport drive reading a rect that could not be right (x=0 for a right:10 button). The lesson joins #163's: **a CSS failure is silent and looks like it is working; read the COMPUTED value, not the class list.** Verified in the running build twice over, because the pane's viewport is 0x0 and `caretRangeFromPoint` needs a real one: **in the pane** (DOM path) - all three ops recorded, empty-note guard held, ledger newest-first with per-row ✕, reload persistence, WIPE keep-list, CLEAR answered through ask() while armed, `LINT()` 0 findings, no console errors; **in headless Chrome through `eyes.py`** (#162's driver, real 1280x800) - hover ring true, **picked unit = TEXT NODE**, node-tight preview landed, the game did not act (screen stayed `menu` under an intercepted click on its own door), export JSON present. **Eye check `shots/172_text_edit.html`**, three live captures. The apply half of the loop is a session's work, not the tool's: find each `orig`, land it under the event-card and voice rules, run LINT, push. | ✅ |

So they stick together better".)* ⛔ **THE LUNGE HAS NEVER ONCE BEEN SEEN, AND THAT IS THE THIRD REPORT OF IT.** #87 built it, #165 read the same complaint as *too small* and gave the recoil a second beat plus 2px - and neither could work, because `clickHex` does `strike(...)` and then `B.sel=null;B.reach=null;render()` on the very next statement, and `render()` does `g.innerHTML=''`. The class was going onto a node that left the document before its first frame painted. Everything in `#bFx` survived - flash, sparks, blood, the floating number - because #bFx is not rebuilt, **which is exactly why the burst looked complete and the one missing part read as too quiet rather than as absent.** The pose is parked on `B.anim` now, the way `B.gore`, `B.peek`, `B.spot` and `B.walk` already are, and re-applied at the sprite's rebuild with a **negative `animation-delay` so it resumes rather than restarting** - without that, a body caught by three renders stutters at the top of the keyframe three times. `poseFx()` is the one door; five hand-rolled `classList.add` sites went through it. **AND THE SECOND KNOB IS `--hitp`, NOT `--pace`.** `--pace` is the battle clock (the AUTO tick, the walk, the stagger between two plates of an oath), so turning it up to see a sword would also slow the hand-over; the impact is fifteen durations already tuned against each other, so they move together at **base × --pace × --hitp**, 2.5 - the middle of the asked band. Every `setTimeout` that strips an impact class moved to `hitPaced` in the same edit. The caption got `--fxp` and a **reshaped** keyframe rather than a stretched one: the old `rise` climbed 26px at a constant rate, so doubling it doubled the time spent drifting, which is the state a number is hardest to read in. It now climbs 14px in the first fifth and **hangs for two thirds of its life**. ⚑ **AND #81's OWN REMAINDER PAID ITSELF OFF.** The arrow was pinned at 420ms *"deliberately not paced"* because the recoil it causes ended at 595 and a slower shot would land after its own effect; at ×2.5 the recoil runs 1.9s, so 760ms lands two fifths INTO the reel - the right way round for the first time. ⛔ **A BODY IS SOMETHING YOU LOOK AT, NOT SOMETHING YOU CLICK.** `.hin` is 33×37 and a sprite is 46-56 tall and up to 48 wide, so every token overhangs its tile - and rows overlap by 10px with the front row's DOM last, so the front sprite won the hit test over everything it covered. The ogre's picture owned a share of the six hexes around him, which are the hexes you walk to get behind him. `pointer-events:none` on `.usp` and `.ubars`; the hex button and #171's clipped `.aimpad` still cover exactly the ground the body stands on. **This is #171's own argument arriving at the other half**: that entry measured the PAD stealing 28 points and never looked at the sprite standing on it. Measured, 145 on-screen hexes × 9 probe points, headless 1280×800: **`img.usp` took 49 points off hexes that were not its own and the ogre's ring lost 12 of 54; both are 0 after one line.** ⛔ **THE ZONE OF CONTROL BECAME THE FRONT ARC, AND THE RULE MOVED BEFORE THE PICTURE DID.** The red edge was already drawing exactly what `partingShots` charged, so painting three hexes while the swing still fired from six would have been a readout printing what the till does not take. `controllers()` is a **second** function and never a change to `engagers` - that one answers *how many bodies have a hand on this one* for the surrounded bonus, the morale situation, the swarm and line counts, the spear wall and both AI brains, and a body at your back is still crowding you even though it cannot stop you leaving. Merging them would be the `walkable`/`passable` failure again. FRONT + both SHOULDERS, off `arcFrom` (the one place sector arithmetic lives), so a `noArc` body keeps all six for free and correctly. **Four sites must agree and all four moved**: the swing, the red edge, the UNDO snapshot's `clean` prediction, and DISENGAGE's card gate. ⚑ **AND THE HEX GRID HAS BEEN SHEARED SINCE BEFORE #105.** The column pitch is 38 and a hexagon's half-width is 18.5, so an odd row nests when its left edge sits at **19**; it was written `27`. #105 point 2 found this exact discrepancy, wrote *"a half-pitch 19 is what the geometry SAYS it should be and is not what the DOM does"*, and then made the ground PAINTER follow the DOM - which kept the terrain under the tiles and left the tiles wrong. Vertically it was always right (42−10 = 32 = 0.75×42), which is why it read as scatter rather than as a shear. `render()` reads `HEXOFF` now instead of a second literal. **Verified**: the odd row's apex against the valley between the two hexes above it, **8.00px off on HEAD → 0.00px** · the painted ground 0px drift on five hexes across the board · a bare checkered lattice cropped from both builds, before a stepped mess and after a honeycomb · `LINT()` **0** · the front arc exactly **3 of 6** neighbours · the struck cursor with **0 false positives and 0 false negatives** against a bow's range-5 footprint, and the same `3 3` hotspot so the pointer cannot jump on the boundary · on `mouseenter`, **63 hexes tinted and `#bRead` still closed** · the lunge measured **after** render at `matrix(1.068, tx 12.62)`, the peak of its own keyframe, settling to identity and clearing · seven blows on one hex capped at 6 and **ran into two neighbours** · the badge reading `⊛` with the count only in `Surrounded ×4 · …` · overflow probe identical to HEAD (`#bField` 40×47, `#bLog` 0×16, both pre-existing) · the three map counters 0/0/0 · **ARENA `four` vs `brigand`, n=100 both builds: 66/100 against HEAD's 67/100**, so the zone-of-control change is balance-neutral - the n=40 run that read as a 12-point drop was noise, which is #89's own lesson about small samples arriving again. | ✅ |

30 walls measured, all 1), level 0, `xp` 0, price ×.75, rags and a cudgel (START_GEAR's own fallback), trait rolled by race (`traitFits` already drops class-gated traits on a class it does not know), `○` not the ✦ fallback; | 8f.203 | **#175 - THE SHAKE COMES BACK A QUARTER, AND FOUR THINGS HAPPENING AT ONCE STOP PRINTING THROUGH EACH OTHER.** *(User, 2026-08-17, the morning after #173 shipped: "the animation of icons (shaking) after strike now too long, true - you can make them 25% faster then now" · "When a couple effects together (like backstab and damage) - they are one on top of each other. Make them nicer - more separate".)* ⚑ **THE FIRST HALF IS ONE CHARACTER, AND THAT IS THE ENTIRE ARGUMENT FOR #173's SECOND KNOB.** `--hitp` 2.5 → **1.875** moves all fifteen impact durations together: the recoil 1.93s → **1.44s**, the lunge 1.49s → **1.12s**, both still nearly double the 0.6s they ran at before #173. Had the fifteen been tuned individually, as they were before, this note would have been fifteen edits and a fortnight of drift. ⚠ **The arrow is the one thing re-checked**: `animShot`'s flight is capped at 760ms and deliberately unpaced because its job is to land INSIDE the recoil it causes, and at 1.875 the recoil is still 1.44s, so the margin holds - below about 1.2 the ordering inverts and #81's remainder comes back. ⛔ **AND THE SECOND HALF WAS THREE DEFECTS WEARING ONE COMPLAINT.** `fx()` pinned every caption to `left = p.x+26, top = p.y+12`, so a landed blow that produces BACKSTAB!, the hitpoint figure, the armour figure and a morale pip printed all four at the same point - and #173's longer hold made it worse rather than better, because they now sat on top of one another for three and a half seconds instead of flashing past. The fix is a **queue per hex, worked out at the one door** rather than nudged at eleven call sites, because only the hex knows how many lines are already climbing off it; the slot is the count of live captions (expiry stamps, so a faded line frees its row) and the order is the call order, which puts BACKSTAB! on top and the figure it earned underneath. **Then the first cut broke twice and only the rendered rectangle found either.** (1) It kept `small ? 2 : 12` as the base and added the row on top, so the ladder collapsed the moment the sizes mixed - measured on a real backstab, two lines 15px apart and the next pair **5px** apart, which reads worse than the pile it replaced because it looks deliberate. **The slot decides the line; the class decides only the size.** (2) The row was 18 and the boxes were 20, because the display face at `--fs5` takes a ~20px box on default leading - every figure in the probe said 17px of separation and `getBoundingClientRect()` said the glyphs overlapped by 3. `.fx` declares `line-height:1` now, the same load-bearing declaration `.hodds` and `.hdmg` already carry. ⛔ **AND `forwards` BECAME `both`, WHICH IS THE BUG THE STAGGER CREATED.** `forwards` fills only the END of a run, so a caption sitting out its `animation-delay` was drawn in its unanimated state - full opacity, at rest - and snapped to invisible the instant its turn came: three lines appeared together and two of them blinked. Nothing was describing what an animation looks like before it starts. ⚠ **The stagger reads `paced` and NOT `fxPaced`**, and that is the difference between a sequence and a queue: `--fxp` is how long a line is READ for, and multiplying the gap by it too put the fourth caption 1.4s behind the blow on top of its own 3.5s life. A beat between two events belongs on the battle clock like every other beat. **Verified in the running build, headless at 1280x800, on a forced backstab through `clickHex` (the real path - calling `strike()` direct leaves the aiming furniture up and the picture lies)**: four captions, **0 overlapping pairs**, smallest gap **3.0px**, boxes 15/15/12/12, delays 0/123/245/368ms so the whole sequence opens inside four tenths of a second; the board measured as NOT moving under them (target hex top 120.6, lines at 138.6/156.6/174.6/192.6); `LINT()` **0**; the three map counters 0/0/0; no font under the 10px floor; overflow unchanged. | ✅ |

Everything after the first battle was `capBalloon` - a 288px parchment comic bubble hung off whichever hex the Captain happened to be standing on, for 2.2 seconds, while the enemy carried on moving underneath it. **That is his "random places" and his "strange" in one sentence, and both are the same defect: the balloon anchored to the SPEAKER and not to what he was speaking ABOUT, and it never took the lock.** So `.ccall` and `capBalloon()` are **deleted** (50 lines of CSS for a box, a face, a name, a two-triangle tail, a left/right flip and a pop-in), and `tutShow`'s body is lifted into **`tutPaint(head,text,anchor,tier,skip)`** with the `TUTSTEPS` lookup taken off the front, so a lesson and a Captain's line are now literally one code path: same card, same face, same four-way placement, same dim, same lock, same CLICK TO GO ON. ⚑ **Nothing new was written to make the blocking happen - `B.tutLock` is #156's, and `nextTurn` has held on it since then.** What survived the balloon is the tier hairline (`teach`/`call`/`heart`/`learn`, re-scoped to `#tutCard` so it beats the plain border rule) and `.capskip`, which is **removed and rebuilt every paint** because the card is reused for the life of the session and a skip button left behind by a lesson would sit on the next CALL. ⚠ `pointer-events:auto` did NOT come with it: it existed because `#bFx` is `pointer-events:none`, and `#tutDim` is a live click surface already. **The portrait is `capPerson()` through `faceOf()`, set EVERY show and not at `tutEl()` time** - the card is built lazily on the first lesson and then reused forever, so a face written at construction would be whoever was Captain the first time the tutorial ever ran and would survive the player starting a new company. It is an `<img>` and not a `<canvas>` for `capBalloon`'s own reason: `shotBoard()` serialises overlays through `innerHTML` and a canvas's pixels do not survive that. ⚑ **NOT ONE WORD OF ANY STEP CHANGED, WHICH IS THE POINT** - the text was already second-person and already read as somebody telling you something; it simply had nobody's mouth on it. `B.cap.el` is cleared in `tutNext` and `tutClose` and nowhere else, those being the only two doors the dim sheet goes down through. **THE MUTTER** is the first note: `BARKS` keyed by **race and not class**, because class is a job and this is not about the job - the ratkin loud and hungry, the ogre slow and literal, the human tired and professional. His own *"Let me see his guts."* is canon. ⛔ **The test for whether a line belongs there is whether you could ACT on it**: everything in `CAPLINES` is an instruction with a body attached, and a mutter names nobody, states no ground, carries no number and asks for nothing - which is also why it is **not mirrored into `#bLog`**, where sixty rows of noise would sit on the one channel the fight is re-read from. ⛔ **RARITY IS THE WHOLE FEATURE AND A BUDGET ALONE IS NOT RARITY, IT IS A QUEUE.** Measured on a stub rig against a benchmark-shaped fight (9 rounds, ~7 attacks each, **4000 fights**): a cap of four with one-per-round spent **all four in 99% of fights and spent them in rounds 1-4**, after which the register went silent - the cap was binding, the odds were doing nothing, and *"в рандомный момент"* had become a fixed opening chorus. **The knob is `BARK_GAP=3` rounds of silence, not `BARK_MAX=4`**, which is only there to stop a 15-round boss becoming a conversation; shipped figures measure **mean 2.74 lines a fight (3 in 75%, 2 in 25%) and roughly even odds across all nine rounds**. `BARK_ODDS{kill:.55, low:.6, hurt:.35, hit:.04}` rank what is worth hearing rather than setting rarity: an ordinary landed blow is by far the commonest thing that happens, so at .12 it won a third of all lines by volume and starved the kill, and at **.04** the split is **hurt 45% · low 21% · hit 18% · kill 16%**, which puts *"когда ты получаешь много урона"* at the top where he put it. The four moments are ranked **once**, in `strike()`, as a chain of else-ifs rather than four sites that each know about it - because a blow that put somebody on the ground would otherwise have spent the round's one line, three hundred lines earlier, on being hit. ⛔ **`!u.monster` AND NOT A RACE TEST**: `build()` derives race from kind and a DOG comes out `race:'ratkin'`, so a race test would have had the pack shouting *"Let me see his guts."* One personality overrules the race and there is exactly one - a body with `pacifist` already pays 18 morale of its own per kill, so `BARK_SORRY` replaces the boast. ⛔ **AND THE OFFSET WAS THE ONE THING THE BUILDING SESSION LEFT UNVERIFIED, AND IT WAS WRONG.** The shipped `hexSpan()*0.9` was flagged in its own comment as *"the first draft and the number to check in the running build"*; driven at the CLOSE stop it printed **through the head of every ogre and most humans**, because the height a head sits above that anchor is not one number but three - **ogre 1.09 × hexSpan · human 0.88-0.99 · ratkin 0.36-0.67** - and a constant raised to clear the ogre leaves a ratkin muttering at a cloud a hex and a half over its own head. **This is #157 exactly** (a body's furniture is anchored to `--head` for the same reason) **and the answer is the same one: ask the sprite.** `up` is now the speaker's own `img.usp` rect plus **22**, and ⛔ **the margin is sized by the KEYFRAMES and not by eye**: `mut` runs +6px at 0% to −10px at 100%, so a line measured at rest is six pixels higher than the frame it is born on - the first fix used +14, which reads as 4px of clearance standing still and **−2 on the opening frame**, through the head, faintly, every single time. 22 is 12 at rest and 6 at the worst frame. The off-top flip reads `foot` off the same rect, because the old `up*0.6` was calibrated when `up` was a flat 0.9 of a hex and now drops an ogre's line 68px into whatever stands below him. **THE PERSONALITIES**: `TRAITSHORT` is **deleted** - a second table keyed by the same twenty keys, written in MOOD where the line has to be written in MECHANICS (*"Finishes the wounded."* for a damage bracket, *"Old hand. Bad knees."* for +4/+4/−1/−8), so a player reading the muster could not tell what any of them DID. It is **derived** now from two fields every personality must declare, joined on `·`, so it cannot drift from the numbers and a new trait cannot forget to have one; the flavour paragraph is untouched and is still what the hover shows. **Four of the twenty-one had no downside at all** and the register has carried *"there are no good or bad traits, there are personalities: if you can name the best one, they are traits again"* in a comment since #145 - a comment cannot fail, so **`LINT` 12 can**. Five new ones, each a rule the player has to place a body around rather than a stat line with a name on it, and ⚠ **every one wired through a hook that already existed** - the aura pass, the wage line, `hitBreakdown`, `dmgMul`, the AI's soft-target term - which is the test a sixth should also pass: **`frightening`** (−6 morale to everyone within 2 hexes, yours included, +5 MOR), **`indebted`** (half wages via a `wage` multiplier in `wageOf`, a missed payday twice as hard - the first personality about the pay-chest rather than the field), **`slowstart`** (−10 to hit rounds 1-2, +8 from round 4, with round 3 neutral so it does not read as a switch nobody saw coming), **`grudge`** (+20% against whichever body last drew their blood, −6 against everything else; `grudgeOn` written in `strike()` where the blood is, and only on `hpD>0` because a swing the harness ate is not a thing they remember), **`loud`** (+4 to hit for every ally adjacent, read off the NEIGHBOURS so the shouter gets nothing from themselves, paid for by **−24 in `targetScore`** - one site, so both brains and AUTO inherit it without being taught). `counter` also gained the half it never paid for (−8 on a turn it moved) and `thriceSoft` is `tr()`-only so the bracket is never confused with BUTCHER, a perk somebody spent a point on. **Verified**: `LINT()` **0** (including new rules 11 and 12) · a full fight driven end to end, two mutters at rounds 1 and 4, exactly the gap · the tutorial card confirmed live with `face:true`, `alt:'You "Cold Hands"'`, tier `learn` and **`B.tutLock===true`** · the mutter measured against every body on the board at **all three camera stops with the animation pinned to its worst frame: 0 through-sprites**, and the only invisible lines belong to bodies whose sprites are themselves off-camera · intent glyphs 0 missing, `?` still 1 · the three map counters **0/0/0** · no font under the 10px floor · no `c:` carrying a figure · no console errors · em dashes in the file went **1333 → 1321**. | ✅ |

Кип ит шарп и лесс текст" · (3) "На карту на некторых боях добавить хазард зон - огонь. Наносящий урон когда в нем и 'подпаливающий' проходящих персонажей. Иногда возникает на 'пустынной карте' и может где-то еще (посмотри, где актуально добавить)" · (4) "Добавить опцию за самоцвет можна в инвентаре карточке персонажа поднять уровень на 1. Работает только раз за катку" · (5) "Если у тебя было два промаха подряд - то третий - обязательно попадание (для того, чтобы не фрустрировать игрока)" · (6) "Добавить 2 фразочки на англ от существ: Ю агли енд ю нов ит - фром ретлинг, когда кого-то убил · Айм ловинг ит (eat) - огр кого-то убил".)* **(1) THE CHEER.** `goalTick`'s payout was a 41-word paragraph in the road-news strip, which on a phone lay across the middle of the map; it is *"✦ SECOND OBJECTIVE DONE · morale +10 · more of you at the fire."* now, and `say2()` grew an optional `tone` so the strip wears **`.cheer`** for it - gold accent edge (ui-scales §2: gold selects and is off the structural scale), upright mono at `--fs2`, cleared on the next plain line. Nine words and the receipt. **(2) "ONE OF US".** `reinforce()`, called from `checkEnd` after both endings and before the mercy branch: when the company's OWN standing bodies (side you, not ally, not pet) are two or fewer, a foe still stands, and **somebody of yours has already gone down or run in this fight** (his *"остались"* - a company that walked in as two is not rescued at the first bell), the road rolls **`REINF_ODDS=.5` once a fight**. On a pass one of the sources this run has earned sends two bodies to the nearest free hexes beside a survivor (`freeNear`, three rings out, never a burning hex), through `besideYou()`, and each mutters *"One of us"* straight through `mutter()` - not `bark()`, because this is the arrival's line and not the register's roll. **The Circle** (the Captain wears the ring, `p.mark==='circle'`) sends two ring-eyed strangers out of the new `REINF_T`, a knife and a club; **the wedding** sends two clan-cousins out of `CLASH_T` (a spear and a sling, renamed), if either race door was taken - both doors now carry **`kin:'wedding'`** and `pickChoice` writes `G.kin`, saying nothing on the card because a card may not promise a rescue that is a coin. ⚑ **It is luck and the line says so**: *"Luck. Two ring-eyed strangers were on this road, and they have seen your face."* ⛔ **Once per source per run** (`G.reinf`, saved in flags), **not at their own fire** (no Circle men in the Circle fight, no cousins at the wedding you are cutting down), **not in the brawl, not on the practice field**. Measured with the harness, the pair standing where two of six had fallen: **the Broken Men 2v6 went from 1/3 without them to 5/5 won or mercy with them**, and the Snare 2v10 stays lost either way, which is the right shape for a rescue - it turns a fight you were losing, not the one you were never going to win. **(3) EMBER, THE GROUND THAT IS ON FIRE.** A new WALKABLE terrain kind, deliberately not the camp's `fire` (the pit nobody stands in) for the reason `tree` and `rock` stay apart: one word, one rule. It earns a word by #61's test because it has one: **costs 2 to enter** (`mcost`, so both brains route round it and a short cut is paid for) · a body that **enters or crosses** one - walked (`walkTo`, the one door every mover uses, so the click, both brains, the pet, the kite and the flee all pay it), shoved, hooked, dragged or run through while routed - is **BURNING: `EMBER_HIT` 6 off the body at once, no armour**, clock `BURN_TURNS` 2 · a burning body takes **`BURN_TICK` 4 at the top of each of its own turns** until the clock is out; standing IN it at the top of a turn is the entry hit again and the clock reset · **water puts it out** (ending a walk on marsh or deep clears the clock). `envHurt()` is the ground's own damage door - no attacker, no arc, and the fall goes through the same two doors `strike()` uses (roster DOWNED, everybody else DEAD, grief to their side); ⚠ **inside `strike()` it DEFERS the fall** (`ignite(d,why,true)`), because the shove and the drag sit ABOVE strike's own `hp<=0` block, which credits the kill to the shover - falling twice would drop the body twice and say so twice. **It does not spread and it does not go out**: a fire that walked would be a second AI. Both brains: `emberPen()` (+14, two hexes and a bit in the scorers' unit) added at **all six standing-spot scorers** - aiTurn's main and fallback, autoStep's shooter, melee and fallback - so nothing ENDS on it when any other hex reaches the target, and it still crosses when that is the only way. Drawn by `lightEmbers()` after the flowers: **one roll for whether this board carries any (`blazeOdds`, taken even when it fails so the rest of the ground is stable per seed), then `blaze` patches of two to four dry hexes each**, off both deployment ends, never on a reserved hex, never touching another patch, field only (fire does not sit on water). **`rocky` carries `blaze:2, blazeOdds:.5`** - his "пустынная карта" is the dust ridge, so the Steading-Line, the Sling-Line and the Thing in Armour - and **`forest` `blaze:1, blazeOdds:.35`** as the "where else", because a wood on fire is a wood you notice; nothing hand-built (camp, outpost, tavern) and not the pinned Fen-Mother. Painted flat under the body (a scorch, six tongues of 6-13, the pit's own warm spill at 38) - the first cut at five tongues of 4-10 read as sparks in the grass on the shot; a `burning` STATUS (🔥, a clock, the note quoting the same constant the tick charges); the hex title states the rule and the way out. Measured over 24 rocky/forest boards: **ember on 10 of them, 4 fights where somebody was lit, one put out in the water, no error, `terrainCheck()` ok on every board that carried it**. **(4) A GEM FOR A LEVEL.** The company sheet's ledger grows a second `.iaid` button under the medicine chest, on the chest's exact shape: *"◈ Cut a gem for a level · goes up one level · costs 1 gem · ONE of these a run"*, greyed with *(you have none)* while the pouch is empty and **shown while unspent** (a button that appears only once you hold a gem is #156's built-and-invisible shape), hidden once spent or at the cap. `ask()` confirms, one gem off `G.camp.gems`, **`levelUp(p)`** so the level pays exactly what a fought one pays (perk, stat, or the level-0 stranger's trade), `xp` floored to the new level's threshold so the ring reads a fresh level and not a negative, `G.gemLevel=p.id` in flags, a memory on the body. Verified live: level 1→2, xp 100→250, gems 1→0, one perk point, button gone. **(5) THE SURE HAND.** `a.missRun` is the attacker's CONSECUTIVE streak, kept in `strike()` beside the fight-total `misses` the ogre's rage reads (two counters, two questions - the file's own `walkable` rule): a miss adds one and floats *NEXT ONE LANDS* on the second, a landed blow zeroes it. ⛔ **Decided in `hitBreakdown` and nowhere else** (#46's rule about where a knob goes): `total` is **100** with a `sure` flag and a *"two wide, this one lands"* part when `SURE_AFTER` (2) is reached, so the aiming card prints 100% with a **sure hand** row saying why, the roll in strike cannot miss, and AUTO spends the sure swing on purpose - one place, three readers. **The player's side only** (`side==='you'`, allies included, because the reason is the player's frustration and a guaranteed enemy blow is the same feeling from the other chair). Measured, log-parsed over 36 fights each way against HEAD in a second tab: **your side's hit rate 67.5% → 72.3%, 62 sure swings in 36 fights**; the enemy's 64.9 → 60.3 moved without a mechanism of its own, so it is read as the mix of who was swinging in shorter fights rather than as a change. Verified live: base 71% → 100% with the streak set, ten forced swings ten landed, streak 0 after. **(6) THE TWO MUTTERS**: `BARKS.ratkin.kill` gains *"You ugly and you know it."* and `BARKS.ogre.kill` *"I'm loving it."* (his *(eat)* - the ogre means it about the body). ⚠ The ratkin line is **six words**, one over LINT 11's ceiling, and it is kept verbatim because a user's own line is canon; at 25 characters it is exactly the width of *"Nothing personal, friend."*, which is what the rule was protecting, so **LINT 11's bound is six now** with the reason on it. Also: `autoEnd` hands the turn on when the acting body has fallen on its own turn (a parting swing on the way, the fire it walked through) - a downed unit with two actions left was a turn nothing could spend; `routStep` stops hopping a downed body. **Verified**: `LINT()` **0** · the eight fights plus wedding and hold complete, no errors, HEAD in a second tab for every measurement above · the ui-scales counters unchanged (0 literal font sizes, 64px alone, 0 edge hexes) · no em dash in any added line · `shots/186_ember_board.html` and its headless capture for the eye check. | ✅ |
| 8f.207 | **#187 - THE AFTER-BATTLE SCREEN ON THE APPROVED HYBRID: AN EMBLEM, RAYS, ONE META LINE, FOUR CARDS ACROSS, AND A HAUL THAT COMES ON A TRAY.** *(User, 2026-08-18, handing over `CLAUDE_AFTERMATH_IMPLEMENTATION.md` written against a mockup made outside the repo: **"Это не приглашение заново придумывать экран. Композиция уже утверждена."** The brief takes the COMPOSITION of an earlier pass - festive but grim header, emblem over the company name, one compact metadata line, prose as a narrow centred column - and the MATERIAL of a later one - layered dark, thin brass edges, soft inner light, receipt tiles - and it is explicit about the seam: everything below the `TAKEN OFF THE FIELD` heading wears the newer treatment.)* ⛔ **NOT ONE NUMBER, PAYMENT OR STRING OF PROSE CHANGED, AND THAT WAS THE POINT.** `takeLoot`, `payFightXP`, `foeReport`, the `AFTER` and `LOOT` tables, `G.clashHaul`, `AFTER.mor`, the mercy branch and `finish()` are untouched; #122's data model, #137's chips-are-built-beside-the-payment rule, #159's mercy screen and #174's ✦ all still hold. This is CSS plus the markup hanging on it. **THE HEADER.** `.amEmb` is a 46px ring with the ⚔ in it and two hairlines running out of its middle, over the existing `.abco` company name, a new `.amTitle` (`--fs8`, the size `.pcard h1` already was, as its own class so the mercy screen's h1 stays plain), and one meta line. ⚑ **The rays are three CSS gradients on the card's own background** - a radial glow, a `repeating-conic-gradient` of 7-degree spokes and a vertical fade to `--g0` - because an image would be a network fetch and the single-file build is the one thing this project cannot trade away. ⚠ **`background-attachment` stays at its default `scroll`**, which on an `overflow:auto` box pins the rays to the card's top edge instead of letting them ride up with the prose. **THE META LINE** is `VICTORY · N ROUNDS · N DOWN · X OF Y CARRIED OFF`, and every figure on it was already on the screen. ⚠ **`VICTORY` is honest here and only here**: `consequences()` is reached from `toSpoils` and the two cards that route through it, all of them won fights, and a beating goes to `toBeaten()` which never sees this line. **THE CREW GRID IS FOUR ACROSS** and `repeat(auto-fit,minmax(160px,1fr))` rather than `repeat(4,...)`, because the roster is not always four: 752px of card gives 181px a card at four (725/4, and five would need 143), and `auto-fit` fills the row for a company of three instead of leaving a hole. ⚠ **THE STAGE IS SCALED AND NEVER REFLOWED** (`fit()`), so the brief's 4 → 2x2 → 1 responsive ladder is not something this build can express: a phone gets this same grid shrunk, which is the answer that predates the screen. **The card is a grid** - portrait 40x46 spanning the first column, name and role in the second, the four numbers across the full width beneath. ⚑ **The numbers span the card, which is the one layout divergence from the mockup, and it is arithmetic**: the mockup's card is 226px wide (990px shell), this one is 181px (800px card), and the four chips want ~120px of the 111px the name column would leave them. Full width gives them 163 and costs no height, because the portrait is taller than the name and role. ⚠ **Tracking decided whether the ROLE wraps**: "BATTLE-MAGE · HUMAN" is 19 glyphs, mono at the 10px floor is 6px an advance, so `.04em` puts the line at 122 and `.02em` at 118 in a 119px column - 4px, and it is 13px of grid height on every card in the company. **INJURY KEPT THE OLD TREATMENT AND GAINED THE STRIP**, per the brief's *"Injury должен сохранить выразительность старой версии... Не заменять это одной тонкой красной обводкой"*: #122's six blood radials stay, the diagonal smear from the mockup joins them, and the red status strip runs to the card's own edges by cancelling its padding with a matching negative margin, written as one token (`--p4`) three times so they cannot drift. ⛔ **AND THE PROMOTION RIBBON LOST TO THE OVERFLOW COUNTER, WHICH IS THE ONE PLACE THIS DEPARTS FROM THE APPROVED PICTURE.** A corner ribbon only reads as one if the corner CROPS it, and cropping is layout overflow: the mockup's geometry measures `.abcrew w23` on the ui-scales §5 counter for every promoted body, forever, on the one card in the game whose job is to be checked for clipped text. ⚠ **`overflow:clip` does not help** - Chrome reports the overflow region either way, measured rather than assumed - and only LEFT and TOP overflow is free, which would have put the ribbon across the portrait's face. Sized flush instead (right:-7px, top:31px, being a 104x16 strip's turned bounding box pinned into the corner) the numbers are honest and it stops looking like a ribbon: with nothing cutting its ends it reads as a stick lying across the name, and covers the name. **So the mark went back to the top-left corner where #122 has had it since the first slice**, and grew from a bare ▲ to `▲ N` with the level on it - the brief's own words are *"компактная ribbon/badge"*, and a badge is compact, adds no height, covers no name, and is legible at the floor, which a rotated 10px string is not. ⚑ **The level NUMBER now appears exactly once**: the badge says what happened, the line under it keeps *"spend it on the sheet"*, which is the half the player has to act on. **THE HAUL COMES ON A TRAY.** `.amTray` holds the chips and the flavour line as siblings, so the answer to *what did that fight pay* is one object rather than a row with a caption under it, and the prose sits BESIDE the numbers behind a hairline instead of below them - the brief's *"Flavour line остаётся отдельной приглушённой строкой, не смешанной с цифрами выплаты"*. All three cases survive unchanged: gear keeps the strip with the reserved art square, resources keep chips off `haul.fx`, and a row with neither still falls back to its own line rather than rendering an empty box. ⛔ **THE TILE RESTYLE IS SCOPED TO `#spoils` AND THAT IS LOAD-BEARING**: `.fxc`/`.fxi`/`.fxn`/`.abfx` are shared with every event card since #143 and with the tavern receipt, so the bigger number (`--fs8`), the round medallion icon and the corner rivets are all behind the screen's id. **Verified by driving a road card on both builds**: identical padding, border, icon size, number size and radius. **THE BUTTON** keeps `.popts`/`.popt` and `id="cqNext"` and gains `.abroad` for the heavier finish, and its 👣 is **`choiceIco({ico:['leave']})`** and never a literal, which is #154's rule - one glyph source, so the day LEAVE changes glyph this button changes with the road. ⛔ **The mercy door does NOT get it** (it opens another screen, it does not leave) and **the decorative supports under the button did not come across**: the brief calls them *"недоразумение, а не утверждённый элемент"*. Screen two's own road button gets the glyph, being the same door. **`aria-expanded` joins the accordion**, which had a caret and nothing a screen reader could hear. ⚠ **THE GAP CAME DOWN TO `--p3` AND THE SNARE IS WHY.** Measured: the emblem is 46px, the prose rule 18, the taller cards 24 and the tray 18, so the hybrid costs ~130px of card height that #122's version did not spend; `.pcard`'s 9px gap runs eleven times here, so `--p3` is 33px back and dropping `.amSec`'s top margin is 8 more. Without it the Snare - the longest aftermath prose in the game (#180) - lands exactly on the 684 ceiling and scrolls before its accordion is even open. With it the Snare card measures **658** and the Broken Men **548**. **Verified**: eight cases driven end to end in the running build on a served desk copy (plain, one scar, one level, one body carrying both, resource haul, gear haul, gear+resources with a `clashHaul` summed in, and a wedding whose `AFTER.mor` prints its own -30 morale chip beside the payout) · **the full mercy flow**, screen one → *"Go and see what they want"* → the pick settling in place → the caption deleting itself → back to `world` · the accordion open and shut with `.on`, the caret and `aria-expanded` moving together, the button still reachable · **LINT() 0 on every case** · the ui-scales §5 probes run on **all ten screens against a `git show HEAD:` baseline in the same browser**: 0 under the 10px floor everywhere, and the only clipped boxes are the four this file already documents (`#menu`, `#wMap`, `#bField`, `#bLog`) - **no new violation** · the 8px phantom scroll on `#spoils` measures **8 on HEAD too**, so it is #150's known `.pcard`-vs-`.popts` padding difference and not this pass · three viewports (1280x720, 1024x600, 900x540, 390x780) all scale with the button in the stage · 0 literal font sizes in the file, 64px still the only literal padding, 0 legacy edge hexes, no em dash in any added line. ⚠ **OPEN AND DELIBERATELY NOT DONE HERE: the three typography tokens still resolve to their fallbacks.** `--display`/`--body`/`--mono` name Cinzel, Spectral and JetBrains Mono and **none of the three is installed or embedded**, measured in the running build, so the whole game renders in Georgia and Consolas today. The brief asks for that hookup to be done *"отдельно и глобально, без Google Fonts/CDN"* - it needs the actual font binaries embedded as base64, it changes every screen in the build, and it is explicitly not this task. | ✅ |
| 8f.208 | **#188 - THE QUESTION ARRIVES INSTEAD OF WAITING IN A CORNER, AND THE ADDRESS COMES BACK.** *(User, 2026-08-18, seven numbered notes: (1) "For a feedback add email where to send and automatically 'send email'. Will it work if I made auto collect of feeback in Github? So anyway make it more sendeble with my email or discord" · (2) "Фідбек - замість рандомних 3 смайліків знизу сторінки, які ніхто не клікає, запускати вспливаш - як в HS ... якщо людина клікає 'погано' то розгорнути в причину - але сильно менше тексту. Це все зберігати у рані", with a screenshot of a shop's own two-step form · (3) "Кнопку копиед ит можна поставить поруд з 'бек' - просто рядом потрібний емейл/діскорд відправити" · (4) and (5), four sentences to delete by name · (6) "From button 'back' delete also small font" · (7) "Дев мод зробити просто шестірьонкою і перенести в правий ніжній кут і зробити прозорішою (щоб не пригортало уваги)".)* ⛔ **#98 ANSWERED "THREE LIT BUTTONS IN THE CORNER ASK FOR AN OPINION CONTINUOUSLY" BY HIDING THEM BEHIND A WORD, AND TWO ROUNDS LATER NOBODY WAS PRESSING THE WORD EITHER.** That is the finding under his note 2 and it is not a styling complaint: **a corner glyph is not a question, it is furniture, and furniture gets looked past**. So the three `.rx` buttons and the whole fold are **deleted**, the word became a handle, and the question moved into **`RXPOP`**, a modal built lazily on first open. **TWO STEPS AND THE SECOND ONE IS CONDITIONAL**, which is the whole design: three faces, one press, gone - and only the unhappy face opens *WHAT WENT WRONG?* over a box. Asking everybody for a reason is how a form gets closed. ⚠ **It may never be a thing you have to answer**: Esc, the backdrop and `NOT NOW` all close it and none of the three file anything, which is #76's not-nagging rule arriving on the one surface that can actually nag - and it **refuses to open while `ask()` is up**, both being z 200. ⛔ **THE GLYPHS ARE DELIBERATELY NOT THE MOODSTATES LADDER.** 💀😟😐🙂😄 is the company's MORALE and this modal opens over screens that are showing it; one glyph may not mean two things (#102's wrong-unit rule). The first cut was 🙁 😊 🤩 and the user's second pick, **👍 🤷 👎** *("try differen emojies - finger up, so-so, finger down so far")*, is what shipped. ⛔ **AND "SO-SO" COULD NOT BE FILED AS `like`, WHICH IS THE ONLY REAL DECISION IN THAT SWAP.** The three keys since #53 print as loved/liked/disliked, so a shrug landing on `like` would put the word "liked" on somebody who said the opposite - #102's rule arriving through what looks like a change of picture. So **`meh` is a fourth kind** and **`super` is RETIRED FROM THE POPUP rather than deleted**: nothing files ★ any more, and it stays in `RXWORD`, in the CSS and in the tally so every one already sitting in a tester's journal still renders, with the LOVED cell printed only when the count is non-zero. ⚠ The row SHAPE is unchanged - that is #57's contract - but a reader switching on `k` meets `meh` for the first time; `RXWORD[r.k]||r.k` already degrades safely and nothing else in the file switches on it. **HEARTHSTONE'S OWN MOMENT IS A FIGHT**, so `finish()` calls `RXPOP.afterBattle(fought)` - **once a run, keyed on `JOURNAL.runId`** and not on a boolean, so a second run in the same session gets its own one ask. ⛔ **The fight's name is taken BEFORE `afterPromotion()` navigates**, because by the time the popup opens the screen is the map and `where()` would file the reaction against the road; driven end to end, a won brigand fight pops a card headed **FIGHT: BRIGAND** over the map. `react(kind,w,why)` takes both as arguments now, and ⛔ **the 8-second GRACE WINDOW went with the corner buttons**: it existed because a lit glyph in the corner of every screen is easy to hit by accident, and a modal you have to press SEND on cannot be hit by accident at all, so the window could now only misread a deliberate second answer as a retraction of the first. **THE ADDRESS IS BACK, AND 8f.105 SAID IT MIGHT BE.** That row removed `FEEDBACK_TO` and the mailto button on an unconfirmed hunch that a personal address was why the artifact share kept refusing, and it ended *"IF THE SHARE STILL REFUSES, THE ADDRESS WAS INNOCENT AND THIS COMES BACK."* It is back as **one constant, `SEND_TO`**, with a `discord` field that prints the moment it is set. ⚠ **So if the share or `deploy.ps1` starts refusing again, this is the first thing to pull, and pulling it is one line.** ⛔ **His GitHub question is answered NO and the reason has not moved**: the published page's CSP blocks `fetch`, XHR and `sendBeacon`, so nothing can POST to a GitHub API or a Google Form. What a page CAN do is NAVIGATE - which is the whole reason `mailto:` works - and the same trick would open a pre-filled issue at `issues/new?title=&body=`, but it needs a public repo, a logged-in account per tester, and it carries the same URL ceiling. So **the clipboard stays the road that always works and the mail is the convenience on top of it**. ⚠ **THE URL IS MEASURED AGAIN, and only on that road.** 8f.105 wrote *"Nothing measures a URL any more"*; a `mailto:` is a URL and clients cut one silently (Outlook near 2 KB), so the mail carries the **lead** - the tester's own sentences - trimmed to **1800 ENCODED characters** and saying so, while the same press puts the whole letter and the journal on the clipboard. **Measured on the encoded string**, because a space costs three characters once it is a URL and a raw `.length` is off by half. ⚠ **And it is a real `<a>` clicked by a real person**: `location.href='mailto:'` from script is top navigation and a sandboxed iframe refuses it. **The reasons typed into the popup now ride in the letter a human reads**, not only in the JSON a parser reads - they are the only prose in the file the player wrote without being taken to a form. **THE CUTS**, all four by name: *"so I know whose run I am reading. A nickname is plenty"*, *"the one thing I cannot read off the journal"*, the notes screen's **YOUR RUN** and *"What the game wrote down while you played. It only ever watched."* ⚠ **A friend's pasted journal keeps its title and caption, and that is not an inconsistency**: whose it is and that it does not touch yours are the two facts that screen cannot be read without. **Back lost its sub-line on all THREE screens that carry one** (poll, notes, rules), because a control that reads one way on two screens and another way on a third is what ui-scales calls *decided thirty separate times*. ⚠ **The copy button moving down beside Back reverses half of #125 on purpose and on a newer instruction**: that round put the send at the top because it was buried under every run block, and it still is at the top - the MAIL is - with the clipboard as the second road at the exit, **with the address printed under it**, a copy button with nowhere named to paste it having always been half a control. **THE COG**: `⚙ DEV.MODE` is one glyph at 30% opacity in the true bottom-right corner, `#reactBar` moved inboard to `right:38px` to clear it, and ⚠ **the label is one glyph in BOTH states, which is geometry and not style** - a wider ON would grow leftwards into the FEEDBACK chip; `.on` carries the state as green, solid and fully opaque. ⚠ **The four tools it reveals did NOT move** - #100's *a stack that half-moves is worse* is about those four, still at 134/164/194/224 clearing ♪ and ⏱ on the battle screen; what moved is the SWITCH, which is not a member of that stack. Also fixed in passing: **`letter()` tested run id 0 for truth**, so a journal whose newest group was run 0 - somebody who reacted on the menu and never set off - produced a letter with no tally and, since this entry, none of their typed reasons either. **Verified in the running build**: `LINT()` **clean** (it takes ~25s, of which `labelViolations()` alone is 14.6s, which is why it outruns a 30s eval bridge) · the three map counters **0/0/0** · the ui-scales file counters unchanged (**0** literal font sizes, **64px alone**, **0** structural edge hexes) · no type under the 10px floor and nothing clipped on the popup, the bar, the cog, the notes card or the poll card · a **real won brigand fight** driven to the aftermath and out, the popup opening over the map headed FIGHT: BRIGAND, and silent on the second fight of the same run · submit files `{k,why}` and Esc/backdrop/NOT NOW file nothing · all three kinds filed and read back through the tally, the ▲ ▬ ▼ report rows and the letter, with the LOVED cell appearing only on a journal that carries ★ · the three doors measure an identical 109x60 · the popup refuses to stack on `ask()` · the blob box is borrowed and given back · the friend's-journal branch still hides every send control · the mailto parses, and a deliberately fat letter trims to a **1744-character href** with the paste note intact · the cog measures **1248..1272** and the bar **1167..1242** in both DEV states · em dashes in the file went **1348 → 1338**. ⚠ **The preview pane composited nothing this session, so every number above is off the DOM and there is no screenshot.** | ✅ |
| 8f.209 | **#189 - THE THREE TYPEFACES WERE NEVER IN THE BUILD, AND NO WINDOW SCROLLS ANY MORE.** *(User, 2026-08-18, two notes against a screenshot of the after-battle screen with a full-height scrollbar down its right side: (1) **"Шрифты очень красивые - можеш, пожалуйста, их подключить и перестроить под это в основной игре?"** · (2) **"Сделать так - чтобы не было листалки всего окна (это допустимо токо в окошке текста и то в некоторых событиях)"**.)* ⛔ **THE FIRST NOTE HAD A FALSE PREMISE AND MEASURING IT WAS THE WHOLE ENTRY.** `:root` has named Cinzel, Spectral and JetBrains Mono since the first slice, and **not one of the three has ever been in the build**: measured with a canvas width probe (⚠ `document.fonts.check()` is useless here, it answers `true` for any family name that has a fallback), all three fell straight through to **Palatino Linotype, Georgia and Consolas**, which is what every screenshot of this game has ever shown and what he was calling beautiful. `art/fonts/build_fonts.py` fetches the woff2 from Google Fonts **once, at build time**, and emits base64 `@font-face`; `art/inject_fonts.ps1` puts it between `/*__FONT_DATA__*/` markers, the same shape `art/inject.ps1` has used for the paintings. ⛔ **NOT an `@import` and not a `<link>`: the shipped build is one file and has to open from a memory stick with the network off.** ⚑ **THE FACE LIST WAS MEASURED AND THE FIRST DRAFT WAS 860KB.** Counting `font-weight:` in the stylesheet says 400/500/600/700 for all three; walking the ten screens and recording the computed (family, weight, style) of every element with its OWN text says **eight triples**: Cinzel 400/600/700, JetBrains Mono 400/600/700, Spectral 400 and 400 italic. No 500 is fetched for anything. Spectral 700 is the one face fetched without being seen, because a `<b>` inside event prose is the likeliest thing a ten-screen sweep misses and faux-bold on a serif is visibly worse than the real thing. **27 faces, 479KB of woff2, 648KB of CSS**, latin + latin-ext + cyrillic - and cyrillic is not decoration: the company name is typed by the player, this player types Russian, and `.abco` renders that name in the MONO face. Cinzel has no cyrillic subset, so a Cyrillic company name falls cleanly through instead of mixing. **THE COST IS METRICS AND IT WAS PAID IN BOXES**: real Cinzel is **16% wider than Palatino** (745 against 641 on the same probe string) and real JetBrains Mono **9% wider than Consolas** (768 against 704), while Spectral against Georgia is a wash at 0.08%. ⚠ Exactly one box broke - "BATTLE-MAGE · HUMAN" is 19 glyphs at a 6px advance plus `.02em` of tracking, which is 117.8 in a 117px column - and `.abkind` goes to zero tracking, the second time #187's own comment has had to be re-measured because the font under it changed. **Five `c.font=` sites hand-copied the two stacks** and are `cssFont(weight,px,which)` now, which reads the custom property off `:root`, so a canvas and a stylesheet cannot disagree about a typeface; ⚠ a `function` declaration on purpose, because it is called from painters defined hundreds of lines below and a `const` arrow there is the TDZ crash this file has shipped once. **(2) THE SCROLLBAR WAS TWO FAULTS WEARING ONE BAR.** The first is **#150's phantom, fixed for `#prologue` in 2026-08-14 and left on every other `.pcard` for five months**: `.popts` is a sticky footer whose bottom padding is `--p7` and the card's was `--p6`, so every card measured 8px of overflow that did not exist. ⚑ **8px IS ENOUGH TO DRAW A BAR AND WHETHER YOU SEE IT DEPENDS ON THE BROWSER, WHICH IS WHY IT SURVIVED**: on overlay-scrollbar Chrome - including the headless one every measurement in this project is taken on - 8px paints nothing and steals no width; on classic Windows scrollbars it is a full-length thumb AND 15px less content, which re-wraps the text and can turn a phantom into a real overflow. The invariant is `scrollHeight - clientHeight === 0`, never "can I see a scrollbar". The selector is `.pcard` now. **The second fault is arithmetic and cannot be styled away**: on THE SNARE, the longest aftermath in the game (#180), the card has **145px** for the crew grid, one row of tight cards is 145, and `PARTYCAP` is **13**. So the column count is authored rather than left to `auto-fit` - one row up to six, **never more than two rows** past that (a company of thirteen is 3 rows x 145 at six columns and 2 x ~160 at seven, so two rows wins by more than a row of cards), with `.tight` at five and `.lean` at seven, which drops the role line because the class and race are on the sheet and on the battle plaque while the kills, the damage and the scar are only here. What the geometry still cannot pay, **`fitAftermath()` takes out of the prose box**, which is precisely the exception the user granted: it measures the real overflow, takes exactly that much, and does nothing at all on a company of six or fewer. ⛔ **AND THE FIRST CUT OF IT DELETED THE STORY.** `.pcard` is a column flexbox, its children are flex items with `flex-shrink:1`, and `min-height:auto` on a box that has just been given `overflow:auto` resolves to **zero** - so the card's own overflow shrank the capped prose the rest of the way and a 165px story collapsed to 0px with no scrollbar to say so. `.abtop` is `flex:0 0 auto` now: the fitter decides that height and flex may not have an opinion. **Verified**: party sizes 4, 5, 6, 7, 8, 10 and 13 driven on the SNARE, **`scrollHeight - clientHeight` is 0 on every one of them**, with the prose box giving 0px at four, 12 at six, 62 at ten and 93 at thirteen · every `.pcard` screen swept for a scrolling box: menu, world, prologue, spoils, camp, promo, epilogue and help all clean (`#sim`'s fight picker is a picker and is pre-existing) · the ui-scales §5 probes on all ten screens: 0 under the 10px floor everywhere and the only clipped boxes are the four this file documents plus `#bTrait`, which is a `-webkit-line-clamp:2` box whose truncation is what the battle rail's 223px reservation is computed FROM and whose full text is on its hover tooltip · `LINT()` **0** · the three map counters **0/0/0**, which matters because name plates are set in the display face and it just grew 16% · 0 literal font sizes, 64px still the only literal padding, 0 legacy edge hexes, no em dash in any added line. ⚠ **AND ONE THING HE SHOULD SEE BEFORE IT STANDS: the fonts he called beautiful were the fallbacks.** Cinzel is an inscriptional Roman whose lowercase is drawn as small capitals, so every name and every heading in the game now reads in a noticeably different voice from the Palatino it replaced. It is the project's own stated intent since the first slice and it is what he asked for by name; it is also one `inject_fonts.ps1` run with an empty `fonts.css` to undo. | ✅ |

Она более красива получается"** with **"События на дороге должны по прежнему выглядить достаточно контрастно"**.)* The picture is drawn inside `drawMap()` **directly before `EDGES.forEach`**, which is the whole of the integration: `NODES`, `EDGES`, `drawNodes()`, `#wToken`, `WCAM`, `wcamApply()` and the zoom/pan are untouched, and the 25 node positions were captured with the picture ON, forced OFF and back ON and are byte-identical across all three. ⛔ **THE PROCEDURAL TERRAIN IS NOT DEAD CODE AND WAS NOT DELETED.** It is what the map shows for the frame before the file lands and what it shows for ever if the file never does; proved by **blocking the asset at the network layer** and reading 816,640 non-black canvas pixels rather than a black frame. ⚡ **THE SECOND CANDIDATE MADE THE REAL FINDING, AND IT WAS HIS NOTE THAT NAMED IT.** The node layer - plate text `201,185,143` on a 74% near-black wash - was coloured against a dark procedural ground, so a bright painting does not wash out the plates by accident, it washes them out by construction. **Measured over the SAME 23 name plates in one run**: the old ground sits at median luminance **48**, the painting at **119**, and **36.8%** of the painting is over 140. That is a fact about the GROUND, so the ground is what is corrected - not the plates, not the roads, not the sights, none of which this entry touches. A flat wash at `WORLD_MAP_DIM=.45` lands the painted ground on **31/53/92** against the old map's **32/48/78**, which is where the number comes from; .30 and .58 were rendered and measured beside it (65 and 42 median) and .30 still loses the plates in the pale valley. ⛔ **AND A LUMINANCE CURVE WAS TRIED ON PAPER FIRST AND BOUGHT NOTHING**: a highlight rolloff and a single alpha land within a few points of each other across this image's range, so the simple one is the right one. ⛔ **`THE GRAUSEN VALLEY` WAS REDRAWN CORRECTLY AND STILL COULD NOT BE SEEN, WHICH THE PIXEL COUNT SAID AND THE EYE DID NOT.** 594 pixels of its exact `#5f553e` were on the canvas and the label was invisible: it is authored as the dimmest of the three region names **on purpose** (*"a region is the ground under the labels, not another landmark competing with them"*), and a sunlit valley floor is the brightest part of the map, so it landed at luminance **85 on a ground of 75** - a ratio of 1.1, which is not a faint label, it is no label. ⚡ The fix is the one the name plates already use, a dark wash under the type: **three stacked shadow passes** (a canvas shadow is drawn per fill, and one pass at blur 6 only moved the ground 75 → 68) take it to **58**, and every authored colour and the whole ladder survive untouched, so the next painting does not re-open the argument. ⚠ **AND THE MEASUREMENT THAT LOOKED LIKE A BUG WAS THE PROBE.** An exact-colour pixel count read 0 for two of the three labels and 548 for the third; one pass of antialiased text never reaches full glyph coverage, so the labels were right and the counter was wrong - a manual second fill over the same text produced the exact colour and proved it. ⛔ **IT IS EMBEDDED, NEVER LINKED, AND THE 404 WOULD HAVE BEEN SILENT.** The brief's own snippet loads `art/world-map-backgrounds/...webp` by path; the published build is ONE `index.html` at the repo root, where `../art/...` resolves ABOVE the repo, so it works at this desk over `serve.ps1` and 404s on the host - **and because the procedural fallback works, that failure shows nothing**. So `build_assets.ps1` gained a webp pass that **reads the wanted key out of the prototype** (`WORLD_BG.<key>`) rather than naming it twice, throws if the folder is missing or the key matches no file, and **ships only the one ground in use** (the folder is the archive and holds the two rejected candidates; embedding all three put ~430KB of picture nobody looks at into every download). `deploy.ps1` asserts `data:image/webp;base64,` **by name**, because this is the one picture in the build whose absence is invisible. `serve.ps1` also learned `image/webp`, which it did not have. ⚠ **AND THE MERGE HAD A SEMANTIC HALF THE TEXT MERGE COULD NOT SEE**: this branch authored `'600 12px Cinzel, Palatino Linotype, Georgia, serif'` in its label helper while #189 was landing, which is a sixth copy of the exact string #189 had just deleted five of; it reads `cssFont(600,px,'display')` now. **Verified in the running build on the MERGED file**: 0 console errors from boot · `WORLD_BG` embedded and the image decoding **1280x638** into a **1280x638** backing store · ground under plates **32/55/90** · the three map counters **0/0/0** · `LINT()` **0** · nothing under the 10px floor · ROAD/NEAR/CLOSE · one road leg walked with the card opening over the map and closing without a shift · and the asset blocked to prove the fallback. ⚠ **OPEN, and it is pre-existing rather than new**: `#wCanvas` is a 1280x638 backing store displayed in a 1280x678 box, so the whole canvas layer - the procedural ground too - carries a **6.3% vertical stretch**. | 8f.211 | **#191 - SIX ASKS IN ONE BATCH: THE GROUND SPEAKS, THE ODDS GO QUIET, THREE PERKS, AND THE GAME GETS ITS NAME.** *(User, 2026-08-18, six numbered points in one message.)* **(1) THE GROUND NAMES ITSELF ON HOVER** (*"when i hover on tile with effect (water, fire, etc) - show this tile effect"*). ⛔ **IT WAS A `title` ATTRIBUTE AND A `title` IS NOT A READOUT**: six kinds carried one, the browser drew it late in its own font outside the stage, and it was written onto `hx` - so **the moment a body stood on the hex the sprite ate the hover**, and the one time you most want to know somebody is standing in fire was the one time nothing said so. ⛑ **The two kinds with the most to say had no title at all**: `marsh`, which is three separate penalties, and the six hexes around an open bloom, which is the strongest effect in the game and was only ever a tint. `groundNote(c,r,u)` is the one builder; `groundTipHTML` puts it on an empty hex through the game's own TIP and `groundRowHTML` prints the same note as a GROUND row on the readout and inspect cards for a hex somebody is standing on - one fact, three readers. ⚠ **The answer is PER BODY**, because `wetproof` and `sizeOf` mean it has to be: an ogre wades deep water, a gilled body pays nothing, and a fen-born reading *"two hexes to step into"* about water she walks through would be #102's wrong-unit rule on the ground. **Plain field returns null on purpose** - a tip that fires on all 195 hexes is furniture. **(2) THREE PERKS, AND NOT ONE OF THEM ADDS A SYSTEM.** **Patient** banks an unspent action on the hand-over in `nextTurn`, which is the only moment the outgoing body's leftover is still readable (`beginTurn` opens by overwriting `u.actions`); capped at 1 and verified not to compound - three patient turns in a row still cap at 3, never 4. **Ground-Wise** joins `wetproof()`, which the file had already made *"the one place that question is answered"*, so it needed no new plumbing: every terrain costs 1 to enter, no standing stride penalty, and `envHurt` halves what the ground itself does (measured 7 → 4). ⚠ **The bloom is deliberately excluded** - it is an aura, not ground, and a perk that switched off the strongest effect in the game would be buying a whole fight for one point. **Elemental Weapon** (mage, tier 6, his gate) rolls FIRE / FROST / VENOM in `unitFrom`, which is *why* it is per-battle - a battle is regenerated and never restored, so it needs no clock, no save field and nothing to reset. ⛔ **All three resolve through effects that already existed** (`ignite()`, `act.cripple`, `act.venom`), so a fire-charged sword and a fire-charged tile put a body in the same state wearing the same badge, put out by the same water; **one new field, `act.burn`, three lines in `strike()`**. ⚠ **The element is merged onto a COPY of each act** - weapon acts are built from `GEAR` and mutating one would hang the element on that weapon for every body that ever picks it up; verified `GEAR[main]` is clean after 200 rolls. A `charged` STATUS entry with a function `d` names what you got, as ONE row rather than three near-identical ones, because `g`/`n`/`col` are static in that register and **a duplicated key there fails silently** - the register's own warning. **(3) THE FIGHT STOPPED TELLING YOU THE ODDS** (*"не хочу, чтобы он видел % попадания. Хочу, чтобы он думал, что побеждает в рандоме"*). `SHOW_HIT_ODDS=false` takes out the hex chip, the plaque's CHANCE TO HIT cell, the aiming card's headline, `inspect()`'s SKILL, and the NEXT ONE LANDS float. ⛔ **IT HAD TO TAKE THE PARTS WITH THE TOTAL**: hiding `73%` while still printing `surrounded +10`, `their nerve −8` and `attack arc BACK +15` leaves the player adding the receipt back up out of the line items, which is #143's two-receipts rule arriving on the aiming card - so the four signed rows and the arc's figures go too. ⛑ **What survives is the STATE**: the arc still says FRONT / FLANK / BACK, because *go round the back* is the lesson this game is built to teach and it is a place to stand, not a number. The damage rows survive whole and the hex damage takes the odds' vacated slot (`.hdmg.solo`, top 16 → 4, which is #164's own arithmetic run backwards). ⛔ **Nothing mechanical changed** - same maths, same AI, same `SURE_AFTER` - so it is one const and reversible. ⚠ The company sheet keeps its TO HIT: he scoped this *"из битвы"*, and between fights that figure is how a gear or perk choice is judged. **(4) NEWS THAT CHANGES THE RUN ARRIVES IN THE MIDDLE.** `say2` gained `warn` beside `cheer` and **the tone now decides the POSITION**: the objective paying, the chest opening, the chest failing to, and somebody walking out over it all land centred at `--fs4`; ordinary narration stays in italic at `bottom:26`. ⚠ **The transform is restated in every keyframe** - the centring lives in `transform`, so a pop written as `scale(.95)` alone would fling the box to the middle-right of the map for the length of the animation. Still `pointer-events:none` and still self-closing, which is what makes a middle-of-screen strip legal at all. **(5) THE FEEDBACK POPUP**: faces reversed to bad / so-so / good, the card 10vh lower (via `top` on a relative box - a margin or backdrop padding is consumed by `align-items:center` and moves it HALF what you ask), and **SEND and NOT NOW deleted**. ⛑ **The pick IS the send now**, and nothing can be lost by losing the button: the face files on the press and the reason is written onto that same row as it is typed, so walking away mid-sentence keeps every word. `react()` gained an `amend` argument so a change of mind rewrites ONE row instead of filing two. ⛔ **And the reason belongs to the unhappy face alone** - typing a complaint then pressing 👍 used to leave it riding on a row the report prints as *liked*, invisibly, because the same press closes the box that held it. **(6) THE GAME IS RABBLEBOUND**, 165 occurrences over 47 files. ⛑ **The discriminator is CASE and it was verified before it was relied on**: `Grimtoll`/`GRIMTOLL` always name the game, lowercase `grimtoll` is always a path. ⛔ **THE ISLAND IS STILL GRIMTOLL** - his call when asked - so `03_WORLD_LORE.md` is excluded whole: it is canon, *"named twice in one word"*, and the toll runs through the content from THE HANGED TOLL-MAN down. ⚠ **The `gt_*` storage keys are untouched, which is not tidiness but the difference between a rename and wiping every save in existence.** **MEASURED**: RABBLEBOUND is **43% wider** than GRIMTOLL in real Cinzel at `--fsTitle` 74 (482 → 687px), clears the 1280 stage by 593px, so `--fsTitle` stands and `#mTitle` is untouched. **VERIFIED in the running build**: 0 console errors from boot · `LINT()` **0** · the three map counters **0/0/0** · 0 literal font sizes, 64px still the only literal padding, 0 legacy edge hexes · nothing under the 10px floor on menu, battle or map · **the clipped-box probe diffed against an `e284b06` baseline in a second tab** and menu and battle are identical to it. ⚠ **That diff also settled `#bTrait` for the second time in two sessions** - both builds clip it, it is 8f.209's deliberate `-webkit-line-clamp:2`, and it is now written into the ui-scales known list so nobody measures it a third time. ⚠ **AND THE SESSION'S OWN LESSON, PAID FOR ONCE: `io.open(P,'w')` TRUNCATES BEFORE IT ENCODES.** A lone-surrogate emoji escape raised `UnicodeEncodeError` mid-write and left the 12MB prototype at **0 bytes** with no commit on the branch. Every edit was re-applied from its own script; the patcher now encodes to bytes FIRST and swaps a temp file in, and a second identical failure minutes later cost nothing. ⚠ **It also found that the prototype is CRLF** (35,393 lines, zero lone endings): byte-mode reads match nothing against `\n` patterns, silently, which reads exactly like a wrong search string. ⛑ **THE ONE OPEN QUESTION WAS PUT TO HIM AND THE ANSWER IS BETTER THAN THE QUESTION.** Elemental Weapon is on the MAGE alone, and the battle-mage was raised as the obvious alternative because he is the caster who actually swings a blade. *(User, same day: "it ads melee - so you mage could be a glass canon. And use him for melee could be a decent trade off".)* ⛑ **It is aimed at the class that is BAD at melee on purpose.** The mage is thin, slow to dodge and poor with a blade, and had one answer to everything - cast, and stay behind the line. A charged weapon does not make him good in a scrum, it makes stepping into one a decision with a price. On the battle-mage, who is already built to stand in the line, the same perk would be a straight upgrade to what he does anyway, which is the "stat increase with a name" this table's own header warns against. **It stays off `warmage` as a ruling, not as an omission.** | ✅ |
| 8f.212 | **#193 - THE MIREHARES, AND THE RED LIGHTS.** *(User, 2026-08-18: *"I want to create and add monsters. Fixed encounter of 2 does and 2 bucks on `swamp`. ⚑ **THE LAST SENTENCE OF HIS BRIEF IS THE ONE THAT FOUND THE BUG, AND NO WIN RATE WOULD HAVE.** With no walk to fall back on, each verb only fired when a target was **already** on a legal ring hex or lane, so `mirehareMove` returned false and the turn simply ended. Measured over 10 arena fights, counting only turns the body could still afford: **the buck stood still for 85% of the fight** and the doe for 33% - a creature whose whole rule is *block his lane* was teaching nothing, because the lane was never threatened. ⚡ **The fix is the second half of each verb, not a new one**: same geometry, same lengths, used to REPOSITION when it cannot strike and paying no damage when it does. Statblocks, dice, costs, encounter, card and art all untouched. Both now act on **0% wasted turns** and charges per fight went 1.7 to 3.3. ⚑ **What ignoring the creature costs, same fight and same brains, only the opening positions moved**: standing off in the open is **0.83 of yours down and 178 taken**, closing to contact is **0.08 and 121** - ten times the people. ⚠ **And the honest mechanism is TEMPO, not denial**: damage per round is 29.7 against 29.5, so what closing buys is a **4.1-round fight instead of a 6.0-round one**. Two levers are named in the spec if that is not enough (the doe's landing hitting the whole ring, the buck's dice scaling with the run), **both deliberately untaken** because the brief said *"Do not redesign the Mirehares"*. ⚠ **TWO FALSE READINGS ON THE WAY, BOTH WORTH THE RECORD.** A naive idle counter read the SECOND `aiTurn` call of every turn as a wasted turn - both acts cost 2 of 2 actions, so `canUse` correctly refuses the follow-up - and reported 90% where the truth was 85%. And **the browser served a cached 14 MB prototype**, so three runs of "after the patch" numbers were really the unpatched build and looked entirely plausible; check `fn.toString()` in the running page. ✅ Card **THE RED LIGHTS** implemented verbatim from his text, companion name taken off a live non-leader; art `EV32` decoding at 586x212, icons 48x52 and 54x40 RGBA. Bench: `tools/mirehare_bench.js`, which **defines nothing** and only measures. ⚠ **OPEN**: the detour door prints its price twice (the derived `−2 days` chip plus his authored *"the journey takes two days longer"*), which is the one-sentence rule in the card file - his text, so it stands until he says otherwise, and dropping the `c:` is a one-word change. ⛔ **One ruling left**: whether *"strait and vertical"* really means the six hex lanes, since a screen-vertical column is a zigzag and there is no seventh straight line to give him. | ✅ |
| 8f.215 | **#192 PART TWO - THE FIFTY-SIX PAINTINGS ARE WIRED, AND THE PICTURE IS A COLUMN RATHER THAN A CHARACTER.** *(User, 2026-08-19: "chatgpt generated all artifacts from you instructions. Can you please connect it to actual objects?", handing over `art/src/items/` with 56 files and a contact sheet.)* Every one of the 51 pieces in `GEAR{}` has its own painting now, plus the five of #192's unbuilt item pack, embedded **verbatim** as 128x128 alpha PNG (`ITEM_ART`, +1.1 MB) and mapped by **one table, `ITEM_ICON{}`** - the same one-place-a-key-meets-a-picture shape as `MAP_SIGHT{}`, so a key with no entry falls back to its glyph and that stays a legitimate shipping state. Measured both ways first: filled slot **53 -> 56 -> 63px** against the 67 pitch, stash row **56 -> 54**, and the shop's 60px box takes its 56px picture without moving. ⚠ **AND ONE EDIT SILENTLY DID NOT STICK** - the stash hunk reverted to its 24px version while the `</div>` that closed it survived, which is a parallel-desk hazard that only a grep for the new identifier catches. **Verify a landed edit by grepping for a string only the new code has**, never by the tool saying it succeeded. Gates: `LINT()` 0, the ui-scales overflow probe **5 and 5 against a `git show HEAD:` baseline in a second tab** (all four known plus `.role`, none new), nothing under 10px, and the map's three counters 0/0/0.
