| 8f.216 | **#194 - THE MAP REMEMBERS WHERE YOU HAVE BEEN, THE LEGEND GOES, THE CLAN WEDDING BECOMES A PLACE, AND THE DAYS STOP EXPLAINING THEMSELVES.** *(User, 2026-08-19, four numbered points and a screenshot with the legend boxed in red.)* **(1) A WALKED NODE STAYS LIT** (*"На карте оставлять подсвеченными пройденные события"*). `done` was **.45** against `far`'s **.38** - a seven percent difference between *you fought here* and *you have never heard of this*, and against the painted ground #190 put underneath, both are simply gone. It is **opacity 1** now and gives up everything else instead: no glow, no `nodebeat`, the ring in the walked road's own `#8a7d63`, and the ✓ drawNodes already writes. ⛔ **IT MAY NOT BECOME A SECOND KIND OF LIVE** - gold-and-beating is the only thing on this map allowed to mean *you may walk here*, and a bright still node would cost a click to discover. ⚠ **The rule sits BELOW `.node.t-*`, not beside `.node.live`**: those set `border-color` at the same (0,2,1) specificity and the sighted ring at (0,2,2), so a `done` rule written in the obvious place loses the cascade on five of seven types and on every painted node, silently. ⚡ **AND THE FILTER WAS MEASURED RATHER THAN CHOSEN.** The first cut, `saturate(.42) brightness(.84)`, was **indistinguishable** from `saturate(.62) brightness(1.02)` in two captures at map scale, which is where this file says stop looking. Drawing a done painting into a canvas through each filter and averaging its non-transparent pixels (Blood on the Road, 2020 px): raw **luma 36.6 / sat .636**, filtered **luma 30.3 / sat .313**. The saturation half halves the colour and does all the work; the brightness half buys a **17% dim on art already at 36 out of 255**, on the one state whose whole instruction was *leave them lit*. Shipped as `saturate(.45) brightness(1.05)`. **(2) THE MAP LEGEND IS DELETED** (*"i feel we can delte this agenda, it doesnt work properly now"*). He is right and it had rotted in **three** ways, each from an entry with no reason to look here: #114/#115/#116 replaced the coloured diamond it keyed with a 96px painting, so it described a chip that **18 of 25 nodes no longer draw**; it named five of the seven types and **never covered `town` or `event`**, the two commonest things on the map; and #190's painted valley floor took the last of a 10px `#8a7449` row's contrast. ⚑ **Nothing replaces it**: the node's hover answers *what is that?* in a sentence, the type tag rides the glyph, and the painting says it faster than either. ⚠ `.mlegend` **stays in drawNodes' sweep selector** though the builder is gone - it is the only thing that can remove a strip left behind by a stale DOM. **(3) THE CLAN WEDDING IS A NODE** (*"Ratking weedin I want as mandatory event on the main paths (you can add it after dogs atack)"*). It was one of eighteen cards competing for seven slots, so a run met it under half the time - and it is the card `G.sins`, the kin reinforcement and `AFTER.wedding`'s morale bill are all written against. Out of `FLOATING` (17 left, still more road than slots) and onto the first corridor as `wed`, and `LINT` 8a's spine list gained it so no future edge can route around a card nothing else can deal. ⛔ **IT IS ONE STOP LATER THAN THE ASK AND THE REASON IS ARITHMETIC, NOT TASTE.** "After the dogs" is between `camp1` (668,322) and `hire` (782,200): **167px apart against `MAP_MIN_GAP` 90**, so a node between them lands **83px from each** and fails before anything else is asked. The plate rule is harsher - a pair needs **110px across OR 79px down** - and camp1→hire has only **114 of x** to spend, so one hop can use it and the other must find 79px of y in a 122px drop. A solver over every integer coordinate in the region returned exactly one family, all of which **sit on the road from the muster down into the fen** (the curve passes 9px from the node centre; the painting is 79px wide) unless The Muster Field moves ~80px. ⚑ **The three options were put to him with those numbers and he chose the one where nothing else moves**: `wed` at **(806,310)**, dogs → muster → wedding → fen, 110px+ clear on every side. The corridor's own beat survives - *the dogs show you that you are too few, the muster answers it, the Fen spends what you bought* - and the wedding lands between the answer and the spending, which is the one window in the act where the company is freshly at strength and has not yet paid for it. That is the right place to be offered a massacre. ⚠ **It costs +1 day on a corridor every run walks**: routes went 19-23 days to **20-24**. ⚠ **Named, like every other fixed node**: `MAP_SIGHT.wedding` has been drawn since #114 and a sight shows from minute one, so the picture announces a wedding whether the plate does or not - a rumour line would be the only hint on the map its own painting contradicts. **"The Clan Wedding"** is 16 characters and wraps to the two lines `NAME_H` is measured for; *"A Wedding on the Road"* is 21 and wraps to three. **(4) THE DAY PLAQUE LOSES ITS HOVER** (*"Delete hover from days (it doesnt need explanation)"*). `WTIPS.day` is **deleted from the table**, not merely unbound - an entry left with no reader greps as live, goes on quoting `hereN`/`destN`, and the first person to notice nothing shows it "fixes" the binding. Both callers go (the plaque and the sun/moon disc are one readout, and half a tooltip is worse than none) and `cursor:help` comes off the plaque in the same edit, because a chip that offers a hover and gives nothing is the defect this surface exists to stop. **VERIFIED in the running build, on the desk's own file rather than a server** - ⚠ **the preview server on 8777 turned out to be another session's, serving the MAIN repo, and reported my own edits missing**; caught by md5-ing the served bytes against both trees, not by reading the page. `LINT()` **0** · the three map counters **0/0/0** · **8 of 8 routes** to the Snare pass `wed`, `armourWhere()` still resolves to `dead`, goal bands unchanged (shrine 2, hire2 1) · the card opens on its node at **586x212** with the `?` door and the derived race mark · a run saved and reloaded standing on `wed` comes back with **8 ticked nodes** · 0 literal font sizes, 64px still the only literal padding, 0 legacy edge hexes · nothing under the 10px floor · the clipped-box probe returns only `#wMap`, which is on the documented pre-existing list. ⚠ **AND ONE THING THAT LOOKED LIKE A REGRESSION AND WAS NOT**: with a ratkin or ogre in the company the wedding shows **3 doors, not 4** - the walk-away is gone. Probed against a `git show HEAD:` baseline in the same browser and it is **byte-identical**, because it is `dropDeadExits` working exactly as written: a free race door dominates a walk-away, and the wedding is that rule's own named worked example. Making the card mandatory does not change it; it only means every player now meets it. | ✅ |
| 8f.217 | **#195 - THE % TO HIT COMES BACK, AND THE MIREHARE OVERLAY STOPS LYING ABOUT WHERE THEY GO.** *(User, 2026-08-19: "1) Bro, return % to hit - both in when i hover on enemywith hit + in extended window more info about hit · 2) For merehere battle show more clear the line of movment of creatures · 3) Run some test fights to be sure - it is readeble and hard is easier, when you get this rules of thier movment", then mid-session "with hit chances showing - basically return, how it was before".)* **(1) THE ODDS RETURN BY THE ONE FLAG #191 BUILT.** `SHOW_HIT_ODDS` false -> **true**, and it is one boolean rather than two surgical edits at the two surfaces he named **because the rule #191 discovered is symmetric**: hiding a total means hiding its signed PARTS, so showing a total means showing them too or the card states a figure it will not explain. Half the family restored is the state #191 itself called *the worst of the two*, reached from the other side - and his follow-up (*"basically return, how it was before"*) confirmed the whole family was what he meant. #191's entire reading is **left in place above the flag**, because it is what makes this a reversal rather than a revert. Measured on the board rather than read: the hex carries `.hodds` **"91%"** at 10px `top:4` in `#8ca35a` over `.hdmg` **"4-6"** at `top:16`, **clear of each other by 4.4px** - which is #164's floor arithmetic holding, the pair having been re-measured at 10px in that pass and then never seen because #191 hid it a day later; the aiming card carries `#rHit` **"91%"** at 20px with the arc back to **BACK +30 · ×1.25 dmg** and all four signed terms printing (**sure hand · surrounded +54 · your nerve 😄 +5 · their nerve 😟 +2**, each with its own derived `MOODSTATES` face); the plaque's cell is back at **flex**; the inspect card reads **SKILL / DODGE 73 / 15**. ⚡ **What stays as #191 left it, because neither was ever a to-hit figure**: the arc keeps its WORD (FRONT/FLANK/BACK is a place to stand) and now carries its figures beside it again, and the ground notes remain the rules of the world. **(2) THE MIREHARE OVERLAY WAS A LIE, NOT AN OMISSION, AND THAT IS THE ENTRY.** Hovering the shipped pair, measured before anything changed: **the doe was painted 24 hexes of which 18 she can NEVER reach** - everything at distance 1 and 2, because she cannot land closer than three - **while 8 legal landings went unpainted**; **the buck was painted 40 of which 22 were unreachable**, and his six straight lanes, the single thing the player has to work out, **were not drawn at all**. The cause is one line: `peekOn` built `reachMap(t,t.speed)`, a cost-based flood fill, for two creatures with `noWalk:true` and **no MOVE act** - #102's wrong-unit rule arriving on the hover overlay, and doubly wrong for the doe because **a leap does not pay for the ground it crosses and a flood fill can only**, so a budget of three that charges for marsh and water reached a fraction of her real ring. ⚑ **THE ANSWER IS THREE BUILDERS WHERE THERE WERE NONE.** `CUBE_AXIS` declares the six lanes **once** (they were `mirehareMove`'s private property, and an overlay drawing a lane the brain will not run is worse than the overlay that was missing: it teaches a wrong rule confidently); `chargeLanes()` walks them, returning `path` / `blocker` / `n`, and the brain's **three** hand-rolled copies of that walk are now three calls with the scoring unchanged line for line; `moveEnvelope()` answers *where can this body END its move* - the ring at exactly N for a leap, the six lanes for a charge, `reachMap` for everything else, **untouched on purpose** so no other enemy's overlay moves. ⛔ **AND `peekThreat` LEARNED THAT A MOVEMENT-ONLY CREATURE THREATENS FROM ITS LANDINGS AND NEVER FROM WHERE IT STANDS**, which is the half that teaches the counters - and both were previously drawn backwards. No hex at distance 1 is adjacent to a hex at distance 3, so **standing against the doe is safety**; `n>=2` is the charge's own rule, so **standing against the buck is safety**. Both counters are what #193's own bench was written to measure and neither had ever been on screen. ⚡ **THEN THE LANES ARE ACTUALLY DRAWN**, on `drawLosRay`'s pattern exactly (an SVG in `#bFx`, imperative and outside `render()`, positioned by `hexPt` off the live DOM so it lands at any camera zoom, `pointer-events:none` so a readout can never be a control): six lines per charger, **dim and dashed for a lane he could run, bright with an arrow-head for a run that ENDS IN A BLOW as the board stands**, and for the doe a dot on every landing plus a bowed quadratic arc onto the ones that pay. A wash can only say WHICH hexes; **only a line says STRAIGHT**. ⛔ **THE FILE WARNED ABOUT THE BUG I THEN SHIPPED FOR AN HOUR, IN WRITING, ON THE BLOCK I DID NOT READ.** `render()` holds a SECOND peek-rebuild site whose own comment says *"a map rebuilt in one and not the other is the bug #171's own overlay would have shipped"*. I taught `peekOn` and not that, so the honest overlay **survived exactly one frame** before the walk flood fill came back under a cursor that had not moved, with `peekEnv` and `peekReach` describing two different boards. ⚑ **It was found by making the live-charge branch fire ON PURPOSE** - the lanes it reported were one move stale - which is this repo's own rule that a check which has only ever returned nothing is indistinguishable from a broken one, turned around and used on my own work. Both sites go through the one builder; grep says **zero direct `reachMap` peek callers remain**. **(3) THE TEST FIGHTS, AND THE INSTRUMENT WAS THE THING THAT NEEDED FIXING.** `MB.gap()` reports the gap **the wrong way round** on this build (IGNORED 0.42 down / 158 taken against ANSWERED 0.83 / 163), and that is not the creature: `arrange()` fires **once**, at `setup`, and **both counters are PER-TURN facts** - the doe cannot land on a body already beside her *and she moves every turn*, so contact has to be RE-MADE every turn. A starting formation cannot express *stay in contact*, so `MB.gap` measures whether the company began in a tidy line. #193 already half-knew this, having recorded that closing is a **tempo** answer rather than a denial. So **`MB.rules(n)`** varies only where our bodies stand when a mirehare acts, re-imposed every turn by wrapping the one function both creatures move through, and at n=20 a side it is unambiguous: **CONTACT 20/20 · 2.8rd · 0.00 down · 46 taken · 2.1 blows** against **NAIVE 14/20 · 12.3rd · 2.50 down · 336 taken · 17.8 blows**. Understanding the movement is worth **2.5 fewer bodies and 289 less hp+armour**, takes the fight from **70% to 100%**, and the pair lands **8.5x fewer blows**. His third point is therefore confirmed as written, and the overlay now draws exactly the geometry that produces the gap. ⚠ **CONTACT is an upper bound on skill, not the expected experience** - a player teleporting into contact every turn with no movement budget and no parting swings; the shipped fight with AUTO on both sides sits between the arms at 6.6 rounds and 0.8 down. **REGRESSION, because the brain was refactored onto the shared builder**: 4 x n=20 per build, against `git show 35227a4:` in the same browser - baseline **6.54rd · 0.84 down · 181.0 taken · 6.26 landings · 3.05 charges**, #195 **6.63 · 0.81 · 181.8 · 6.34 · 2.95**, 20/20 wins on all eight runs, every delta far inside each build's own spread (rounds swing 0.75 within one build). Gates: `LINT()` **0** · the ui-scales clipped-box probe **3, all documented** (`#bField`, `#bTrait`, `#bLog`) with **`#bFx` at 0/0**, so the new SVG created no scroller · **0** under the 10px floor · 0 literal font sizes, 64px still the only literal padding, 0 legacy edge hexes · the map's three counters **0/0/0** · `MB.axes()` six straight lanes, collinear, ring 18 · no console errors. ⚠ **The line weights were set by a SCREENSHOT and not by taste**: at `.50` alpha and `r=2.4` the doe's eighteen landing dots vanished into her own wash, and her ring is the whole of what she teaches. ⚠ **Built on desk `work/hit-odds-mirehare` rather than the main tree, at his word mid-session** - *"be carefull with main file - i am aslo working with texts in it right now"* - so the prototype was never touched on main and the landing is a merge he chooses the moment for. | ✅ |

A 175-word body measures 14 rows and the panel still does not scroll. **(2) THE HOVER MODE IS NOT A SECOND WAY TO DO THE SAME THING - IT IS THE ONLY WAY TO REACH TEXT THAT HAS NO CLICK.** Counted in the shipped file: `#gtTip` is ONE box driven by fifteen hover sources across three screens, built `pointer-events:none` and hidden on the anchor's `mouseleave`, so the cursor can never arrive on it and `caretRangeFromPoint` can never be asked about it. Every word of that is player-facing prose - what a trinket does, what a race is - and ✎ TEXT has been blind to all of it since #172. So the mode has three parts and only the three together are worth anything: a **dwell** opens the editor with no click; the tip is **snapshot at the pick**, off the live `#gtTip`, while the cursor is still on its anchor; and the `title=` of the nearest ancestor that owns one. The editor then offers whichever of the three exist as **targets** and the ledger records **which**, because "find `orig` in the file" is a different job for a body string than for a `title=`. ⚠ **THE DWELL RESTS ON MOVEMENT, NOT ARRIVAL**: a timer started at `mouseover` fires 420ms after you ENTER an element, so crossing a screen opens an editor over whatever you passed. Restarting it on every move means the editor opens where you STOPPED, which is also exactly when a hover box is up and settled. ⛔ **AND A HOVER-BOX EDIT HAS NO PREVIEW AND MUST NOT FAKE ONE** - `tipOn` holds a function and rebuilds the box on the next enter - while a `title=` does preview, because it is an attribute on an element that survives. Proved end to end in the running build: hovering the roster's captain mark opened `✎ TEXT` + `🛈 TOOLTIP`, and hovering a sheet chip caught **725 characters** of race lore that the tool could not previously see. **(3) THE MARKS BECAME PICTURES, AND THE WORD CAME OFF THE LIST.** ⛔ **The old set was not wrong, it was unreadable ALONE**, which is a different fault: ⌇ ➹ ✷ † ⬤ sort a list you are already reading, and the ask was to delete the caption. ⛔ **AND THE RACES HAD TWO VOCABULARIES, WHICH IS THE REAL FIND** - `RACE_ICON` said ◆ ▪ ⬢ (authored as small/medium/huge body marks) while `RACE_ICO` on the event doors has said 🐀 👹 🧑 since #154: the same fact, two symbols, on two screens a player crosses in a minute. One table is the source now and the doors READ it, in the safe direction (declared 24,000 lines above, so no temporal dead zone). Classes: ⚑ captain · 🔱 spear · 🏹 archer · 🔮 mage · 🗡🔮 battle-mage · 🗡 cutter · 🪓 brute · ○ no trade. **All five new glyphs appear ZERO times elsewhere in the file**, checked before they were written against `CHOICE_ICO`, `RES_ICON`, `GEAR_MARK`, `SLOT_ICON`, monster `ico`, `iconFor` and `MOODSTATES`. ⛔ **THE WORD ONLY COMES OFF A LIST**: the roster, the battle rail, the tavern strip, the aftermath's recruits and the muster wall lose it; the promotion card, the trade you are PICKING, the lore box that IS the explanation and the camp's news line keep it, because a symbol is worth having when it sorts twelve rows and worth nothing on the one row you are already reading. The words survive on `data-cls`/`data-race` where `bindLoreTips` reaches and on a plain `title=` where it does not - and **bindLoreTips now strips that title**, so exactly one box ever opens. ⚡ **AND IT FIXED A SHIPPED CLIPPING NOBODY HAD REPORTED**: the battle rail is 194px and on `HEAD` an ogre SPEARWOMAN overflowed it by **2px** and an ogre BATTLE-MAGE by **23**, cut off inside the line. The outcome prose loses the clause the label carries (*"a week of walking it off. Nothing permanent."*, *"It mends."*) and both em dashes; `injure` stores a COPY, having pushed the card table's own object onto the roster since it was written. **(6) EVERY CARD OPENS IN THE MIDDLE.** #104 anchored a card to its node and #156 put the camera in that mapping; with a 620px card on a 1280px stage the anchor is CLAMPED against an edge for half the map, so THE DEBT opened hard left and SOMETHING IN ARMOUR hard right and the painting inside - which since stage 4 is the first thing on the card - landed wherever the node happened to be. `placeDlg` centres; every caller still hands its node coords and `DLGAX`/`DLGAY` still record them, so the anchor is one block away if it is ever wanted back. **Driven: the same card opened from `hold` and from `snare` lands 0px off the map's centre.** **(7) RARITY, WHICH DID NOT EXIST.** *"give more often rare and epic items"* had nothing to count: no `rarity:` field on any of the 51 rows, though `.claude/rules/static-event-art.md` has promised one since #192. A won fight rolls a second piece at **30% (23 rare, 7 epic)**; a cache at **60% (42/18)**, because it is the one place the player was told about in advance and walked to on purpose. A find is filtered by `size`, so a company with no ogre cannot be handed ogre scale - an authored row may do that because it is about a specific corpse, a roll has no such excuse. **Measured over 20k rolls per source: 70/23/7 and 40/42/18 exactly, and not one `unique` piece drawn in 20k.** Worst plausible company (human only) still has 10 rare and 3 epic to draw from. The tier prints as a WORD in its colour; the white/green/purple FRAME is a later UI pass with its own picture, per his answer. **(15) A TREASURE MAP**, in the brigand table and in `snarejoin` (the Snare taken from inside the clan's line - *"when you have joined them"*). It is a PLACE, not a stat, and the machinery existed: `takeLoot` calls `plantStash` and a node ahead becomes The Cache. `company:true`, so it never takes a trinket slot. ⚠ **The row is filtered out of the deal where nothing can be buried** - `snarejoin` is fought on the LAST node of the act, where `reachAhead` is empty, so it would otherwise have handed over a map to nowhere every single time. Found by reading `plantStash`'s own gate rather than the new row. **(4) THE MUSTER HIDES ITS STAT LINE** and the walk-away wears 👣. #157 moved the personality to a hover on exactly the argument that applies here - *"the name is the thing you compare, the sentence is the thing you look up"* - and left the longest line on the row printed in full. Hidden, not deleted: this is the one screen where the numbers decide something, so `tellLine` goes onto the NAME's hover beside the trait's. The muster is hand-built, so #154's intent glyphs had never reached it. **(12) THE WORD IS CLASS, NOT TRADE** wherever the game means a class: the class picker's heading and button tag, `NO CLASS YET`, the level prompt, the muster's two labels, one tutorial step, the memory line. `ico:['trade']` (buy/sell) and every commercial *trade* in the prose are untouched. Found by sweeping every quoted string, which is how the first pass missed three of them. **(13) A DOOR THAT IS NOT A DECISION LOSES ITS SUB-LINE** - four continue buttons whose caption's whole content was that there was nothing to decide - and **it loses the `<i>` too**: `choiceNote` returns '' on a one-door pickup and both renderers wrapped that in an empty `display:block` box. **(11) THE MAP HOVER PRINTED ITS TYPE TWICE**, once as a mono subtitle and once as the body, which falls back to the same `TYPETAG` for a live node with no event - so a hire node read SWORDS FOR HIRE, then SWORDS FOR HIRE, under its own name, with the glyph's `title` making a third in the browser's tooltip. The subtitle goes; the body stays, because the body is the branch that knows the difference between live, walked, marked and far. **(16) THE MAP'S TEST-MODE OVERLAY ONLY SPEAKS WHEN SOMETHING IS WRONG** - the all-clear ran along the foot on every frame, which is #106's furniture rule; the red crowded-pair report is untouched. **(14) *"Through the marsh. Slower, but quiet."* IS DELETED** from the last fight: it bought the first round for a day nobody is short of on the last node of the act. `ambush:true` survives (the wedding's massacre door is the other user) and `FIELDS.snare`'s note is corrected, because it cited that door as the reason for its board. **(9) A DOOR THAT HANDS YOU A BODY SAYS "NO ROOM" BEFORE IT IS PRESSED.** *"with ogres event you don't have option ogre to join - when your party is full"* - **the door was never missing**. It was live, said *an ogre may join*, and the room check ran INSIDE `pickChoice`, so a full company spent its one choice to be told afterwards that nobody follows a captain with no room at the fire. From outside that is indistinguishable from the option not existing, which is how it was reported. Derived off `recruit`/`recruitPreset`, never authored, so the four preset doors nobody reported are fixed too; the appended walk-away asks the same predicate, or a card whose only live door was a full-party recruit would soft-lock. **(8) THE LONG FIRE's second part: 151 words to 99.** Both halves of his own speech survive (canon); what went is the stage directions, the sentence that repeated the card body's own third line, and the mechanical paragraph down to the two rules it states. **(10) THE BOTTOM OF THE MAP IS INSIDE THE MAP AGAIN, AND IT TOOK THREE READINGS.** At the default NEAR stop the map overflows by **121px** and six nodes are cut - a fact about the ZOOM, not the layout; and `b.style.top` is CAMERA space while `getBoundingClientRect` is SCREEN space, so mixing them reports a node's own caption as sitting 81px below itself. Pinned to ROAD and to identity the real number is **22px and three captions**. ⛔ **EVERY GLOBAL FIX IS ARITHMETICALLY CLOSED**: the stage is 720 = bar 42 + map 678 exactly; `--fs1` is a 10px FLOOR so nothing scales down; `vy()` cannot map into a shorter band because the terrain canvas is stretched by the same ratio and the nodes track the PAINTING (#190), drifting up to 79px at the foot; and the four nodes may not move, which is what #194 paid for. So the caption rises - and **the first cut hung it above the painting and the map's own gate caught it**: `spacingViolations` 0 → 2 and `labelViolations` 0 → 1 against a `git show HEAD:` baseline driven to the same screen, four plates having moved 122 design px into a row nobody had touched. Shipped version lifts each caption by **its own overflow and nothing more** - 14 to 37px on four nodes - written per node as `--lift` off the same arithmetic `plateBox` uses, so the stylesheet and the checker read one figure. Lexing the `<script>` region properly (single quote / double quote / template / line comment / block comment / regex) says the player reads **188**, and splits the rest into **1,071 JS block comments, 68 CSS comments, 9 lone placeholders, 2 HTML comments and 2 inside `LINT()`'s own detectors**. The battle legend settled it outright: of its four sibling lines, three carried an em dash and the fourth already read **`held - somebody is facing this hex`**. Held." and "It will not fight. printing the URL there would claim a page nobody can open. ⚠ **Driven in the browser**: `play/index.html` boots to the map with no cog, `TEST.on` false, no ⚙ Playtest notes row, and a planted `gt_test=1` changes nothing. `play/index.html` is `-diff linguist-generated` and `merge=ours` like its sibling. **THE DOCS.** `WHAT_TO_TEST.md` took its third cut (4,240 lines to 1,745; 2026-08-16 and earlier behind `git show 1d2e1b3:`), the backlog's shipped table gained #199, #201 and #202, `DEPLOY.md` the two flags and the `play/` URL, README §3 and §7 re-dated and re-measured, and `event-cards.md`/`ui-scales.md` carry the two findings. ⚠ **Built on branch `work/playtest-build` in the main folder at the user's word; main is untouched and the live link unchanged until it is merged.** Gates: `LINT()` 0 (fourteen numbered checks now) · `node --check` on the extracted script, prototype and both built pages · no em dash in EVENTS · the three closed-vocabulary greps unchanged (0 / 64px / 0) · nothing under the floor on menu, practice field, help, poll, sheet, battle, spoils · the clipped-box probe returns only the documented `#menu`, `#bField`, `#bLog`, `#bTrait` and `#wMap`. | ✅ |
| 8f.225 | **#203 - THE ITCH BUILD, AND THE THIRD-PARTY IFRAME THAT WOULD HAVE HANDED HALF ITS PLAYERS A TITLE SCREEN WITH NO BUTTONS.** *(User, 2026-08-19: "подготовь билд на итч. И сделай описание проекта под итч (исходя из моих сообщений про игру)".)* **THE FINDING, AND IT IS THE WHOLE ENTRY.** itch serves an HTML game from ITS OWN origin inside an iframe on the page, and a browser set to block third-party storage does not hand that iframe an empty `localStorage` - **`window.localStorage` THROWS SecurityError on access**. The game is ONE inline script and three of its top-level statements read storage before anything else runs (`AU.on` off `gt_mute`, `AUDIOPREF` off `gt_audio`, `TEST.on` off `gt_test`), so the first throw aborted every line after it. ⛔ **MEASURED RATHER THAN REASONED ABOUT, AND THE PICTURE IS THE ARGUMENT**: `shots/203_itch_iframe.html` writes the BUILT page into an iframe with a throwing getter defined first (a navigation would reset the window and lose the override), and what rendered was RABBLEBOUND, the pillar under it, and **no buttons at all** - one uncaught `SecurityError` and a front door that reads as a badly made game rather than as an error. ⚑ **ONE SHIM AT THE TOP OF THE SCRIPT, NOT FORTY CALL SITES.** `localStorage` is a configurable own accessor on `window`, so it is replaced wholesale with an in-memory object and the ~40 readers and writers are untouched and cannot forget; ⚠ **the probe WRITES**, because a browser can hand out a readable store that refuses to keep anything, and a read-only probe would pass and let the save fail silently later. **`STORE_OFF` is the other half**: the front door replaces its own footer with *"this browser is blocking storage: the run will not survive the tab"*, which is the difference between a player who knows and a player who loses an hour. Verified by playing IN the blocked iframe: menu, prologue, brawl, spotlight, no errors. **THE EMBED SIZE IS ARITHMETIC AND NOT A PREFERENCE.** `fit()` scales the whole 1280x720 stage by `min(w/1280,h/720)`, so the itch viewport multiplies every glyph in the game: measured in the bench, a 960-wide embed sits at **0.75** and the 10px floor `.claude/rules/ui-scales.md` exists to defend renders at **7.5px**. 1280x720 and the fullscreen button, written into the script's own output and into the page doc. **THE PACKAGING.** `tools\build_itch.ps1`: player build into a fresh stage, zip, then **read the zip back** - index.html at the ROOT (the commonest itch mistake, answered by itch with a blank frame and no reason), 11+ cues, 100+ pictures, the marker present and the cog absent, the storage guard present. ⚠ **NOT `Compress-Archive`**: PS 5.1 writes entry names with BACKSLASHES, which the zip spec forbids and unzippers disagree about, so a lenient one makes a file called `icons\favicon.ico` and a strict one drops it; entries are written by hand with forward slashes and the check refuses a backslash. The zip was then **extracted and played** from the extracted bytes, which is the same rule as deploy.ps1 step 3 one layer out. **THE PAGE.** `docs/ITCH_PAGE.md` carries the tagline, the body text, the tags and the settings, written off his own framing of the game (the pillar, the rabble who are not mercenaries, personalities rather than traits) with **every number taken from the running build** rather than from the docs: 39 road cards, 19 fire incidents, 26 places, 7 trades, 26 perks, 26 personalities, 13 seats at 2/1/3 a body. **Five screenshots and a cover**, captured at 2x from the EXTRACTED ZIP through `tools/playtest/eyes.py` because the preview pane composites no frames, and the cover is a 630x500 crop of the front door's key art tight enough to read at the 315x250 itch actually draws. ⛑ **AND THE SHOOT FOUND A DEFECT THE PROBES CANNOT SEE**: the aftermath's crew card breaks a nickname INSIDE the word (*"Weatherhe / ad"*) once `fitAftermath` goes to six columns. Measured before touching anything - the name column is **131px** at four across and the longest nickname is **80px**, so it is a six-across-only fault - and **deliberately not fixed here**: `.abname` carries `overflow-wrap:anywhere` precisely to stop the card overflowing, the alternative clips instead of wrapping, and this is the card #187 and #189 both got burned on. It is named in `SHIPPED.md` and the screenshot is simply not one of the five. Gates: `LINT()` **0** · nothing under the 10px floor on any screen · the clipped-box probe returns only the documented `#menu` · map counters **0/0/0** · no em dash in EVENTS · `node --check` on the prototype and on the zipped page. | ✅ |
| 8f.227 | **#205 - THREE THINGS OUT OF THE FEEDBACK ROUND, AND ALL THREE WERE MEASURED BEFORE THEY WERE BELIEVED.** *(User, 2026-08-19: three numbered items after a round of collected feedback.)* ⛔ **1. THE HEALTH BAR OVER A HEAD HAS BEEN A SUB-PIXEL ACCIDENT SINCE #157.** *("На мобильном устройстве (андроид) и мак буке шкала здоровья в бою почему-то не отображается".)* It is not a phone bug and it is not a browser bug. `*{box-sizing:border-box}` is the first rule in the file, so a row declared `height:2px` with a 1px border top and bottom leaves `2 - 2 × border` of content and `.ubars div i` is `height:100%` of THAT. **And the border is not 1px**: the board is drawn inside a camera `zoom`, `zoom` scales computed lengths, and measured in the running build one stop computes the border at **0.513px** (0.97px of fill survives) and another at **1px** (**exactly 0** - `getBoundingClientRect().height` on every fill on the board, both rows, every body). So the same build painted a hairline of red on one machine and nothing at all on the next, which is why two people described it as two different faults and neither could see the cause. The rows carry their own border now - health `4px` = 1 + **2 of fill** + 1, armour `3px` = 1 + **1 of fill** + 1 - and **the anchor moved with them or the pair would sit on the head**: #163 left exactly 1px of clear air, a 5px strip hung at `--head - 6`, and the strip is 3 + 1 + 4 = 8 now, so the hang is **-9**. Checked across five fights and **52 bodies** including the Fen-Mother and the Thing in Armour: every fill ≥ 1px, and the pair overlaps no sprite, no mood face and no white flag. ⛔ **2. THE PARTING SWING WAS ONLY EVER ANNOUNCED AFTER IT WAS CHARGED.** *("Когда выбегаешь из зоны контроля - то давать какой-то ворнинг. Как минимум делать иконку стоп красной и рядом меч и красным подсвечивать юнита - который будет бить".)* Everything the board said before the click was a RING - the red edge under your own feet - and it is drawn on every held hex all turn, so it says *you are held* and never *this step costs blood, and HE is the one taking it*. With MOVE in hand the hex you are standing on now wears **⊘⚔** in the refusal red the `--curStrikeNo` cursors are already drawn in, its ground goes louder, and **every body that would actually swing is lit red** (a drop-shadow on the sprite, not an edge - an edge on a hex somebody is standing on is mostly hidden by them, which is the whole reason the request asks for the unit). ⚑ **#173's own note named four places that ask about control and must agree; the picture is a fifth, so the test stopped being copied and became `partingRisk(u)`** - `partingShots` swings from it, the UNDO snapshot's `clean` test is its emptiness, render draws it. It goes out by itself the instant DISENGAGE is pressed, because `safeMove` empties the list, which is the lesson that card exists to teach said in the picture for the first time. Measured off: safe move, a controller who has spent its swing, a body in your back arc, and an attack card in hand - all four report nothing. ⛔ **3. THE TAP-ROOM SCRIPT COULD DEADLOCK ON ONE MAN'S NERVE, AND TWO MORE DEFECTS SAT BEHIND IT.** *("В начальном бою твои солдаты не присоединяются. Токо бармен ... вы с барменом против 3х типов".)* Every wave is gated on Harl being dead or through the door - wave one on him, wave two on wave one, the crew on wave two - and the break was latched: `T.phase===1` guarded the sentence that breaks him and the first routed frame set `T.phase=2`, so nothing could ever break him twice. ⛑ **Driven end-to-end in the running build with a Captain who does not swing, the brawl sat at phase 2, round 17, two bodies on the field, every wave flag false.** Unlatching it was not enough and the second measurement is the useful one: with the position setter trapped he ran for the door on even rounds (`walkTo<tavernFlee<aiTurn`) and walked back at the Captain on odd ones (`walkTo<step<aiTurn`) from round 4 to round 55, because `capTick` zeroes his nerve and **beginTurn's situational block hands 8 of his 52 back at the top of his own turn - .154 against a .15 break threshold, four tenths of a percent.** ⚑ **A scripted exit may not hang off a number that oscillates**, so it hangs off the script's own flag: once broken he flees whatever his state says, and `tavernFlee` puts his nerve back on the floor at the top of his turn so the plaque and the white flag agree with it. Behind it: **`tavernPlace`'s fallback was the PREFERRED HEX, occupied or not** - two bodies on one hex is one body, because `at()` returns the first, so the second is on the field with no way to see it or hit it, which is exactly "he never came in" (a bounded ring search now, and FALSE rather than a stack); and in `checkEnd`'s catch-net **wave two put a KNIFE on the field and then fell straight through to the win** on a count taken before it fired, while **wave THREE was never in the net at all** though the comment there has promised the crew since #108. Same passive drive after the fix: **Harl out at round 6, wave one at 6, wave two at 8, the crew at 8, ten bodies on the field.** ⚠ **AND ONE OF THESE FIXES SHIPPED BROKEN FOR TEN MINUTES INSIDE THIS ENTRY**: a stray `*/` closed the new CSS comment early and the prose after it was parsed as a selector, which ate `.hin>.zwarn` whole. `#cssTail` did not fire (the parser recovered at the next brace) and the file read correctly. **It was found by reading the COMPUTED style of the mark** - colour bone, 16px, z-index 1 - which is this file's own rule about measuring rather than reading, arriving on its own edit. Gates: `LINT()` 0 · nothing under the 10px floor · overflow diffed against a `git show HEAD:` baseline in a second tab (`#bClass`/`#bTrait` differ by one sub-pixel of zoom rounding on both builds and nothing in the diff can reach them) · `spacingViolations` 0 · `labelViolations` 0 · `MAP_SIGHT` orphans 0 · the #194 road-crosses-a-painting check 0. | ✅ DONE |

THE BOARD CANNOT BE MOVED OUT FROM UNDER THE CHROME, AND THE ARITHMETIC IS WHY.** *("one enemy was positioned behind the End Turn button".)* Measured: the board is 588x416, the FULL stop is z 1.64, so it draws at **964x682 in a 1280x720 window** - 316px of horizontal slack and 38 of vertical. `#bPlq` reaches x 272 at the bottom-left and `#bEnd` starts at x 1046, so the bottom band offers **774px of clear ground to a 964px board**; spending the vertical slack upward instead would crop the top row's heads under `#bCap`, and the two closer stops PAN, so their overlap moves every turn anyway. There is no offset that wins. ⚑ **So the chrome yields instead**: `veilChrome()` measures eight controls against every sprite once a render and drops anything with more than 8% of a body behind it to half opacity, back to full the moment the pointer is on it. Measured across five fights: `#bTop` in three of them, `#bPlq` in one, **`#bEnd` in the Snare - which is the report** - and nothing at all in the two-body tavern. ⚠ `#bActions` is deliberately not in the set: it is the row of cards being chosen from. ⛔ **3. THE ROAD CARD COULD NOT BE SCROLLED AT ALL, AND ITS OWN NEIGHBOUR ALREADY KNEW WHY.** *("scrolling doesn't work very well. I couldn't scroll properly with either the mouse wheel or the scrollbar" - macOS.)* `#wDlg` is a CHILD of `#wMap`, and `wpanInit`'s wheel listener `preventDefault()`s every notch to step the map camera. **Measured: one wheel over the card's own heading came back `defaultPrevented` true with `WCAM.i` 1 to 0** - the card did not move and the map zoomed out behind it. The `pointerdown` handler two lines below has excluded `#wZoom,#wDlg` since it was written; the wheel simply never got the same sentence. ⚑ **And the scrollbar half is the other platform's half**: macOS paints an OVERLAY scrollbar and hides it until something scrolls, and `#bLog` was the only box in the game that styled one - so with the wheel dead there was nothing to grab either. Every scroller now declares a 6px thumb and a `scrollbar-color`. ⚑ **And the "delete where unnecessary" half was real**: the card had **two scrollers nested one inside the other**, and measured on ten cards including the three heaviest **the outer one has never scrolled** (569, 611, 632, 649, 657 against a 658 cap, `scrollHeight === clientHeight` on every one) while the prose box scrolled on exactly one. It is `overflow:hidden` now - not `visible`, because `#wChoices` is `position:sticky` and needs a scroll container to pin against. ⚠ The stage is a fixed 1280x720 that is SCALED, never reflowed, so those numbers are the same on every device. ⛔ **4. AN ACT THAT APPLIES AUTOMATICALLY HAS NO SELECTED STATE TO DRAW.** *("When I hover over a skill that applies automatically, it would be nice if it showed the range/distance for that skill".)* COMMAND (3), HOLD THE LINE (2), ROAR (2) and SHE CALLS THEM IN (4) have no `range` and no `reach` - they have no TARGET - so the footprint every other act draws was the one thing the board would not paint. ⛑ **The first cut hung it off `B.sel` and that was wrong for a reason only the running build said**: these acts resolve INSIDE `selectAct` - spend, apply, `autoEnd` - so **clicking the COMMAND card leaves `B.sel` as the standing MOVE and hands the turn on**. The request said HOVER and the request was right. It rides `paintPeek` now, the one light overlay pass, and **it paints occupied hexes**, which is the whole difference between an order and a blow: a swing wants to know where it can LAND, an order wants to know WHO it reaches. Measured: COMMAND lights 33 hexes and three bodies, HOLD THE LINE 18 and two, MOVE clears it. ⚑ **`aura:` is the field and four sites read it** - the handler that charges the effect, the ring the FX draws, the card's sub-line (*1 ACT · 3 HEXES*) and this preview. It was written in three places per act before. ⚠ SPEAR WALL gets no ring: its zone is the FRONT ARC at reach 2, not a radius, and a circle would be the wrong shape. ⛔ **5. THE LANE WORD COMES OFF THE HEX.** *("коли наводиш лучника, не обов'язково прописувати знизу кліер шот ор блокд - колір лінії та % вже дають достатньо інфи".)* #36 shipped `.hlos` with an argument that was true on the day and has been overtaken by its own build: *"a percentage alone cannot say which"*. It is not alone any more - the lane is DRAWN, in `SHOT[k].col` with `SHOT[k].dash`, and the hex is filled per state. ⚑ **Nothing is lost, and that is why the deletion is safe**: the lane is still in WORDS one hover away, where `hitBreakdown` writes *screened*, *a body in the way*, *over a boulder*, *long shot* and *extreme range* beside the figure each one costs. ⚠ BLOCKED is the one case the premise does not cover, because its percentage is deliberately withdrawn; the dark red fill and the red dashed ray carry it. ⚠ **AND A MEASUREMENT TRAP WORTH KEEPING: A CSS TRANSITION IN A NON-COMPOSITING PANE NEVER ADVANCES.** The veil read `opacity: 1` with the class provably on the element and the rule provably the only match - because `.veiled` carries a `.18s` transition and the hidden preview pane freezes its clock at the value it is LEAVING. `getAnimations().forEach(a=>a.finish())` before the read is the fix, and it reported 0.5 at once. | 8f.231 | **#208 - SKILL-TREE GROUNDWORK: THE THREE RULINGS THE BATTLE COULD ALREADY EXPRESS.** *(User, 2026-08-20, while iterating the skill-tree lab: "For capitan basic skill that availible from the begining - hold the line that gives a dodge. It costs 1", "Boost morale automatically unlocks from 3d lvl", "Spear wall inposible to do when enemy near by (make it unuvalible and kind of grey - and if hover on grey - the reason)". The fourth ruling of the same message, DISENGAGE off the basic set and into a general perk, is deliberately NOT built: the game has no general-skill tree yet, and the whole zone-of-control economy (#72, #111, #137) hangs off every body owning DISENGAGE - removing it before the tree exists to grant it back would soft-lock every scrum. It waits in the lab with the tree.)* HOLD THE LINE costs 1 instead of 2: at 2 it was the whole turn, so it competed with COMMAND and lost, and the user's ruling makes it the starter a level-1 captain actually uses. COMMAND is gated to `(p.level\|\|1)>=3` inside unitFrom(), so the morale order unlocks ITSELF at level 3, no perk point: the arm before the voice. Foes never pass through unitFrom(), so no enemy lost anything. And SPEAR WALL cannot be RAISED in a scrum: `wallBlocked()` sits beside wallLive() asking the same adjacency question with the same bracewall exemption, canUse() refuses off it (one door, so both AI brains, AUTO, the hotkeys and the click inherit the refusal), and the greyed card paints NO ROOM: SOMETHING IS ALREADY INSIDE YOUR REACH in its receipt exactly the way a cooldown paints READY IN N. ⚠ **wallLive() never threw outside a battle only by ACCIDENT**: its `u.spearwall` early-out fires before alive() reads `B.units`, and B is null between fights (measured: it throws). wallBlocked() has no such accident, so it guards `!B` explicitly. Verified by probe on the served build: a level-1 captain fields MOVE · sword · KICK · HOLD THE LINE(1) · DISENGAGE and no COMMAND; at level 3 COMMAND appears; the spearwoman keeps her wall. The design bench for the whole tree is `tools/skilltree_lab.html`: level-0 class doors that ARE the weapon mastery, class skills granted free at 3 and 6, a bought level-9 skill with a real downside per class, the multiclass-or-mastery capstone at 10, and the grey-with-reason mechanic demonstrated on the wall and on wrong-weapon class skills. | |

Three voices fire on shipped prose with no new writing: THE BROKEN MEN's *"Food and the cart," the captain says*, OUR CALIBRE OF FILTH's *"You are our calibre of filth"*, and THE COIN IN THE ROAD's `{TELLER}` paragraph, whose finder is then the face on the plate. ⛔ **AND THE TELLER IS ONLY THIS CARD'S SUBJECT IF THIS CARD ASKS FOR HIM.** `G.fireTeller` is never cleared, by design - `{TELLER}` must resolve to the same body in the body, the outcome and the effect prose - so the first cut put the coin's finder on the front plate of A WEDDING ON THE ROAD three nodes later. The card's own raw text is the gate. ⛔ **ONE STAGE, THREE DECKS, ONE FUNCTION.** `evStageOpen` is what makes the form canon rather than a treatment the road happens to get: the road, the fire and the vignette had two and a half renderers between them, which is exactly the shape `choiceNote` was merged out of in #150, `raceDoorPick` in #159, `applyHurt` in #197 and the mood echo in #176 - one fact with two implementations, and every one of them had drifted by the time it was found. Each deck now hands in four things: its title, its prose, its doors, and what pressing one does. **The camp card's thirteen-line door builder was deleted** (it said the same thing as openEvent's thirteen), the fire's lead came off the header and onto the encounter mark, and the vignette's old `ON THE ROAD - ON THE ROAD` fix promptly became the bug again one line apart - same rule, one surface out: the more specific line keeps the lead and the other says nothing. ⛑ **THE MODE FOLLOWS THE CONTENT AND NOT A CLASS ON `#wDlg`.** Twelve functions open that box; a class the other eleven had to clear is a rule that survives until somebody writes the thirteenth, so the stylesheet gates on `:has(.evstage)` and the muster, the shop, the returner and the withdraw screen were verified untouched at 620px wide. ⛔ **THE DOORS ARE ON THE GAME'S OWN PALETTE**, which is his third note answered literally: the sketch's `#63766f`/`#82958b`/`rgba(13,22,21)` are this build's BATTLE chrome (`--e4`/`--e5`), i.e. a colour that means *you are on the field*. Every hex is lifted off the column card's own `.choice` rules - `#2a2114` ground, `--e2` edge, `--g3` lit, `#8c3a31`/`--blood-lit` for the fight, `#5a7a6a`/`#8ca35a` for the race door - and the prose voices took the same treatment (`--gem` and `--wood`, not the sketch's teal and orange). ⚠ **AND THE SKETCH'S OWN TYPE WOULD NOT HAVE PASSED**: it sets the door label at 12.5px and its receipt at **8px**, under the 10px FLOOR, and clips both with `white-space:nowrap;text-overflow:ellipsis` - the exact regression table in `.claude/rules/ui-scales.md` §4. Every size on the stage is a `--fs` step, every pad a `--p` step, every structural edge an `--e`, and the door label WRAPS. **THE PACK**: the eight intents and three races are paintings now (`INTENT_ART`/`RACE_ART`), plus eight wide **1672x941** stages (`JSTAGE_ART`), built by `art/build_j_pack.py` into their **own marker** `/*__J_PACK__*/` - never into `/*__ART_DATA__*/`, because that injector rewrites 10 MB and two desks running it is the unresolvable conflict at the foot of `PARALLEL_SESSIONS.md`, and this is the half of the build still being redrawn. ⚠ **A MARK IS PNG AND A STAGE IS JPEG**: the marks are cut-outs on a button's own ground and a JPEG has no alpha; a stage has no transparency anywhere and a PNG of one is 2 MB against 180 KB. ⚠ **AND A `cook`/`as-delivered` FLAG RATHER THAN A MEASUREMENT.** The first cut gated the alpha floor on *how much of the canvas is faint*, and that number cannot tell the two cases apart: the v1 intents read 2-8% because they carry a real wash across 384x512, and the v3 race cards read 2.7-4.4% because a one-pixel antialiasing ring is a big share of a 128px box. Same number, opposite meanings; what actually differs is whether the file still has to be resized, and only the pipeline knows that. ⚠ **A MARK IS A COLUMN ON THE STAGE AND A CHARACTER EVERYWHERE ELSE** - the finding `.claude/rules/static-event-art.md` already wrote down for the item icons. The stage gives it a 44px cell (**and a card whose doors are ALL bare does not reserve it**, which is 2 camp deals in 40); the camp card, the loot picker and the aftermath's road button keep it inline at **17x22, the biggest size that costs nothing** (measured on a three-door fire card: 16x20 and 17x22 both leave every row at 54px and the card at 575, 18x24 takes two rows to 55). ⛑ **THE TREATMENT FOLLOWS THE PICTURE, AND THE CLASS IS CALLED `fit` FOR A REASON.** The dimming and the blur exist to make a 586x212 letterbox survive a 1.9:1 crop; a J master is composed for this frame and the PROCEDURAL fallback is drawn at this box's own size, so neither is being cropped and running the crop treatment over them read as mud on the right half of the wedding. Derived at render time, so a card re-exported wide is one row in `JSTAGE` and nothing else is typed. ⚠ **`placeDlg` MEASURES THE CARD NOW** instead of reading `DLGW`: there are two card widths on the road and a constant centred the wrong one, opening the stage 280px right of centre - #197's own complaint through the back door. Verified landing identically from nodes at x100 and x1216. ⚠ **A COLUMN FLEX SHRINKS ITS ITEMS BEFORE IT SCROLLS**, which came back as THE BROKEN MEN's outcome with its chip row cut in half and no scrollbar; `flex:0 0 auto` on every child, found on the OUTCOME and not on the card, because a card that fits never shows it. ⚠ **AND THE BLURRED BACKGROUND IS SCALED FROM THE BOTTOM-RIGHT CORNER**: the sketch writes down that its own scene reports 19x9 of deliberate overflow, and `.claude/rules/ui-scales.md` §5 forbids adding a fifth entry to the known-overflow list to make a decoration fit. | 8f.235 | **#212 - AN ALLY IN THE LANE IS A PRICE NOW AND NEVER A REFUSAL, ASHA GETS HER NAME, AND HER FIRE CARD WAITS FOR THE FEN.** *(User, 2026-08-20, three notes: “change full block of ally for an archer, it reduses accurecy rather then block fully”, “Not name ash - but Asha”, “Event on the road with asha a bit later - after fen mother (when she tells stories)”.)* ⛔ **`losState` COUNTED BODIES AND IT COUNTS ENEMIES NOW.** The old rule was `seen.length>1 -> BLOCKED`, i.e. any two bodies in the lane withdrew the shot, so the commonest way for a player to lose a bow for a turn was **their own line closing up in front of it** - and a withdrawn offer reads as the game taking the turn away rather than as a position to fix. The block is now taken off the FOES in the lane alone and what your own people cost is a new fifth state, **CROWDED, -20**. ⚑ **IT KEEPS THE FUNCTION'S OLDEST LAW, WHICH IS WHY IT IS FIVE LINES AND NOT A REWRITE**: #82's *the lane is worth the worst thing in it, never the sum*. One enemy across it is -22 whether or not your shieldman is standing there too, because -22 is already the worse of the two - so nothing adds up and the verdict is still readable off the board. -20 also needed no argument about where it goes in the one-word ladder: the ladder has been sorted by COST since #82, so CROWDED slots between the enemy in the lane (-22) and extreme range (-18) by arithmetic. ⛔ **AND THE REFUSAL IT REPLACES WAS COPIED INTO FOUR PLACES, WHICH IS THE REASON `laneClean` EXISTS.** Both AI brains hand-wrote *a body across the lane* as `k!=='BLOCKED'&&k!=='OBSTRUCTED'`, twice each; a fifth state would have had to be remembered in all four and the next caller written without it. `LANETIER` already held the answer (CROWDED and OBSTRUCTED are both tier 2, both five hexes of walking), so one predicate now serves the preference in `aiTurn`, the preference in `autoStep` and both `laneOK` filters. ⚠ **AND THE PREFERENCE HAD TO GROW, NOT SHRINK**: #36's contract is *an archer who occasionally puts one through his own shieldman reads as broken*, and that reading got MORE likely the day the lane stopped refusing him, so a crowded lane is refused outright while a clean one exists exactly as an obstructed one is. ⛑ **TWO THINGS THE BOARD WAS SAYING WRONG, BOTH FOUND BY READING THE DOM RATHER THAN THE CODE.** `clickHex`'s BLOCKED line said *“you would be shooting your own people”* - the one case the branch can no longer reach - and `inWay` marked **one** body, off `los.who`, in a state that is by definition about more than one: the board was telling the player to move the front man and the lane was still crowded once he had. `los.all` is the bodies the VERDICT is about and not everything in the lane, which is not the same list: one of theirs plus two of yours is OBSTRUCTED and marking your two men as the problem would be the readout naming the wrong bodies. ⚑ **PROVED AGAINST A `git show HEAD:` BASELINE DRIVEN TO THE SAME BOARD**, two headless browsers, same row, same archer, same two men standing in it: HEAD says **BLOCKED / mayAim false / no odds**, this build says **CROWDED / mayAim true / 29%**, and the six-case sweep reads CLEAR 0 · SCREENED -8 · CROWDED -20 (one of yours, not braced) · CROWDED -20 (two of yours) · OBSTRUCTED -22 (one of theirs among them) · BLOCKED (two of theirs). **(2) SHE IS `Asha “Nobody’s”`, AND THE `id` STAYS `ash`.** The id is not a name: `castMember`, `member()`, her sprite key `ratkin_you_thief`, her portrait `POR10` and every run already in `localStorage` reach her through it. Renaming it would have renamed her in five places that are not the screen and stranded every saved run. `LINT`'s NAMES list moved with the roster it guards. **(3) `afterNode` IS THE FIFTH GATE IN `campPool`, AND THE FIRST ONE THAT IS A PLACE.** `castMember` already held THE WRONG COLOUR back until she is in the company - and she joins at Blood on the Road, the fourth node of the run - so a card that is *a woman who has never told anybody her story telling it* was landing at the first or second fire the company ever built, on strangers. It waits for The Black Fen now. ⚠ **A NODE ID AND NOT A DAY COUNT, DELIBERATELY**: `armourWhere` carries the long note on why a rule anchored to PROGRESS breaks silently the day the map is reordered, and the Fen is the beat the user named. ⛑ **AND THE GATE FAILS THE SAME TWO SILENT WAYS EVERY GATE IN THAT BLOCK FAILS**, so `LINT` reads both: a node the map no longer has, and a node only SOME companies walk - either way the card simply never comes, for the life of the project. Proved by moving the gate into each fault on purpose and watching it report (`afterNode “nowhere” is not a node on the map`, `afterNode “sling” is missable`), then restoring it to silence. `wed -> mother -> vill` is the only way through, so nobody can miss it. **Gates, in the running build:** `LINT()` **0** · zero font-size literals, one padding literal (`#wBar`'s documented 64px), zero hardcoded structural edges · nothing under the 10px floor on the battle screen · the clipped-box probe **identical on both builds** (`#bField`, `#bClass`, `#bLog`, and `#bTrait` measuring 3px on any body that has one, which is the documented `-webkit-line-clamp:2`) · no em dash in `EVENTS` or `CAMPS`, and none added anywhere · every road door still wears an intent, none wears three, `mystery` still 1, 0 figures in any `c:` · the card still measures **137 words, MEDIUM**, because not one word of it changed · the incident pool is 8 before the Fen and 9 after, so nothing dead-ends. | ✅ DONE |
| 8f.236 | **#213 - THE PRICE OF LEAVING A ZONE OF CONTROL MOVES OFF THE BODY AND ONTO THE HAND.** *(User, 2026-08-20, with a screenshot: "when in zone of control show engayd (croosed cirlce and nives) not on the character But rather near your movment cursor - it changes from white to red and it adds the knives. And highlights enemy who will hit and probability".)* ⛔ **#205 DREW ⊘⚔ OVER THE TORSO OF THE BODY ABOUT TO MOVE, AND IT IS THE RIGHT FACT IN THE WRONG PLACE.** While a move is being chosen the thing the eye follows is the POINTER; the body it was drawn on is the one square the player has already stopped looking at. So the mark is `--curWalkZoc` now - the walk cursor in red with crossed blades hung off the corner - and it is stamped by a grid-level `zoc` class on `#bGrid.cMove.zoc .hex.lit`. ⚠ **`.lit` AND NOT EVERY HEX**: that is #206's own test for "ground a click would actually take", so the red hand appears on exactly the hexes that would charge the swing and the plain boots stay on ground that is not a step at all. Measured live: `.lit` → red, an enemy hex → the bow (the click there looses an arrow, #155), everything else → white. ⛔ **AND THE ⊘ DOES NOT COME WITH IT, WHICH IS NOT A TRIM.** ⊘ is what the three refusal cursors mean, and this step is ALLOWED - it just costs blood. A refusal glyph on a legal move is one mark meaning two things on the one surface where being wrong stops the click. ⚠ Same `13 13` hotspot and the same 26px of boots, or the pointer JUMPS when the cursor swaps; 32x32 and not 34, because Firefox refuses a larger cursor outright. ⛑ **AND THE SECOND HALF WAS THE HALF #205 NEVER ANSWERED: HOW LIKELY.** It lit the sprite red and stopped, so the board said WHO and never HOW MUCH - and two of them at 22% is a different decision from one at 68%. Each swinger now carries `⚔NN%` off `hitBreakdown(e,u,atk)`, and the figure is EXACT rather than an estimate because of the call order: `partingShots(u)` fires BEFORE `walkTo`, so the roll happens while the mover is still on this hex, and the 0.5 the swing carries is DAMAGE and never touches the chance. `atk` is the same `acts.find(a=>a.reach)` `partingShots` swings with, so the promise and the till cannot disagree. ⚠ Keyed on the swinger's own hex and not on `hexesOf`: the Fen-Mother is ringed on her tail and carries the number once, verified live (2 lit hexes, 1 chip). ⚡ **THE PLACEMENT WAS A MEASUREMENT AND THE FIRST CUT WAS WRONG.** Top-left measured **31px wide in a 33px box**, i.e. straight across `.hodds`. Driven at the 2.2 camera stop the 37x42 hex reads `.hin` y 3-40, `.hodds` y 7-17, `.hdmg` y 19-29, the sprite y -12-33, and the row in FRONT painting later over y 32-42 - so there is no uncontested slot, only the cheapest collision. It went to the FOOT of the hex with an opaque plate and a red edge, because a plate survives a body standing there where a text-shadow does not, and because the two readouts it would otherwise cover are on screen every turn while this one is on screen for the length of one decision. LINT 0, map counters 0/0/0, the battle overflow probe still the documented three. | ✅ |
