| 8f.238 | **#215 - THE MIRROR, AND THREE THINGS THE BATTLEFIELD WAS SAYING TOO LOUDLY OR TO THE WRONG CREATURE.** *(User, 2026-08-21, four notes: "show % of opportunity atack only if i am triyng to move from the enemy (when cursor of movment hovers red). Otherwise don`t show", "Don`t show class for beasts animals on the battlfield", "Morale more transparent on the batlfield", and "i want you design mirrored battle. When all of your actions mirrored. You chosing to move left - and moving right, etc. Put it in the practie field so far. Same with hitting".)* **(1) THE PARTING-SWING FIGURE SPEAKS ONLY WHILE A STEP IS BEING AIMED.** #213 put `⚔NN%` on the board for as long as MOVE was in hand - and MOVE is the STANDING selection since #91, so a body two enemies are holding carried two red plates through the whole turn, including while the player was reading the board or deciding not to move at all. It rides the same test the red hand rides now: `.lit` ground under the pointer, i.e. exactly the hexes `--curWalkZoc` appears on. ⛑ **THE GATE IS A CLASS AND NOT `:has(.hex.lit:hover)`, AND THAT IS THE ONE REAL DECISION IN THE ITEM.** The `:has()` version was written first, shipped, and measured working - and then the mirror below needed a pointer tracker anyway, because there the hex the click LANDS on is not the hex the pointer is over. Two hover mechanisms describing one pointer is the *one fact, two implementations* shape this file has deleted four times (`choiceNote` #150, `raceDoorPick` #159, the mood echo #176, `applyHurt` #197), so `handOn`/`paintHand` is the one, `#bGrid.aiming` is what it stamps, and the state lives on `B` so a render cannot lose it. ⚠ The red GLOW on the swinger stays: *these bodies have a hand on you* is true all turn, *and this one lands 41 times in a hundred* is an answer only a destination asks for. **(2) A BEAST IS NOT A PEOPLE, AND THE RULE ONLY EVER COVERED HALF THE LINE.** This file has said *"classes are a human idea... monsters get their kind and their nature, never a class"* since the plaque was written, and *"a monster is never a profession - it gets its bestiary face and nothing else"* on the token badge. Both are about the CLASS. Nothing said it about the RACE or the PORTRAIT, so on the shipped build **a Lurcher's hover readout said `🐺 PACK-BEAST · 🐀 RATKIN` with #211's ratkin painting beside the word, and the battle plaque drew `C1_Ratkin_Rank_and_File` on every dog and every mirehare - `C2_Ratkin_Chieftain` on The Bitch.** ⚠ **AND THE `race` FIELD IS NOT THE BUG TO FIX**: `build()` derives race from kind and a dog falls through to 'ratkin', which `ringOf` (the SWARM), the AI's weights and the barks all read - shipped balance, and not what was asked about. The DISPLAY changes; the fact stays. ⛑ **`BEAST_KIND` IS `build()`'s OWN `ANIMAL` LIST, HOISTED** - it was already sitting inside that function deciding who gets a personality (*"a dog is a dog"*), which is the same question, so it is one array with two readers, the move #195 made with `CUBE_AXIS`. `big`/`passive` keep `C5` (`C5_The_Cub`, a beast sheet); everything else falls to the generic silhouette the Warden already uses. ⚠ A fen-thing is deliberately NOT on the list: its kind IS 'ratkin' and it is a twisted one, so its sheet and its race word are both true of it. **(3) THE MOOD FACE, TURNED DOWN A SECOND TIME.** .66 -> **.40**, grayscale .35 -> **.55**, and the loud rungs (😄 😟 💀) .95 -> **.78** with them, because what has to survive is the GAP between a rung that is news and one that is not. The paragraph directly above it in the CSS is #164 answering this exact complaint once already, which is the shape the battle-screen entries keep recording: by the second report a note that sounds like taste is a number that is wrong. Deleting the quiet faces outright was considered and refused - a token with NO face already means HAPPY (#91 point 16), so a second silent rung would make that read meaningless. **(4) THE MIRROR: A PRACTICE-FIELD RULE WHERE THE HAND IS FOLDED.** `SIM.rule` is the fourth picker on the practice field (`straight` | `mirror`), fenced exactly the way `SIM.ground` has been since #61 - `mirrorOn()` asks `SIM.on` FIRST and `simRestore` clears it on every exit, so it cannot reach a run. ⛑ **THE FOLD SITS BETWEEN THE POINTER AND THE BOARD, AND THAT ONE DECISION IS WHY IT IS FOUR FUNCTIONS AND NOT A PASS OVER THE ENGINE.** `clickHex` is already the ONE door every board click goes through - a step, a swing, a shot, a working, both halves of a throw - so folding its argument answered *"same with hitting"* for free and can never fall out of step with a rule it does not know about. And because the fold is on the POINTER, everything that is not a hand (AUTO and both AI brains, which call `clickHex` directly) plays straight: **the mirror is a fact about the player, not about the game.** ⛔ **ACROSS THE ACTOR, NEVER ACROSS THE BOARD.** Reflecting in the board's centre line was the first idea and it is unplayable - the acting body does not move, so a hex one step away lands eleven columns off. ⛑ **IN CUBE SPACE IT IS A SWAP AND THE ROW NEVER MOVES**: take the delta from the actor, exchange x and y, add it back. `x+y+z=0` survives the exchange and z IS the row, so a mirrored hex is on its own row with its column folded about the actor's - a true vertical mirror in screen pixels, half-row offset included (verified against `hexCentre`: an east neighbour is +38px and its fold -38, an up-right one +19 and its fold -19). Written in `DIR` terms it would have needed a per-parity table, which is the second copy of the geometry #173 spent an entry deleting. ⚠ **OFF THE BOARD IS A REFUSAL AND IT COSTS NOTHING** - same contract a blocked lane and an oversized shove already have - and it is the mode's one tactical consequence: in the mirror the middle of the board is where your options are, and a body backed into a corner has half of them folded into nothing. ⚠ **READING THE BOARD IS NOT FOLDED.** Odds, damage, the readout card and the lore boxes all answer about the hex they are on; what is folded is the hand, and `paintHand` marks the landing hex with the same ivory ring `.lit:hover` already draws, so no click is ever silently dead. It is an inverted mouse, not a blindfold. The four act-cursors come OFF in the mirror (a `--curStrike` over an enemy the click will not touch is #102's wrong-unit rule with the pointer as the unit) and one mirrored pair of boots goes on; a dashed line down the acting body's column says what the fold is without a word. ⚠ The fourth picker is a ROW and not a fourth column, by arithmetic: the grid is measured at 255px a column on the 860px card and a fourth takes all four to 191, which puts "Something in Armour" onto two lines. Adding it took `.pcard` to **683 against its 684px ceiling**, i.e. one pixel of overflow and a scrollbar on a card that has never had one, so `.simpick`'s max-height went 352 -> 336 (all three lists already scroll; the card is 667 now). **Gates, in the running build:** `LINT()` **0** · zero font-size literals, one padding literal (`#wBar`'s documented 64px), zero hardcoded structural edges · nothing under the 10px floor on the picker or the battle screen, in either mode · the clipped-box probe **identical to a `git show HEAD:` baseline driven to the same two screens** (practice picker 0/0, battle 3/3: the documented `#bField`, `#bTrait`, `#bLog`) · map counters 0/0/0 · no em dash added (1118 before, 1118 after, 0 on any added line) · all eight `regress()` fights complete, guard `ok`, no errors, and they complete unchanged with `SIM.rule='mirror'` set because AUTO does not use the pointer's door. **Driven, not read:** pointing at a hex to the RIGHT walked the body to the LEFT, onto the exact fold; pointing at the left ogre put 17hp into the right one and left the left one untouched; a fold that lands off the board moved nothing, spent nothing and said so; the landing mark survives a render and clears on leaving the grid. ⚠ **AND A HARNESS TRAP COST HALF AN HOUR AND IS WORTH WRITING DOWN**: `runFight` stubs `render=()=>{}` and restores it in a `finally`, capturing `_r=render` on entry - so a `regress()` that is CUT OFF mid-run leaves the stub installed, and the next `runFight` captures the stub as "the real one" and restores THAT. Every later render is a no-op, which reads exactly like a broken feature. The tell is `B.stamp` staying `undefined` after an explicit `render()`; `render.toString()` is the confirmation. | ✅ |
| 8f.239 | **#216 - THE PRACTICE FIELD PRICES EVERYTHING ON ONE SCALE, THE CREW IS HAND-PICKED, AND SIX FIGHTS STAY LOCKED UNTIL THE ROAD SHOWS THEM.** *(User, 2026-08-21: "when piking my crew - that you can pick specific caracters, Also you can lvl up them and add artifacts from the pool. Each character and artifact has its points value. And for each fight it is recomended point values for easy, mid, hard. Not in the dev mod, important fights (like fen mother) or rabbits, or thing in the arnor - locked, until you open it in the campeign. This one in the future would help both for AI and manual balancing, and also for safely chickng the game and learning to fight." And: "each fight has recomended points value (the points value we should calculate for each fight in the game, so we can compare thread lvls later). But it is visible only in dev mod for campeign. In the dev mod you can show this points for battls also on global map (so it would be easier to balance the final route)".)* ⛔ **THE POINTS ARE DERIVED, NEVER AUTHORED - #137's rule priced into a scale.** Both sides of every fight come out of the same two factories (`unitFrom` for the roster, `build` for a statblock), so ONE `unitPts` prices the unit and everything else is a sum: a character is what he would be on the field (gear, trait, level, perks by construction), a fight is what it fields minus what stands beside you (the clash's allies SUBTRACT, and a list takes the worst door while a door's own dev chip prices its own side), and an artifact is what it adds to the body wearing it. sqrt(offence x staying) x PTS_SCALE 0.42, **and the four founders came out at exactly 100 on the first calibration**: "bring 100 points" means "bring the company the run starts with". ⚑ **THE SCALE AGREES WITH ARENA MEASUREMENTS IT NEVER READ**: the seasoned six prices 202 and the six-body Snare 199 - a 0.99 ratio on the fight #160 measured at 45% win - and the ladder sorts the act (brawl 36 · chase 51 · wedding 60 · clash 72 · mirehares 92 · pack 99 · brigand 109 · slingline 120 · hold patrol 140 · snare 146 · steading 149 · mother 157 · armour 164 · host at the bells 175 · circle 257, the unwinnable one at 2.6x the founders). ⚠ **TWO BLIND SPOTS THE FIRST NUMBERS EXPOSED**: the wedding priced 80 because the formula could not read `drunk`, which is that card's whole difficulty tuning (#159) - it reads it now and the massacre sits at 60; and five levels moved a spearman TWO points, because most perks are conditional (+8 dodge if unmoved) and price as nothing, so a level carries +3% for what the formula cannot see. ⚠ **EVERYTHING RANDOM IS SEEDED FOR THE MEASUREMENT** (`seededly`): the steading rolls its wall, build() rolls foe personalities, a caster tops up his schools - one number per build, or the picker flickers. The tavern is special-cased to the room's whole roster minus the barman, because its waves belong to the script and not the builder. ⛔ **THE LOCKS ARE META, NOT SAVE**: `gt_seen_fights` is marked at the one door beside the journal write in startBattle (same `!SIM.on` gate, so a practice fight can never unlock anything), survives run death because a spoiler only works once, and armour · mother · mirehares · snare · circle · wedding wait behind it; TEST.on opens all, with `dev-open` on the row. ⚑ **THE BENCH IS RECIPES, NOT PEOPLE**: a hand-picked row is (source, level, gear) and the person is REBUILT from it on every read, so lowering a level can never leave yesterday's arithmetic on the body; levels are paid through levelUp's own bank and spent by policy (stats down the class's priority, perks the first open door of each tier - the bench says so in its own footer); the Captain is row one and unremovable; twelve is the cap because twelve is the deployment-slot count. Dev readouts: `⚔ N pts · you M` on every fight door through choiceNote's own join, and the worst door's points over every battle node on the map (9 nodes chip on the shipped map, 0 with TEST off). LINT grew the wiring check (every fight prices to something, a lock may only name a fight that exists), proved by seeding a bogus lock key: 0 → 1 → 0 findings. Gates: node parse clean, LINT 0, font floor 0, overflow probe clean on the bench, the three scale greps closed against the HEAD baseline. ⏳ **The easy/even/hard multipliers (1.3 / 1.0 / 0.75) are a first cut** and they assume the player's tactical edge is worth nothing, which it is not (the hold patrol at even points measured 93% for the six in #183) - measure the bands against win rates when the balancing pass starts. | ✅ DONE |
| 8f.240 | **#217 - A BEAST IS A RACE OF ITS OWN, AND THE DOGS STOP BEING RATKIN.** *(User, 2026-08-21: "we can put independent race 'monters' - with differnt stats" and "but monsters doesnt have 'class' like humanoids".)* #215 fixed what a dog SAID and left what a dog IS, on the argument that the `race` field is shipped balance. That was the right call with the information it had, and the information was incomplete: **the field had five readers, not three, and two of them are stats.** ⛔ **THE LAST BRANCH OF THE DERIVATION IS A DEFAULT, SO IT CAUGHT EVERY DOG IN THE GAME.** `build()` named `wyrm` and `cub` as beasts and swept the remainder into 'ratkin', which is how The Bitch, the Lurchers, the Runts and the Hold hound have shipped as ratkin since the line was written. `BEAST_KIND` is the list and #215 had already hoisted it out of this same function - one array, three readers now (who gets a personality, who is stamped `monster`, and what race a body is), which is #137's derive-never-author rule and the reason a new beast needs no line here at all. **WHAT THE FIVE READERS WERE, AND THE TWO NOBODY HAD COUNTED:** ⚑ (1) **THE SWARM**, and it is KEPT: `FORM.swarmRaces` is `['ratkin','beast']`, because the rule's own line has always read *"too many small bodies on you to swing at any one of them"*, which is as true of a pack as of a clan, and a pack surrounding you is what that fight is about. Humans stay off it for the reason the header already gives. ⛔ **AND IT WAS TWO LITERALS, NOT ONE**: the rule inside `hitBreakdown` and the **≋ badge the hex draws** each spelled `'ratkin'` themselves, so the day one moved the board would have marked a body the rule did not charge - #102's wrong-unit bug with a formation as the unit. One list, both sites. ⚑ (2) **THE STRIDE, WHICH IS A STAT AND WAS NEVER MEANT TO BE ONE HERE.** `speed:RACESTEP[rc]||Math.max(2,t.speed)`, and the comment three lines above it says in words *"Monsters are not a race and keep their authored speeds."* The Runt is authored at **6** and every Runt in the game has been fielded at **5** since that line was written, because 'ratkin' has a `RACESTEP` row and 'beast' does not. The comment was right and the code disagreed with it. ⚑ (3) **THE DODGE, WHICH IS THE OTHER STAT, AND THE POINTS WERE MOVED RATHER THAN DELETED.** `raceKey` returns null for a beast, so the silent `RACEDODGE.ratkin` **+5** every dog was drawing goes away - and every dog's authored `dodge` went up by exactly 5 in the same edit (Bitch 24->29, Lurcher 26->31, Runt 28->33, Hold hound 25->30). The shipped number is unchanged and it is now written on the statblock, which is #176's rule (*when a deletion would take a fact with it, move the fact*) and the user's own framing in one move: a monster's numbers live on its own sheet. ⛔ **A BLANKET `beast:+5` IN `RACEDODGE` WAS THE OTHER OPTION AND IT IS THE WRONG ONE** - it would have handed the same five points to the Fen-Mother, the cub and both mirehares, none of which have ever had them. ⚑ (4) **THE BARKS AND (5) THE AI TEMPERAMENT WERE BOTH ALREADY ANSWERED, AND CHECKING BEAT ASSUMING ON BOTH.** `bark()` gates on `u.monster` and never on race, with a comment naming this exact trap; #215's `monster` stamp is what closes it. And `doctrine()` returns EARLY on `u.kind==='dog'` with its own hardcoded loner brain, while every use of `const rat=u.race==='ratkin'` sits behind `on&&` - so `rat` is computed for a dog and applied on neither path. **Nothing to fix, and the only way to know that was to read the branch rather than the grep.** ⛑ **AND FIXING THE RACE BROKE A SOUND THAT WAS ALREADY BROKEN, WHICH IS WHY `sfxStrike` IS IN THIS ENTRY.** Its body picker named `cub` and otherwise leaned on the dogs arriving as 'ratkin' to get a sample at all, so the moment the race was honest a dog would have fallen through to **`hit_human`** - a dog sounding like a person being hit. It reads `isBeast` now, which also caught that **the MIREHARES have been landing on `hit_human` since #193**, because they declare `race:'beast'` and the chain had no branch for it. **Gates, in the running build:** `LINT()` **0** · all eight `regress()` fights complete, guard `ok`, no errors · **55 enemy bodies across all fifteen `startBattle` keys swept** (not the harness's eight - the Hold hound is fielded by `chase`/`hold` and the mirehares by their own key, so the eight-fight list had never once built a hound), **0 warnings**: every beast is `race:'beast'`, carries `monster`, draws no personality, draws nothing off `RACEDODGE`, and sounds `hit_beast`. **Measured, not read:** the pack fight over 200 seeded runs, **200/200 won** before and after. Forcing the Runt back to 5 reproduces the pre-change baseline **exactly** - 5.79 rounds, 1.02 of yours down, and the same swarm count to the strike - which isolates the whole behavioural delta of the change to that one number: **+0.3 rounds (5.79 -> 6.09) and a 200-run worst case of 3 bodies down instead of 2.** The swarm still fires at 3.5% of your swings, and it fires slightly MORE than with the Runt clipped, which is the right direction: the fastest dog arriving is the fastest dog arriving. ⚑ **AND THE FIRST PROBE REPORTED THE SWARM AT 0% ON A BUILD WHERE IT WAS FIRING NORMALLY**, because the measuring script hardcoded `'ratkin'` in exactly the way the two call sites did. The instrument had the same bug as the thing it was measuring. **The ≋ badge and the -14 were then proved to be one question by putting a body in the fault on purpose**: three dogs on it reads `≋ Swarmed: -14 to hit` on the hex AND `swarmed -14` in the breakdown, two dogs reads `(none)` and no swarm line. A check that has only ever fired one way proves nothing. ⚠ **THE THING IN ARMOUR IS `race:'unknown'` AND STILL SOUNDS `hit_human`, DELIBERATELY UNTOUCHED**: a sample that said *beast* would answer the one question that card exists to refuse. ⚠ **AND LANDING IT NEEDED `claim.ps1 verify` TO LEARN A CASE NOBODY HAD HIT.** The pre-commit guard scans a diff's added lines for `#NNN`/`8f.NNN` and refuses any another session holds - and it **cannot tell a number being SPENT from one being CITED**, which did not matter for as long as every desk branched off main, because main's numbers are swept and the only held number a diff could name was a foreign one. This entry is stacked on `work/mirror-battle` at the user's call (it extends #215's `BEAST_KIND` instead of duplicating it), so it cites #215 and 8f.238 repeatedly and **has to** - the entry is unreadable without saying what #215 did and did not do - and both claims are still held, because a claim only sweeps itself once it is on MAIN. **The guard refused an honest commit for quoting its own parent.** ⛑ **THE TEST IS THE MERGE SKIP'S OWN TEST, ASKED OF ONE NUMBER INSTEAD OF THE WHOLE COMMIT**: is this number already in the committed record on this branch? Then an ancestor spent it and this commit is only referring to it. `Get-ShippedNumbers` took a `$Ref` parameter defaulting to `main`, so the branch record is read by the **same parser** as main's - this file's own stated rule, after the CSS-hex trap taught it that one fact with two readers means only one of them gets the guard. ⛔ **THE CLAIM IS NOT DROPPED, WHICH IS THE HALF THAT MATTERS**: the number is still not on main, so another session must still be refused it; the filter reaches one `verify` and nothing else. Fails CLOSED - any trouble reading `HEAD` leaves every held number standing. **Proved by making it do both**: the real diff passes and names the two numbers it is citing rather than passing silently, and a seeded line spending a live claim from a desk this branch does NOT carry still exits 1 with the COLLISION. ⛑ **AND THE GUARD THEN CAUGHT THIS PARAGRAPH.** The first draft of the sentence above NAMED that desk's two numbers, which made them added lines spending a foreign claim, and the commit was refused a second time - correctly. A number is cheap to describe and expensive to print: **an entry that has to talk about somebody else's live claim says whose it is, never which.** ⚠ A separate pre-existing fault was found beside it and deliberately left: **`verify` with no argument throws** where `verify staged` does not, because the unstaged path runs `git diff HEAD` and PowerShell 5.1 turns git's `LF will be replaced by CRLF` warning about `art/build_j_pack.py` into a terminating `NativeCommandError`. The hook uses `staged`, so nothing is blocked; the by-hand verb is the one that bites. | ✅ |
| 8f.241 | **#218 - THE CARD UNFOLDS FROM A LIT SEAM, AND A DOOR'S EDGE SAYS WHAT KIND OF ACT IT IS.** *(User, 2026-08-21, two notes: “i also want when event unfolds - add some animation to the screen (like unfolding) - similar as choices where in frostpank” and “improwe a bit design of butons on the choices sreen (i like icons and placment) - maybe some borders more thematic and coreleated to choice”.)* **(1) THE UNFOLD.** Frostpunk's decision panel does not fade in, it OPENS. The painting parts from a lit hairline at the middle (`clip-path`, so the two `.evback` rules that make a cover-cropped letterbox survivable are untouched), the frame opens with it, the left column arrives behind them in reading order, and the doors cascade one after another once the last beat is reached. ⚠ **470ms END TO END, AND THE NUMBER IS READ OFF THE ANIMATION TIMELINE RATHER THAN A SAMPLING LOOP** - the first sampling pass said 725 and was measuring its own latency. The first beat of prose begins arriving at 170 and the column is still at 450; `openEvent`'s own synchronous work (the DOM, two bust canvases, `placeDlg`) is 27ms of that and is not animation. ⛔ **THE FIRST CUT WAS 600ms BECAUSE THE TWO LONGEST MEMBERS ARE A DELAY PLUS A DURATION AND NOBODY HAD ADDED THEM UP**, which matters because this is paid on every card in the game - the ceiling is what a player sits through thirty-four times in a run, never what looks best once. ⛔ **NOTHING IS A CLASS ON `#wDlg`, WHICH IS THE RULE THE STAGE WAS BUILT ON.** Twelve functions open that box and a one-shot class the other eleven must remember to clear is exactly #209's drift. Two things re-arm it by themselves: `.evstage` is a brand new element on every open (`evStageOpen` writes `dlg.innerHTML=` from scratch), and `#wDlg` goes `display:none` -> `flex` through `.on`. ⚠ **THE BOX'S GATE IS `.on:has(.evstage)` AND NOT THE `:has` ALONE, AND THAT IS A FACT ABOUT HOW A CARD CLOSES**: every close in the file is `classList.remove('on')` and nothing else, so the HTML is left standing and `#wDlg` goes on matching `:has(.evstage)` for the rest of the session - an animation hung on the `:has` would have fired once, on the first road card of the run, and never again. ⛑ **AND `backwards`, NEVER `both`, WHICH IS THE ONE THING THAT NEARLY SHIPPED SILENTLY.** A `forwards` fill keeps the animation's final `transform:none` in force after it ends, and an animated transform outranks a declared one - so `both` on the doors killed `.choice:hover{transform:translateX(4px)}`, the single piece of feedback a door has, with nothing in the console and nothing in the stylesheet looking wrong. Caught by writing a transform INLINE and reading it back after the animation had finished (`matrix(1,0,0,1,4,0)` = the declaration still wins), then confirmed with the real pointer. ⚠ **AND EVERY RESTING STATE IS THE NATURAL ONE, WHICH IS A SAFETY RULE AND NOT A STYLE**: `.claude/rules/ui-scales.md` §5 says the preview pane composites no frames, so a build where these never advance has to come out merely STILL, never one where the doors are invisible and the card cannot be answered. The seam's own base opacity is 0, the frame's base inset is its final one, and `prefers-reduced-motion` drops the lot. **(2) THE DOOR'S EDGE.** ⛔ **THE OUTLINE IS STATE AND THE RAIL IS INTENT, AND MERGING THEM WOULD BE #102's WRONG-UNIT BUG.** The four-sided border is a readout and has been since #137 (`--e2` rest, `#8c3a31` warning, `#5a7a6a` race, `--g3` hover) and `.claude/rules/ui-scales.md` §2 forbids flattening an accent edge into the scale, so none of it is touched. What was empty is the 2px LEFT RAIL: every door in the game wore the same `--e3` whatever it did. It is `iv-<key>` off the door's own `ico` now - #137's derived-never-authored on its sixth surface - so a door written next month gets its rail untaught and not one card gains a field. Every hex is one the build already paints with, which is the user's own #209 note answered a second time: fight `--blood-lit`, evil `--blood`, help `--grain`, take `--wood`, trade `--morale` (⛔ **and not `--gold`, which is STATE** - a gold rail on the 24 trade doors would read as *these are chosen*), rest `--gem`, honor `--ore`, and **leave nothing at all**: it is 28 of the 158 doors in the two decks and it is the null option, so a rail on it would tint a third of every card to say *nothing happens here*. The 19 bare CAMPS rulings fall to the same default, untold, for the same reason they carry no glyph. ⛔ **THE FIRST CUT READ `ico.slice(-1)` AND ONE DOOR BROKE IT INSIDE AN HOUR.** THE WARM SPRING's second door is marked `['take','leave']`, which is two VERBS rather than a verb and a moral colour, so “last wins” handed the null rail to a door that takes something. A door wears EVERY intent as a class now and the stylesheet's ROW ORDER answers it - five verbs, then `help` and `evil` - which is `.claude/rules/event-cards.md`'s *the verb comes first and the moral colour second* written as a cascade rather than as a `.slice()`, and it deletes the second table this was going to grow. ⚠ **A PSEUDO AND NOT `border-left-color`, FOR SPECIFICITY**: hover sets the `border-color` SHORTHAND, which writes all four sides, so a rail painted as a border is erased by the pointer at the exact moment the player is reading it. **Gates, in the running build on the desk:** 14 of 14 `ico` combinations in `EVENTS`+`CAMPS` resolve to the intended rail, and ⛑ **the cascade was proved by making it FIRE** - appending an `.iv-fight` row after the block flipped `fight,evil` from blood to blood-lit and removing it flipped it home, because a check that has only ever agreed with you is indistinguishable from one that is broken · the overflow probe and the 10px-floor probe are **identical to a `git show HEAD:` baseline driven to the same card in a second headless browser** (one entry, `#wMap`, on the documented known-and-pre-existing list) · `LINT()` **0** · `spacingViolations` 0, `labelViolations` 0, `MAP_SIGHT` orphans 0 · the three scale greps match HEAD exactly · the real pointer on a door: 4px slide, outline to `--g3`, rail 3->4px and glowing in its own intent colour · no em dash · every road door still wears an intent, `mystery` still 1 · **8 of the 15 evil-railed doors are not already red**, which is where the rail is new information rather than a second copy of the warning. ⚠ A strip built to photograph the eight rails came back UNSTYLED on the first try because it was hung off `document.body` - not a bug, the scoping working: every rule is `#wDlg .evchoices .choice`. | ✅ |
| 8f.242 | **#219 - THE OPENING'S TWO CARDS ARE ON THE EVENT STAGE, AND THE STAGE STOPPED ASSUMING WHICH BOX IT IS IN.** *(User, 2026-08-21: "make intro (before fight) and after picking name in the same event style (after you kind of get people and money)", and on the art: "Add just curent images, i will replace them now with gpt".)* #209 made the painted 1180x620 stage canon for the road, the fire and the vignette, and wrote the law under it: **"a new deck adds a fifth caller, never a second renderer."** The opening was the deck nobody had counted - THE THREE BELLS and THE MAN IN THE CORNER, prose and doors both, still drawn by `proCard` into the 800px column the rest of the game left behind in one afternoon. `prStage()` is that fifth caller and it is nine lines, and **not one word of either card changed** - which is #209's own result (*"99 cards changed form and none of them changed a word"*) arriving on the last deck. ⛔ **THE STAGE WAS HARD-WIRED TO `#wDlg` IN THREE PLACES AND NOTHING COULD SEE IT UNTIL A SECOND HOST EXISTED.** `evFlow`, `evFront` and the beat pips all read `$('wDlg')` by name, and `#wDlg` is a child of `#wMap` - which does not exist yet when these two cards are read. Which box the stage is in is STATE now (`EVSTAGE.dlg`, through `evHost()`), and `placeDlg` is opted out of (`place:false`), because it centres inside `#wMap` and reads `$('wDlg')` itself: an unguarded call would have moved the WORLD's card instead of its own. ⛑ **AND THE CSS IS `:is(#wDlg,#prDlg)` RATHER THAN A SHARED CLASS, WHICH IS ARITHMETIC AND NOT TASTE.** `:is()` takes the specificity of its most specific argument, so all **30** stage selectors keep exactly the weight they had; a class would have lost to `#wDlg .bd{overflow-y:auto}` and handed the stage its own scrollbar - the fault #206 deleted from this very card. ⚑ **THREE THINGS THE FORM GAVE THESE CARDS FOR NOTHING, ALL DERIVED.** The contract card's two quoted paragraphs come out as HIM and its two narrated ones as the room, because `evBeats` has always read the first character of a paragraph. The opening's fight door is red without being told, because `evDoors` reads `danger` the same way it reads it on a road card - it had been the one door in the game styled by hand. And the opening fans **one** cast plate where the contract fans **two**, *a table for one* against *It appears you are a company now*, because `cast:` is the one thing each caller hands in. ⛑ **THE CONTRACT CARD IS THE FIRST CARD IN THE GAME WHOSE FLOW DOES NOT FIT, AND IT WAS MEASURED RATHER THAN EYEBALLED: 698 into 518**, against **439** for THE DOOR SHRINE, which is the heaviest card the road has. It carries a receipt strip AND a 7x7 naming form on top of its prose, and no road card carries either. Three cuts, each right on its own merits: the picker goes to **one list per row** (four rows to two, 115px to 53, tracking off, and no word clipped - which is #202's own trade on `.role`, made for the same reason), the strip's "THE ROOM SETTLES" heading and its divider are **hidden inside the stage** (a road card's receipt is chips and nothing else, #143, and a third small-caps heading under a title and a mark is the furniture this file keeps deleting), and what is left - **99px** - **fades at the top edge instead of slicing a line in half**, off a `.cut` class set by `evFlow` and by the box's own `onscroll` so it is gone the moment the player scrolls back. ⚠ **The threshold is 8px and not 0**: the opening card measures 521 into 518, three pixels of sub-pixel rounding across six beats, and a fade over a card that is effectively full reads as a smudge on its first line. ⚑ **AND THE HOOK WAS USED THE SAME HOUR.** The cards shipped cropping the old 460x190 prologue plates, by his own call (*"Add just curent images, i will replace them now with gpt"*), and `prStage` asks `jStageFor` FIRST precisely so that the replacement is not a code change. It was not: GPT delivered **`EV-J64_three-bells-prologue-stage.png`** and **`EV-J65_contract-after-brawl-stage.png`**, both exactly 1672x941, and landing them was two file copies, `python art\build_j_pack.py` + `art\inject_j_pack.ps1`, and **two rows in `JSTAGE`** (`prologue:'EVJ64'`, `contract:'EVJ65'`). `.evstage.fit` dropped the crop treatment by itself, which is what that class is derived for. ⛔ **AND THESE TWO PAINTINGS SHOW THE COMPANY, WHICH IS AN EXCEPTION AND NOT A DRIFT.** The standing composition rule keeps the party in the hero cards on the left and out of the painted right side; the user explicitly approved breaking it here, because a tavern confrontation with nobody in it and a contract scene with nobody to hand the purse to are not scenes. It binds these two keys and nothing else. ⚠ `EV00P` and `EV00E` are NOT deleted: they are the fallback for a build where the J pack has not been injected. Verified in the running build and in both generated outputs - 63 `JSTAGE` keys, 0 orphans, `jStageFor('prologue')` and `jStageFor('contract')` both return JPEG data, the root and player builds both compile, and the player marker is in `play/index.html` alone. ⚠ `proCard` now has no live caller and is kept for the same reason `contractSettle` and `prologueEnd` are - the parked joke door (#134) and the parked floor door (#138) both land there - but a restored door lands on the STAGE, as a `doors:[]` row and not a fourth argument. **Gates, in the running build:** `LINT()` **0** · both cards driven end to end by real clicks - the painting steps beats 0-4 and stops dead, the fight door reaches the battle screen, a name button does NOT step a beat, and the contract door pays 80, writes `G.coName` and lands on the map · six road cards re-driven and unchanged (all still hosted in `#wDlg`, all still 518/518, none `cut`) · clipped-box probe **empty on the prologue screen** and `#wMap` alone on the world, which is the documented pre-existing one · nothing under the 10px floor · zero font-size literals and zero hardcoded structural edges · map counters 0/0/0 · no `JSTAGE` orphan · every road door still wears an intent, none wears three, `mystery` still 1. | ✅ DONE |
| 8f.243 | **#220 - THE WORLD BAR COUNTS BODIES, THE MOOD IS A FACE, AND THE MENU MOVES TO THE MAP'S TOP RIGHT.** *(User, 2026-08-21, three notes on the global map: "Add 1 more thing: amount of units. You can add just human class icon and number. For party size instead of protrait", "at mood show only icon on global map - not text. Text and full info show only when expanded", "Move a menu to the top right".)* **(1) THE COUNT HE ASKED FOR HAD NEVER BEEN ON THIS BAR, WHICH IS THE FIND.** `partyUsed()` is SEATS and seats are size-weighted - ratkin 1, human 2, ogre 3 - so a company of four read **8/13** and nothing on any screen said four. The party chip is the race mark, the headcount in bold, and the seats in the dim `.nm` label the PAYDAY chip already uses. ⚠ **AND THE WORD GOES BETWEEN THE TWO FIGURES, WHICH IS A MEASUREMENT AND NOT A PREFERENCE**: driven on the shipped save the first cut read `4` `8/13 SEATS` with the two numbers 6px apart, i.e. **48/13** to anybody not already reading closely. `SEATS 8/13` is the PAYDAY chip's own order, a `.nm` label then its figure, and it costs the chip nothing (127px either way). ⚠ **`rIcon('human')` AND NOT `rMark('human')`**: the mark writes `data-race`, which `bindLoreTips` opens the RACE lore box on, and this picture does not mean "there is a human in the company", it means "a body". ⛔ **THE PORTRAIT IS OFF, AND `--barChip`'s ARITHMETIC WENT WITH IT.** #197 derived 30 from the 24px painted bust plus its border and the chip's own padding; nothing on the bar is taller than a race mark now, so 30 is a **declared** height from here on and the comment says so rather than going on quoting a bust that is not there. ⚑ **AND THE ONE MARK STANDING ALONE IS BIGGER THAN THE OTHERS**: #211 measured 16px as the biggest `.rart` that costs nothing IN A LINE OF TEXT, and a `.cchip` is not a line of text - it is a flex row with `min-height` already pinned at `--barChip`, so a mark is free until 30 - 2 - 2 = 26. `.cchip .rart` is 22 and the chip measures 30 before and after. **(2) THE MOOD CHIP IS THE FACE, AND THE EXPANDED READING ALREADY EXISTED.** `WTIPS.mood.t` has been `ms.ic+' '+ms.n` since #106 built it, so deleting the word cost nothing: the hover still opens **😐 AT EASE**, the colour is still on the border where it always was, and the chip went 96px to 41. ⚠ **THE FACE MAY NOT STAY AT LABEL SIZE.** `.bcchip` sets `--fs1`, which was right while the face was a bullet in front of STEADY and is the item-icon finding again the moment the word goes - a picture standing alone at 10px is a smudge. `.mface` is `--fs5` with `line-height:1`, which keeps the emoji's line box off the chip: 21 against `--barChip`'s 30, so the strip does not move. **(3) ⛔ THE FIRST PLACEMENT OF THE MENU WENT STRAIGHT THROUGH THE WORD CLOSE.** `top:52` is the first clear row under the 42px bar by arithmetic, and it is `#wZoom`'s row - ROAD / NEAR / CLOSE, x 1118..1270, y 52..75 - which sits nowhere near either rule in the stylesheet and which nothing named. **Found by a probe over every visible box in the map's top-right corner**, run because the capture showed two words in one place. ⚠ 82 is the row and it is arithmetic: `#wZoom` ends at 75, the button measures 24, and the tester's stack starts at `#winBtn`'s 134. ⚠ **SCOPED `body:has(#world.on)`** the way `#audioBtn` carries its own two overrides, and for the same reason: `MENU_ON` draws this button on five screens and the battle's top-right corner is already the audio button's at `top:74`. Verified live - on the battle screen it is still bottom left at (100, 686). **Gates, in the running build, driven on the world screen:** `LINT()` **0** · nothing under the 10px floor · the clipped-box probe `#wMap` alone, which is the documented pre-existing one · `spacingViolations` **0**, `labelViolations` **0**, `MAP_SIGHT` orphans **0**. | ✅ |
| 8f.244 | **#221 - THE PAYDAY AND THE CARD STOP STANDING ON EACH OTHER, AND THE PAYDAY SAYS WHAT IT PAID.** *(User, 2026-08-21, with a screenshot of the payday line lying across the middle of THE BROKEN MEN: "on the event screen payday often blocks the event - maybe we should do first paday, and then event. Or oposite" and "And in payday window - shorter tex - more amount of crons (bigger size) and when next one".)* ⛔ **THE LAYER LADDER ALREADY SAID THIS AND THE CODE DID NOT.** #112 wrote *content outranks chrome; only a modal outranks content*, and `#wNews` has sat at **40** against the card's 30 ever since - so the one line in the game that is deliberately drawn in the MIDDLE was drawn in the middle OF THE CARD. ⚑ **AND IT IS NOT A Z-INDEX FIX**: since #209 a card is a 1180x620 painted stage on a 1280x720 screen, so there is nowhere for a 520px box to stand. The two have to take turns, which is exactly what the user proposed. **(1) BOTH HALVES OF THE TURN, BECAUSE THE COLLISION HAS TWO SHAPES.** A loud line raised while a card holds the map is HELD in `G.newsQ` and played by `worldTick` when the map comes back - that is the `f.days` path, a door that costs a day and ticks a payday while its own outcome is on screen, and it is the commoner of the two. And a card about to open WAITS for a line already playing (`newsHold`), which is the arrival the screenshot caught. ⚠ **The quiet strip at the foot of the map is untouched**: it is narration, it is meant to be caught out of the corner of the eye, and it has never been in the way of anything. ⛔ **THE GUARD IS `G.moving`, AND NOTHING NEW WAS INVENTED FOR IT.** The tail of `travel`'s `arrive` is a closure now and `G.moving=false` is the first line INSIDE it, so the map stays locked for the length of the line - `G.moving` is what `travel`, `openProvisions`, the three camp doors and every node's onclick already ask. Without it the player could walk off during the 3.4s gap, and `firstTime` is false by then, so the place would never have spoken again. The map draws exactly as it does mid-walk minus the destination, because `G.dest` is cleared at the top of `arrive`. **(2) THE PAYDAY IS A RECEIPT.** `say2` takes an optional figure, and a loud line that has one renders `◉36` at `--fs8` in the display face over a `--fs1` clause. Paid: *◉36 / Every hand at the fire, paid. Next payday in four days.* Unpaid: the same shape in `--blood-lit` with what is still owed. ⚑ **The word "Payday" came off the front of both** - the chip says PAYDAY, the coin sound says it, and a gold figure in the middle of the screen says it louder than either. ⚠ **TWO TEXT NODES, NOT ONE STRING**, so neither half is ever parsed as markup: `say2` has written `textContent` since it was found missing in a refactor, and that property is kept. ⛔ **AND THE DURATION IS READ OUT OF THE STYLESHEET, NEVER DECLARED TWICE.** Turning the card into something that WAITS turns 6.5s from a fade into a pause the player sits through, so a receipt runs **3.4s** (`#wNews.big.rcpt`) and only the loud lines that are PROSE - somebody walking out of the company - keep 6.5. `say2` reads `getComputedStyle(el).animationDuration` after the class is on and the reflow has happened; a second copy of that number in JS would be the two-receipts bug wearing a stopwatch. ⚡ **AND THE HEADLESS DRIVER LIED ABOUT A HANG FOR TWENTY MINUTES.** `openEvent` stopped answering over CDP and every fresh connection wedged with it, including `1+1` - which reads exactly like an infinite loop in new code. `Debugger.pause` on a second socket said otherwise: the interrupt landed in the battle watchdog's ordinary `setInterval`, i.e. the page was **idle**. The same one-connection script then passed identically on this build and on `git show HEAD:`. ⚑ **The lesson is the instrument, not the bug: a probe harness that opens a socket per call and never closes one will eventually stop answering, and an unresponsive driver is indistinguishable from a hung page until you interrupt the page and read where it actually is.** **Gates, driven on the world screen:** `LINT()` **0** · nothing under the 10px floor · the clipped-box probe `#wMap` alone, the documented pre-existing one · map counters **0/0/0**. | ✅ |
| 8f.245 | **#222 - `claim.ps1 verify` THREW INSTEAD OF ANSWERING, AND THE TIDINESS WAS THE BUG.** *(Found while landing the desks of 2026-08-21: the by-hand check refused to run.)* `tools\claim.ps1 verify` threw a `NativeCommandError` while `verify staged` answered normally, which is the shape of a fault that hides. ⛔ **UNDER `$ErrorActionPreference='Stop'`, REDIRECTING A NATIVE COMMAND'S STDERR IS ITSELF WHAT PROMOTES A WARNING INTO A THROW.** Unredirected stderr is inert; the `2>$null` that reads as housekeeping is the whole fault. `Get-StoreRoot` has carried that note since #144 and `PARALLEL_SESSIONS.md` records the trap, and this one call site got neither. ⚠ **THE TRIGGER IS ORDINARY AND WILL RECUR**: `git diff HEAD` refreshes against the WORKING COPY, so a single tracked file sitting with LF endings is enough for git to warn that LF will be replaced by CRLF, and the redirection turned that sentence into a terminating error. ⛑ **WHERE IT HID, WHICH IS THE PART WORTH KEEPING.** A `--cached` diff reads the INDEX and never touches the working copy, so `verify staged` never emitted the warning - and the pre-commit hook calls exactly that. So no commit was ever refused and nothing looked broken, while the BY-HAND check was unusable on any checkout with one such file. **The by-hand check is what a session runs BEFORE it writes a number into the docs**, which is the exact moment this file exists to defend: a guard that works only where nobody is looking is #144's lesson arriving on the other verb. ⛔ **AND IT MAY NOT FAIL QUIETLY, WHICH IS THE OTHER HALF.** The diff is the entire evidence this backstop has, so dropping the redirection alone would have turned a throw into a silent *"clear: nothing added"* on any git failure. `$LASTEXITCODE` is read the way `Get-StoreRoot` already reads it and a failed diff refuses loudly; neither `git diff` form passes `--exit-code` or `--quiet`, so non-zero means failure and never *"there were differences"*. ⛑ **PROVED BY MAKING THE FAULT HAPPEN ON PURPOSE**, which is this project's own rule for a new check: `art\build_j_pack.py` rewritten with LF endings, `verify` throws at `claim.ps1:678` on HEAD and answers clear with exit 0 on this commit, `verify staged` clear either way, the file restored byte-identical afterwards. `claim.ps1` stays pure ASCII and CRLF and parses clean. ⚠ The row you are reading is late: this entry landed as code on 2026-08-21 and wrote no changelog, shipped or backlog row at all, and was found by a completeness sweep over the three files rather than by anybody missing it. | ✅ |
| 8f.246 | **#223 - NINE SMALL ASKS, AND FOUR OF THEM WERE ONE SENTENCE THAT NOBODY HAD MEASURED.** *(User, 2026-08-21, a batch: "Scroll that doesnt work" · "Instead of seats 'expansion symbol'" · "hover on 'circle' from the event - doesnt have hover" · "Delete additional wording from bag ... and similar from 'pet'" · "sometimes hover of arts covered by left screen" · "swap places of menu and music" · "the map itself is a bit blurry, i feel original was cleaner (and less light)" · "When i hover on 'payday' - show exact ammount of coins how much i pay to each person" · "When i writing text of negative feedback - add the option 'send'".)* ⛔ **THE CARD'S SCROLL DID NOT WORK BECAUSE #219 WROTE `pointer-events:none` ON THE BOX THAT SCROLLS.** The beat-advance listens on `.evstage`, so a click on the prose reaches it BY BUBBLING and never needed the declaration - what it bought instead was a scroller nothing could touch: the wheel's hit target resolved to `.evstage`, which is not a scroll container and is not an ancestor of the flow, so a notch scrolled **nothing**, and a `pointer-events:none` box has no scrollbar to grab either. ⛑ **Proved by making it fail**: with the flow overflowing, the nearest scrollable ancestor of the hit target is `wFlow` on this build and `NOTHING` the moment the old value is forced back. ⚠ **And #206's scrollbar styling had never followed the scroll.** #206 dressed every scroller in the game after macOS was found hiding its overlay bar, then #209 moved the card's scroll from `.bd` to `.evflow` and left the rule behind, so the one stage that overflows drew the platform's raw default - which is the white bar in his screenshot. Same 6px, same `--e3` thumb, and the computed `scrollbar-color` now matches `#iChar` and `#iRoster` exactly. ⛔ **THE ◎ CIRCLE CHIP HAD A HOVER ALL ALONG AND 100% OF IT WAS CLIPPED**, which is why it was reported as not having one. Its popover is 117px tall hanging above a host inside `#iLedger`, a **24px** row with `overflow` set: **121px cut off the top**, i.e. not one pixel of it had ever been on screen since #136 built it. That is #156's shape - *a feature can be BUILT and INVISIBLE, and it gets reported as missing* - and no reading of the handler could have found it; only asking the box where it was. ⛑ **The "hover covered by the left screen" is the same defect on the other axis**, and the sheet was carrying four at once: ARMING SWORD **61px** off the left, BAG and OFF HAND **21px** each, all cut by `#iChar`, the middle column that #200 left as a scroller. The three hosts (`.iact`, `.slot`, `.wchip`) are `position:fixed` now with one placer, `popPlace`, that centres over the host, flips below when there is no room above and clamps to the stage. ⚠ **`.lowpop` is deleted with the bug**: it was this same fault patched for one host, on one axis, with a hand-written 70px threshold, and it could never have saved a chip whose clipper is 24px tall. ⚠ **The containing block is `#stage` and that is measured, not assumed** - `#stage` carries a transform and an IDENTITY matrix still establishes one, so a pop written to `left:0;top:0` lands at viewport `0,40`, and every number `popPlace` computes is stage-local and divided by `fit()`'s scale. ⛑ **And `drawInv` now hides `#gtTip` first**: `bindTip`'s header has warned since #105 that a tooltip whose element is destroyed never fires `mouseleave`, and every equip on that screen destroys the row under the cursor. ⛔ **THE MAP: A FLAT WASH DARKENS AND FLATTENS, AND FLAT IS WHAT READS AS BLURRY.** Both halves of *"blurry ... and less light"* are one measurement. Luminance of the painted ground on a 4px grid, as p10 / median / p90 with the spread between the ends: raw painting 77/125/176 **spread 99** · the shipped `wash .45` 46/73/101 **spread 55** · the `.58` the old note computed and nobody took 37/58/79 **spread 42** · `brightness(.58) contrast(1.2) saturate(.9)` 27/61/96 **spread 69**. So the wash was costing **44% of the picture's local contrast**, and darkening it further - which is what "less light" asks for on its face - costs more. The filter is 16% darker at the median than what shipped AND a quarter more contrast. ⚠ **The canvas was also being enlarged 1.22 before anybody saw it**: a 1280x638 backing store rendering at **1562x827** (`#wMap` is 1280x678 and `#wCam` zooms 1.22 at NEAR, the default stop). It is 2560x1276 now with `setTransform` keeping every coordinate in design space, so the roads, the region names and the plates are drawn into real pixels. ⚠ **What this does NOT fix, plainly**: the three sources in `art/world-map-backgrounds/` are 1280x638, exactly the design box, so the terrain itself is as sharp as a 1280-wide file gets and only new art moves that number. ⛑ **THE PAYDAY HOVER NAMES EVERY WAGE**, derived off `wageOf` - the one function that answers this - with `dailyUpkeep()` as the total under it, so a row can never drift from the bill on the chip. ⚠ It surfaced two figures no screen had ever shown: the Captain's single crown, and the personality `wage` multiplier under it. ⛔ **SEND COMES BACK ON ONE BRANCH, AND IT IS A REVERSAL OF #191**, which deleted it at this user's own order (*"delete options 'send' and 'not now' they are not needed"*). #191's argument still holds - the face IS the submission and the reason is written as it is typed - and it is not the point: **the engineering was right and the silence was the bug**. Nothing was being lost and nothing was SAYING so, and from the outside a thing you cannot confirm and a thing that did not happen are the same thing. ⚠ What #191 deleted stays deleted: the button lives inside `#rxWhy`, which only 👎 opens, it files nothing (the row is already written), and it acknowledges for 520ms before closing. ⛑ **AND SWAPPING ☰ MENU INTO THE BAR BROKE THE BAR, WHICH ONLY THE SCREEN SAID.** MENU is **71px** against ♪ ON's 46, and `#wBar` reserved 64 - so `#wRes`, which ends exactly at 1280-64, was overrun by **17px** and the gem count printed under the M. 86 leaves the strip ending at 1194 with 5px of air. ⚠ The reservation's own comment said it was for *"the run of bar the centred day plaque swings through"* and `#audioBtn`'s said it was for ♪; the plaque measures x 566..713, nowhere near that edge, so the second comment was the true one. ⛑ The seat count wears **⤢** instead of the word SEATS, picked by RENDERING the candidates at the real 10px rather than by naming one (↔ and ⇔ go to thin ticks, ⧉ reads as "copy", ⤡ points the wrong way, ⛶ means expand the VIEW), and the bag and the empty pet slot stop printing a promise the `.pop` already keeps on demand. **Gate: `LINT()` 0 · no em dash · every road door has an intent · one `?` door · `spacingViolations` 0, `labelViolations` 0, `MAP_SIGHT` orphans 0 · nothing under the 10px floor on world, sheet or card · overflow probe reports only `#wMap`, which is on the known-and-pre-existing list · the three scale greps clean (0 font-size literals, paddings `1px 2px 86px`, 0 hardcoded structural border hexes).** | ✅ |
| 8f.247 | **#224 - THE SEVEN-ASK BATCH: THE PRICE MOVES TO THE CURSOR, THE EARLY GROUND OPENS UP, AND THREE THINGS ARRIVE THAT THE FILE ALREADY HAD THE PARTS FOR.** *(User, 2026-08-21, seven numbered asks in one message.)* **(1) THE PARTING-SWING FIGURE RIDES THE STEP.** #213 put the chance on the body that would take the swing and #215 gated it on the pointer; this finishes that thought - the price goes where the decision is being made. `render()` collects `B.zrisk` and `paintHand()` builds ONE chip on the lit hex under the boots, `⚔ 41% · 22%`. ⛑ **THE HEX AND NOT THE MOUSE, WHICH IS THE ONE PLACE IT DEPARTS FROM THE LITERAL ASK**: `paintHand` already folds the pointer through the MIRROR, so a chip pinned to the pointer's own pixels would price a hex nothing is going to happen on. ⚠ **THREE FIGURES THEN `+N`**, measured in design px against a 37px hex - two is 71 wide, six was **211**, i.e. five and a half hexes of red plate over the ground being aimed at - and every body is named in the hover, capped or not. The swingers keep the red sprite glow and lose the number: two readouts of one price is the receipt rule arriving on the board. ⛑ **AND THE WHOLE 'CHEAPEST COLLISION' TABLE #213 HAD TO SETTLE FOR IS HISTORY** - a lit hex has nobody standing on it, so there is no sprite, no `.hodds`, no `.hdmg` and no arc ring to lose to. **(2) THE SECOND AND THIRD FIGHTS ARE FOUGHT ON OPEN, QUIET GROUND.** ⛑ **NAMED FIGHTS AND NEVER A COUNTER**: asked which two, the user said *"is chase and blood on the road"*, and a rule phrased against a fight's PROGRESS through the run breaks silently the day somebody reorders the map (#71). `chase` goes `pool:['open']`, `blaze:0`, `trees:26 → 7`, `marsh:.99`; `clash` goes `pool:['open']` and `marsh:.90 → .97`. ⚠ **7 TREES AND NOT 0, BECAUSE OF #147**: the card is *the alder cut*, an alder cut IS a coppiced clearing, and a board with no trunks would be the Broken Men's fault in reverse. **Measured on the running build:** clash **19 blocked hexes → 0** and marsh 17.3 → 6.3, chase **40.3 → 13** and marsh 5.3 → 2 with the fire patch gone; the untouched forest (`brigand`) reads 45.6 as the control. **n=16 a side says neither fight moves**: clash 4.6rd/0.75 down → 4.3rd/1.0 down, chase 4.7rd/0.25 → 3.9rd/0.06, 16/16 won on all four arms. **(3) THE WEDDING DOOR IS FREE UNTIL YOU SHUT IT.** ⛑ **EVERY OTHER `NOWITHDRAW` ROW IS A FACT ABOUT THE PLACE AND THIS ONE IS A FACT ABOUT WHAT YOU HAVE DONE SINCE YOU GOT THERE** - the wedding is the only fight in the game the company STARTS, so it is the only one where *can I still walk away* has an answer that changes. A row may be a FUNCTION now, which costs one line and no callers: the control reads `noFleeWhy` every render, so the button turns to NO WAY OUT on the same frame the first body goes down. ⚠ **DEAD AND NOT DOWNED** - a brawl won without killing anybody is still one you may leave. #159's **22** mood is deleted rather than reduced, because the company that paid it can no longer reach this row at all. ⚠ **AND THE RECEIPT HAD TO LEARN TO JOIN ITS PARTS**, exactly as `choiceNote` did in #150: built by concatenation it read *"no spoils · −0 mood"*. **(4) THE SECOND SHAPE.** A trinket that casts an illusion of its bearer, drawn identically, +25 dodge, one hit and it is gone, two of the CASTER's turns if nobody touches it, and it never moves. ⛔ **IT IS NOT IN `B.order`, WHICH IS THE DESIGN AND NOT A SHORTCUT**: a body in the turn order takes a rail slot and a beat of the clock to do nothing, so the clock it is measured against is the caster's, ticked in `beginTurn` where *"two turns after the cast"* is actually a sentence. ⛑ **IT EXERTS A ZONE OF CONTROL ON THEM AND NOT ON YOU, FOR FREE AND UNARRANGED**: `engaged` is adjacency so an enemy beside the shape is in a scrum and its bow pays for it, while `partingRisk` filters `acts.some(a=>a.reach)` and the shape has none - so it can never swing and the red ring never appears under anybody it stands beside. The lie is told to the enemy and never to the board. ⛔ **AND `checkEnd` HAD TO LEARN IT IS NOT A SURVIVOR**, or a company whose last body went down with its own illusion standing would leave the fight running with nobody in it and no turn that could ever be taken. `strike` pops it as the FIRST thing after the hit is confirmed, before `hitLift`, the BACKSTAB float, the damage and the death path - an illusion must never enter the part of that function that is about bodies. `unitFrom` reads `act` off all three slots, which is #141's rule (`dodge` wired to the off hand, silently ignored on a trinket) applied before it can bite. **(5) THE KILL TALLY IS A RACE MARK AND A FIGURE.** ⛔ **THE HEADS WERE A FIFTH RACE VOCABULARY AND NOBODY HAD COUNTED THEM**: `KILLHEAD` drew a ratkin as ⬩ and an ogre as ⬢ on the one screen that also prints the painted race mark two inches above it, on the same body - #196's own finding, on the surface neither #196 nor #211 reached. The three real races take their glyph from `RACE_ICON` and beasts get 🐾, authored here because `RACE_ICON` is *the three faces of the island* and a paw is none of them. ⚠ **READ AT CALL TIME AND NEVER INTO THE TABLE**: `RACE_ICON` is declared ~1,500 lines below, so `ic:RACE_ICON.human` is a const read from its own temporal dead zone, which throws at boot and takes the script with it. The repeated-glyph form is gone (four ratkin read ⬩⬩⬩⬩, a thing you count rather than read) and the tally left the top of the sheet, where #136 point 10 had already argued it did not belong - the chip came back afterwards and that argument was never re-answered. **(6) THE RUN REMEMBERS WHAT KIND OF DOORS IT TOOK.** `G.deeds`, one integer per `CHOICE_ICO` intent, derived from the doors' own `ico` the way #160's `sins` is - so a ninth intent, and a ninth deck, join the tally untaught. `markDeed` is the one door both pickers call and `markSin` keeps its own job (it carries a PHRASE and three cards quote it). Read out on BOTH endings, between the roster and the tally: the route says where you went, the crew says who with, the tally says what you have left, and none of the three has ever said what you DID. ⚠ A two-glyph door counts on both columns - the row is *how often did this company do X*, not a partition of its doors. **(7) THE CROSSBOW.** dmg 24-34, am .90, range 4, **two hands**, shoves a man-sized body a hex, no penalty whatever in a scrum, and one action on the crank after every shot. ⛑ **EVERY ONE OF THE FOUR CLAUSES WAS ALREADY A MECHANISM**, which is why it is a row and not a system: `push` is the halberd's primitive, `pointBlank` is one clause skipped in `hitBreakdown`, and `reload` is a lock `spend` sets and `canUse` reads - all three at doors both AI brains already go through. **Measured: 31% adjacent against the hunting bow's 5% on the same hex.** ⛔ **`hands:2` IS THE HALF NOBODY ASKED FOR AND THE HALF THAT MAKES IT A DIFFERENT WEAPON** - the bow is deliberately one-handed so the archer's second hand is a decision, and the crossbow ANSWERS that question in the weapon and pays with the quiver and the shield. ⛔ **THE WINDING IS A CARD AND NOT A TWO-ACTION SHOT**: a two-action shot spends the turn, shoot-then-wind lets you shoot, walk, and wind next turn, and the player decides which. ⚠ **`maxSize:1`, AND THE FIRST CUT WROTE 2 WHICH IS A NO-OP** - `sizeOf` is big→3/ogre→2/else 1 and the force block already refuses `d.big` on its own line, so 2 refuses nothing and would have shipped as a field that looks like a rule. ⛔ **AND WITHOUT A BRANCH IN BOTH BRAINS IT IS A TRAP**: the shot greys itself and nothing knew how to un-grey it, so a company under AUTO would fire once and carry a stick for the rest of the fight - #128's spear wall exactly, and #99 before it. ⚠ The AUTO branch **returns** rather than falling through: measured, it wound and then spent the second action WALKING with an enemy four hexes away. **Gates:** `LINT()` **0** · CSS greps **0 / 1px 2px 64px / 0** · no em dash in `EVENTS` · 94 doors with an intent, **1** mystery · map **0/0/0** · floor **0** and the clipped-box probe returning only the documented pre-existing list on the menu, the battle, the company sheet and the epilogue · the eight-fight regression clean, no guard hits · save round-trip for `deeds` including a save written before it existed. Picture `shots/224_board.html`. | ✅ |
| 8f.248 | **#225 - THE INJURY LADDER: A FALL COSTS MORE EVERY TIME, AND THE THIRD ONE IS THE LAST.** *(User, 2026-08-21: "Давай кардинально пересмотрим систему травм ... Добавляем темпрорари инджури. Это когда юнит первый раз упал ... Если персонаж с травмой падает - то он получает перманентный шрам. Если персонаж со шрамом и травмой падает, это пермадез персонажа. (кроме капитана, он может получить 2 шрама)".)* This is **backlog #34**, and it is the user's shape rather than the one the spec parked. #34 said SCARRED -> MAIMED -> DEAD and could not ship for six months because MAIMED is #4's body parts and #4 is unbuilt. The middle rung here is a **temporary injury with a clock on it**, so the chain is **sound -> INJURED (a week or two) -> SCARRED (forever) -> gone**, every rung is a thing the player already understands, and nothing new had to be invented to carry it. ⛔ **#34's OWN CONDITION IS KEPT WHOLE: death is the end of a visible chain, never a roll.** The only random thing is WHICH injury lands; who dies is decided entirely by who the player chose to walk onto a field with. ⚑ **AND THE INJURY HEALS, WHICH IS THE WHOLE SECOND CHANCE** - a scarred body whose injury has run out is back on the bottom rung, so the top of the ladder is only ever reached by taking somebody hurt into a fight. ⚑ **THE DAY BAND WAS MEASURED AND THE MEASUREMENT MOVED THE ASK.** He guessed 5-20 days and asked for 2-4 battles; walking all eight roads of `allRoads('hold',QUEST_GOAL)` with the EDGES day costs gives **22-26 days a run, 3-6 map battles, a median FIVE days between them**, plus the card fights (18 of the 40 road cards carry a battle door) - so a fight lands about every three days and 2-4 battles is **7 to 14 days**. ⚠ **20 would have been 80% of the road** and still catch a median of only two further map fights, because there are so few of them: a rule read off the fight count alone would have looked reasonable and shipped an injury that never mends. ⛔ **ONE APPLIER, `takeFall`, AND BOTH DOORS CALL IT** - the won fight and `carryEverybodyOut` (the withdrawal and the beating) were the two places that turned a downed body into a scar, and this file has caught two-copies-of-one-fact four times (`choiceNote` #150, `raceDoorPick` #159, the receipt #176, `applyHurt` #197). ⛑ **`nextRung(p)` IS THE ONE ANSWER FOUR READERS ASK**: the fall, the warning before the fight, the badge under the feet and the sheet. Proved by stepping a throwaway body up the ladder and checking the prediction against the result on every rung - member **hurt -> scar -> dead**, Captain **hurt -> scar -> scar -> dead** (his `scarCap` is 2, the user's own exception), and a scarred body whose clock ran out predicting `hurt` again. **NINE INJURIES**, each one penalty and a little blood, priced against the two statuses the board already had (DRUNK -12 to hit / -8 dodge, CRIPPLED -10 dodge): -2 on a stat, -8 dodge, -12 to hit, -15% damage, or **one action a turn** (the DEAD ARM, which is the ask's *"не может бить второй раз за ход"*). ⚠ **`despActs` FLOORED AT 2 AND WOULD HAVE HANDED THAT SWING BACK**: free while nothing in the game had an `actionsMax` of 1, and every existing case is unchanged by the `Math.min`. The board shows it as a **✚ under the feet** in `.stbar`, amber for a scar coming and red on the last rung - two STATUS entries and one state, the `spearwall`/`wallbroke` shape, because `col` is static in that register and the colour IS the warning. The **warning before the fight** is #34's own condition (*a warning before deployment, not a confirmation dialog; it says who, and it says it once*) and it is `tutPaint`, the one teacher #182 left standing, rationed **once per body per rung** through `p.warned`. ⛔ **THE FIRST CUT OF THAT CARD LIED**: it joined every fresh name and told a company that *"Vesna, Marrow fight hurt AND ALREADY SCARRED"* over a Vesna with no scar on her - the game stating a fact about somebody that is not true of them, on the one card whose whole job is to be believed. Two groups, two sentences, one card. **Caught by driving a MIXED pair**, which is the case nobody sets up by hand. ⛔ **AND `fst(proseName(p))` IS NOT A SHORT NAME**: the Captain has no first name - his prose name IS his nickname - so a run that rolled him *"the Reasonable"* printed **"the is hurt, not scarred"** on the card that teaches the whole system, and it only shows on some runs, which is why it survived three tests (*Weatherhead* and *Ditchwater* are one word and read perfectly). `shortWho` is the one reader now. ⚑ **THE DEAD CARD WAS DESIGNED IN #187 AND HAS BEEN WAITING ON `p.deadBattle`, WHICH NOTHING SET**; this sets it, and the crew grid appends the fallen after the living so the one body the screen most has to talk about is not deleted from it by the splice that removed them from the roster. **The Captain's own last rung ends the run** through `toDefeat(why)` - he is `id:'you'` and `iSel`, `capPerson`, `capTick`, the world bar's face and the whole tutorial voice are written around him being there, so a company that outlives him is a half-built state rather than a harder game. ⚑ **AND `lostSoul()` GAINS THE FOURTH STATE #85 LEFT ROOM FOR**: THE DREAM has never had a corpse to be about, `fell` outranks `cut`/`hole`/`sent`, and its one sentence of evidence is the only one of the four about evidence that is still THERE. **Measured after:** `LINT()` 0, no em dash, 0 doors without an intent, 1 `?` door, the map's three counters 0/0/0, nothing under the 10px floor and nothing clipped on the sheet or the aftermath. ⚠ **The aftermath's 13-body overflow is PRE-EXISTING and slightly better now** - measured against a `git show HEAD:` baseline in a second tab, same seeded company, same fight: baseline 17px, this build 13px - and the injury strip was shortened to `hurt · <name> · 8d` to get there, because the long form wrapped to three lines against the scar strip's two. ⏳ **Open, and deliberately:** the road's `armourSac` puts a body down before a fight starts and now goes up the ladder like any other fall, which can spend somebody the player did not know they were spending; and AUTO does not yet refuse to walk a last-rung body into a losing exchange (#34 asks for both). | ✅ DONE |

Three rules, one per clause of the ask: the **slot is rolled first** off `FIND_SLOT` (main .45 · armour .30 · off .12 · trinket .13), **nothing already held** worn or in the cart, and a main has to be an **advance** - harder than the weakest weapon somebody swings, or it brings `range`/`reach`/`spellRange`/`push` that no weapon in the company has. ⚡ **THE ADVANCE TEST WAS MEASURED AND FOUND TOOTHLESS ON ITS FIRST CUT**: the working stave hits for 6-10 because hitting is not what it is for, so "the weakest weapon carried" put the bar at a mean of **8** and every roll passed. **THE CAMPAIGN WALK** marches the founders down the fight list paying XP through `payFightXP`, spending the banks by the #216 bench's own policy, mending between fights, with the joins editable (Asha before the Broken Men per #212, Skree + Bruht + the six's armour step before the Snare) - so each row is the fight at the points the road would actually deliver, which is the "important" half of the ask. **The three strategies are `AIP.you` profiles and nothing else**, per AIP's own contract ("all 1 = the real AI, all 0 = the pre-rework rush"): AGGRESSIVE zeroes flank/kite/fallback/cohere/hold/doct and keeps `ev` (straight in, aim kept), STEADY is the brain at `cohere:3, flank:.4, pile:1.3`, and the enemy always plays FULL. ⚑ **VERIFIED AGAINST THE HOUSE INSTRUMENT before anything else was believed**: founders on the Sling-Line read 10/10 in the tool and `ARENA.match('four','slingline',8)` says 8/8 - same loop, same answer. **AND THE FIRST HOUR OF ITS EXISTENCE PAID FOR IT THREE TIMES:** **(a) the #216 band multipliers mislead in both directions and are now measurable on demand** - the Broken Men banded HARD measure 70-90% and the Sling-Line banded HARD measures 100%, while the PAST HARD tier is honest (0-30% everywhere it is claimed); **(b) the strategies split per fight**, which is what makes them worth having: STEADY takes the palisade defense 100% against AGGRESSIVE's 60%, AGGRESSIVE takes the Broken Men 90% against the brain's 70% and clears Blood on the Road in 4 rounds with nobody down; **(c) ⚠ a stronger-on-paper company can measure WORSE**: L1 founders win the Sling-Line 10/10, founders + Asha 3/10, and L3 founders 5/10 against L2's 10/10 - isolated to the fifth body and to the third level separately, reproduced on two independent seed families, and cross-checked against ARENA so it is the game and not the tool. ⚠ **Mirror-AI's own caveat stands over that finding** (#89: a mirror loss can be a hard human win) - it is the first question FOR the balancing pass, not a retune performed here. **Perf:** ~70-370ms a fight warm; the default grid (12 fights x 3 strategies x 10 seeds = 360 fights) fills the table row by row in ~3-4 minutes, STOP works mid-run, and COPY exports the table as markdown. ⏳ **Open, and deliberately:** the in-game AUTO / auto-resolve strategy picker off these same three profiles - one caller when wanted, since the profiles are AIP rows - waits on the prototype being free and on a mockup, per the standing gate. | ✅ |
| 8f.253 | **#230 - ALL FOUR STATS ARE ILLUSTRATED, NINE GRADES EACH, AND THE SIZE WAS PAID FOR OUT OF THE SHEET'S OWN AIR.** *(User, 2026-08-21: "I got icons for stats. Can you please add them to the build ... 9 things for each stat", then "size 32px - lets try to make it like that".)* **36 paintings**, `prototype/assets/stat-icons-final-32px/`, embedded verbatim at their native 32x32 off that folder's own handoff table: HANDS on str, BOOTS on agi, BRAINS on int, HEARTS on mor. #204 had left **two of four illustrated at 16px and agi and mor drawing nothing**, which that entry recorded as a shipping state to look at. ⛔ **THE ROWS ARE IN TELLS ORDER, STRONGEST FIRST, AND THE PACK SHIPS THE OTHER WAY ROUND** - `TELLS[k].s` is descending because `tell()` reads it with `.find(v>=threshold)` - so the generator reverses and every row carries its grade as a comment. **Verified byte-for-byte: all 36 rows match the file on disk they claim to be AND the word they stand beside**, which is the one fact here that must never drift. ⛑ **One `<img>` builder now (`statIcoAt`), and the second one had already drifted**: the hover ladder wrote its own tag with its own hardcoded 16 and went on drawing nothing for agi and mor after those sets would have existed. **WHAT 32px COST.** The tell row is driven by its picture at that size, so it went **17.4 → 33**, and four of them want **+53.4** in a column that measures 678 into 678. ⚠ **The Captain is not the worst case here, though #200 made him the worst case for width**: a crew member carries a 25px dismiss foot against his 2, so the slack is **30px, not 53**. Paid in `--p` steps off that block's own gaps and edges - `#iChar` gap 6→4 (14) · `#iChar` padding 9→6 (6) · `.itl` padding-bottom 2→0 (8) · `#iStats` padding 6→4 (4) - **32 raised against a 23.4 bill**, each with its arithmetic written on the rule it changed. ⛔ **AND THE FAULT IT WAS DEFENDING AGAINST IS INVISIBLE TO EVERY COUNTER THIS REPO HAS.** `#iBody` was `flex:0 1 auto;min-height:0` while `#iDollBox` inside it is a fixed 196, so the column SHRANK it and, because `#iBody` is `overflow:visible`, the doll **painted over the SKILLS label instead of clipping** - and ui-scales.md §5's clip counter skips `overflow:visible` by design, so it read **0** throughout. **HEAD does this already**: a body wearing THE CIRCLE with a promotion point banked overlaps by **12.6px**, silently, today. `#iBody` is `flex:0 0 auto` now, so the column runs out of room honestly and scrolls. Measured on a crew member, this build against HEAD: clean **fits / fits** · a point banked **fits / scrolls 8** · THE CIRCLE **fits / scrolls 21** · both **OVERLAPS 12.6 / scrolls 38**. ⚠ **That is a real cost against #133's "one screen, no scroll", taken knowingly**: a few pixels of scroll on the states that do not fit is a smaller lie than a painted figure lying across a caption. ⛑ **The hover ladder is where the art actually pays**, nine rungs at 1:1 with the current one lit, and its name column was widened to `auto` **scoped to `.tells`** - the nerve rung's 74px is load-bearing for its own second column, and at 74 every stat rung wrapped, which is what made the tip 454px against the 16px one's 383. ⚠ **Three of the four families were upscaled to 32 from a 24px master**, so their pixel grid is already uneven and drawing one into 16 would be a 2:1 halving on top of a 4:3 stretch; the winged boot's trail is 1px wide. **Never resample these.** ⚠ **The negative margin is -1 and may not go to -2**: the exporter's `Clear-OuterPixel` leaves EXACTLY one transparent pixel and the art reaches it on HANDS and BOOTS (measured with PIL, not assumed). ⛑ **AND THE BRAINS WERE RE-EXPORTED THE SAME DAY, ON HIS READING RATHER THAN ON A COUNTER** (*"5 and 6 very bad readible"*, *"brains - make them more full height"*). He named grades 5 and 6 and the measurement agreed exactly: BRAINS content heights ran **14 16 18 17 / 14 15 / 24 23 24** where HANDS ran 18..26 and BOOTS 21..30, so the two bare brains were an **18x14 painting in a 32px box beside a 30x30 boot** - and the dip was an anomaly rather than a size ladder, because grades 1-4 are WEAKER and BIGGER. ⛔ **They also carried a detached speck**, 4 orphan pixels on grade-0 and 10 on grade-plus-1, orange, sitting where the gold ring's stem hangs on grades 7-9: what was left when the ring came off. It is why a bounding box could not size these at all - grade-0 measures **26px tall as a box and 14px as a brain**. `export_brains_fullheight_32.py` rebuilds the family from the 24px masters in ONE resample (the shipped 32s are already a 4:3 stretch), drops any component under a fifth of the main one, and normalises to a common content HEIGHT of 26 capped by a 30px width - **a height and not a box, because filling 30x30 with every grade would have made the small bare brain the biggest picture in the family, above the crowned ones.** ⛑ **An integer factor where one fits, the fractional fit only where none does**: 2x turns every source pixel into an exact square and 2.14 turns some into 2 and some into 3, which on a 14px-wide painting is visible at 32 - compared at 14x before it was chosen, and worth 2px of height. Only the two bare brains can take one (everything else is 20-21px wide at the master, so 2x would be 40 in a 30px box). Result **15 18 21 20 / 22 22 / 26 24 26**, and the ladder still reads flat lump → bare brain → crowned by size as well as by colour. **Gates:** `LINT()` **0** · map **0/0/0** · nothing under the 10px floor · clip counter **0** · the three closed-vocabulary greps identical to HEAD · no em dash in the 177 added lines · every tip fits the stage at the true 1280x720 (the "32px past the bottom" reading was a letterboxed 800px driver, which is #197's pin-the-camera lesson wearing a different hat). | ✅ DONE |
| 8f.254 | **#231 - THE BOARD ANSWERS WHEN IT IS ASKED, THE PRICE OF LEAVING STANDS UNDER THE BODY THAT PAYS IT, AND THE STAMP MOVES TO THE FOOT OF THE SCREEN.** *(User, 2026-08-21, six asks in three messages: "opportunity atack percent show under the caharcer - only one number and just swords and % red" · "On enemy % to hit them show only when i manually pick an ability, or hower on one (then show others who are in area of my reach)" · "damage and chance to hit 20% smaller" · "Flag of mood a bit smaller and a bit righter, then now (so its not colapsing that much with character icon)" · "add to the buttom right corner of the game curent build.
