The bar is 22. ⚡ **AND THE SHEET'S BARS SHIPPED SQUASHED FOR ONE ITERATION** on `.ibars .bar{height:7px}` beating `.sbar>.sbb` at the same specificity and a later source position. **A tie is decided by which entry wrote which block, which is not a design.** | ✅ DONE |
| 8f.272 | **#248 - THE MAINTENANCE PASS: THE HARNESS STALL IS NAMED AND CLOSED, THE THREE SCALES ARE CLOSED AGAIN, AND THE DOCS STOP SAYING WORDS THE GAME NO LONGER USES.** *(User, 2026-08-25: "check last 10 iterations. If everething done / complete ... If you see smaller bugs - fix them. Then update recent docs, concept itself - to match actual butch ... Archve / delete / close stuff not needed, so we don`t spend time each session it".)* **⛔ THE STALL WAS THE ONLY THING IN THE LAST TEN ENTRIES RECORDED AS UNRESOLVED, AND IT IS A HARNESS DEFECT WEARING A GAME BUG'S CLOTHES.** #236 saw `1rd DRAW/STALL HIT GUARD` once in ~32 runs; #239 spent about **155 clash runs** on it across six different loops, disproved its own first theory, and wrote the row as UNRESOLVED with two named suspects and the note that *"`runFight` already returns `guard`, so the instrument exists"*. It did not, and that is why nobody could close it: `HIT GUARD` says the loop gave up and nothing says what it gave up ON. ⛑ **THE REPRODUCTION IS A SEQUENCE, NOT A SEED.** A fresh page then `regress.js` is clean; a fresh page then `gates.js` then `smoke.js` then `regress.js` stalled **4 of 4**. Both #236 and #239 recorded that shape in passing (*"the first `regress.js` after a run of `gt.py eval` probes on the same page"*) and read it as noise. It is the whole cause. **⛑ WHAT IT ACTUALLY WAS, TRAPPED RATHER THAN ARGUED**: a `defineProperty` setter on `B.tutLock`, re-installed after every `startBattle`, caught the write with a stack - **`capSay` -> `capTick` -> `nextTurn`**. So a spotlight card goes up mid-fight, `nextTurn` takes its `if(B.tutLock){B.tutHold=true;return;}` door, and the loop spins to 3000. **Nothing is wrong with the game**: that hold is #156's deliberate *"a spotlight stops the clock"*, a player clicks the card and `tutNext` resumes the turn through `later()` - which a synchronous loop can never let fire. ⛔ **AND THE FLAG THAT WAS SUPPOSED TO PREVENT IT EXISTED, WAS DOCUMENTED, AND WAS NEVER SET.** `tutFlush` opens `if(window.TUT_SILENT)return;` with the comment *"the harness switches this on"* - and `tools/harness.js` never did. Six ad-hoc probes from #247 each set it by hand, having rediscovered the need one at a time. **This is #202's finding exactly** (*"a check a document says exists is a claim about the build"*), one layer down: a CONTRACT a comment says is honoured, and no caller honouring it. **⛑ THE GATE MOVED DOWN ONTO `tutPaint`, WHICH IS THE ONE DOOR.** Three sites read the flag (`tutFlush`, `hurtWarn`, `hurtTeach`) and all three are the tutorial's; `capSay` reaches the same dim sheet through `capCard`, which is a **fourth path no flag covered**. `tutPaint` is the only function that raises the sheet and the only one that writes `B.tutLock`, so one gate on it is every caller - and `false` is already the documented word for *"this card did not happen"*, which every caller honours (`capSay` returns without spending its line, `tutShow` steps to the next in the queue). The three earlier checks stay: they refuse cheaper, and `hurtTeach` also guards a `G.` one-shot that must not be spent. **⛔ AND A SECOND, SEPARATE CROSS-WIRING CAME OUT WITH IT: `tutNext` HANDED A WORLD-TOUR STEP TO A BATTLE.** Its own comment says *"the battle's queue wins when a fight is up, the world tour's is drained everywhere else"* and the line under it contradicted the sentence: `B.tut` exists on the BRAWL and on nothing else, so on any other fight the first clause is false and the fall-through drained `WTUT.q` - a card about the top bar stopping the clock of a fight it has nothing to do with. ⚠ **It is not a live soft lock**: `worldTut` fires once a run behind `G.wTutDone` and the dim sheet swallows every click on the map underneath it, so a player drains the three steps before a road can be walked. Fixed anyway, and refusing the queue strands nothing - the fall-through still takes the sheet down and the steps wait for the next `tutNext` outside a fight. **A tour that waits is a tour; a fight that waits is a soft lock.** **⛑ AND THE GUARD REPORTS ITSELF NOW**, which is the half that outlives this entry: `runFight` returns a `stuck` object (round, idx, the current body, `tutLock`/`tutHold`/`busy`, `checkEnd`, both side counts, the lesson queue) read inside the `try`, because the `finally` nulls `B` and B is the whole question. `regress` prints it. **Measured: 4 of 4 stalling before, 0 of 6 after**, on the identical four-command sequence, plus 40 consecutive `clash` runs on a prepared page against 1 in 20 before. Both halves of the tutorial re-driven and still working: the world tour raises its sheet and one click takes it down, and a battle lesson still locks the fight and still releases it. **⛔ THE THREE SCALES WERE OPEN AND `.claude/rules/ui-scales.md` §5's OWN GREP SAID SO.** The padding counter is documented to return `86px` alone and returned `0px 1px 3px 86px`. `.rmem .rlv` - the roster tile's level plate, shipped by #246 - carried `padding:1px 3px`, a literal on neither the scale nor §3's two-row geometry table, and `#bGround` wrote its apron fallbacks as `var(--aprT,0px)` where `0` is the same length and not a decision. The plate is `var(--p1) var(--p2)` now: measured on all four roster rows, the tile is **69px tall and the column 148 wide, both unchanged**, the plate 21.2x14 -> 23.2x16 with its bottom at 142 in a row that ends at 151. **The file's own three greps read 0 / `86px` / 0 again.** **⛔ THE DOCS SAID `nerve` AND `provisions` AND THE GAME HAS SAID `MOOD` AND `SUPPLIES` FOR DAYS.** #232 renamed the pool on 48 player-facing surfaces and #245 built `RES_NAME` so the word and the pattern come off one table - and `01_GAME_CONCEPT.md`, which the changelog's own header names as the file that holds *what is true now*, still priced five spells in **nerve** and ran a whole section called *Provisions are what the wounded eat*. 19 nerve + 12 provision sites in the concept, one in `README.md`'s live feature list, one in `03_WORLD_LORE.md`'s magic clause. ⚠ **`nerves` SURVIVES AND IS MEANT TO** (*"shoots worse up close - nerves"*): that is the feeling and not the pool, which is #232's own rule about the idiomatic use. ⚠ **AND THE RECORDS KEEP THEIR WORDS**: `CHANGELOG.md`, `SHIPPED.md`, `WHAT_TO_TEST.md` and the two benchmarks say what was true when they were written, which the changelog's header requires. **⛔ 878 EM DASHES IN THE DOCS, AGAINST A HARD RULE OF NONE - AND THE REASON THEY SURVIVED WAS AN ASSUMPTION.** The backlog parked them because #199 *"needed a lexer to tell a player-facing string from a code sample, and the docs have the same problem worse"*. ⛑ **THEY DO NOT.** That difficulty is a fact about one 3.4 MB HTML file; in markdown a fenced block opens and closes on ````` and an inline span on a backtick, so the whole lexer is four lines. `tools/dev/emdash.py` counts by WHERE the dash sits and rewrites prose only: **1,197 prose, 0 left, 24 inside fenced code untouched** (`.claude/rules/` reads clean for that reason - its one hit is the regex that TESTS for em dashes), 1,028 lines changed and 1,028 lines back, so nothing gained or lost a line. ⛔ **TWO KINDS OF FILE ARE EXCLUDED AND ONLY ONE IS ABOUT EFFORT.** `docs/playtests/` is a RECORD OF WHAT A HUMAN WAS SHOWN - three runs taken three days before #199 swept the game, so every dash in them is a faithful capture of text the game really printed. **A transcript that is corrected is not a transcript.** The frozen `archive/CHANGELOG_*` pair and three closed files are out because nothing appends to them. ⚠ `archive/BACKLOG_ENTRY_SPECS.md` is IN: it lives under `archive/` for size, not for status. **⛑ AND A CENSUS FOR THE SHAPE #245 AND #247 BOTH PAID FOR BY HAND.** Both entries found content that was BUILT and could not be REACHED, and both found it because a human said *"I have never seen X"*. `probes/reach.js` asks it of every table that maps a key to a thing: **art orphans 0 across all four tables**, **0 road cards without a wide master** (#214's road half is done) against **19 camps that still letterbox**, `MAP_SIGHT` unreachable 0 (LINT 8h holding since #245), 4 GEAR rows with no painting (`crossbow`, `shape`, `tmap`, `draught` - the record said two), and 7 `ITEM_ICON` keys with no GEAR row, all of them documented (5 wagon fittings + the two specced-not-built items). ⚠ **The seven `EVENTS` keys on no node are all opened by CODE** - `cache` is written onto a node, `holdmen`/`courier` by the hold schedule, `refused`/`joined` by the sin count, `armour` is an ambush and `wynn` comes out of the Snare - so that column is a list to read, never a gate. **Gates:** `LINT()` **0** · floor `[]` on both screens · clip the documented set · `iChar` overlap `[]` · map 0/0/0 · no em dash in `EVENTS` · smoke 0 throws over 40 road cards, 19 camps, 58 gear hovers and every body sheet · the eight arena fights clean · `gt.py check` 3 scripts 0 problems · `record.py check` 0 errors. | ✅ DONE |
| 8f.273 | **#248 ROUND TWO - FIVE RULINGS TAKEN, AND THE ONE THAT WAS ASKED AS A COMPLAINT TURNED OUT TO BE ARITHMETIC.** *(User, 2026-08-25, answering the gathered list: "Stone shield - do" · "b6 - good, close" · "b8 do it, great" · "b7 - if it answere, solved - close" · "c3 - find how to resolve it".)* **⛔ C3 IS THE ENTRY, AND THE COMPLAINT IT CAME FROM WAS NOT LITERALLY TRUE.** The row read *"all three candidates say the same thing"*; measured over 400 musters, two candidates share a whole four-sentence line **0.7%** of the time and all three share one **0%**. ⛑ **WHAT IS ACTUALLY THE SAME IS THE REGISTER, AND IT IS ARITHMETIC RATHER THAN WRITING.** Over **1,740 bodies** (500 musters plus 60 rolled companies) str/agi/int run **3 to 13, median 8, p95 11** - against thresholds authored at 17/15/13/11/9/7/5/3/0. So the median body landed on the **sixth of nine phrases**, the top four were unreachable (`Very strong` needs 13; the highest str in 1,740 bodies was 12), and the commonest reading on each axis was `Not strong` 38% · `Heavy-handed` 39% · `Plain` 40% · `Nervous` 31%. **Three candidates read as three flavours of disappointing and nothing on the wall could ever read as good.** ⚡ **AND #230's PAINTINGS WERE PAYING FOR IT, WHICH IS THE HALF NOBODY WOULD HAVE LOOKED FOR**: `statIco` is keyed on the same index, so **str showed 5 of its 9 grades and agi and int 6 of 9** - four paintings per family had never once been on screen. ⛑ **THE BANDS MOVE AND NOT ONE WORD OF THE VOCABULARY DOES**: 13/12/11/10/8/7/6/5/0, so the median takes the MIDDLE phrase, p85 takes `Strong` and p95 `Very strong`. After: `Strong enough` 39% · `Steady enough` 40% · `Sensible` 35%, grades seen **str 5→8, agi 6→9**. ⚠ **`mor` IS DELIBERATELY UNTOUCHED** - it runs 1 to 19 because relics, traits and the mood pool push it, and it already used **9 of 9**. **A stat whose ladder fits gets left alone.** ⚠ **AND THIS IS A READOUT CHANGE, NOT THE PARKED STAT REWORK**: #204 re-derives this ladder from scratch when it lands; until then what a stat is CALLED should at least describe the bodies the game rolls. **⛔ THE FINGERPRINT STONE SHIELD IS BUILT, AND IT NEEDED TWO ENGINE FIELDS NOBODY HAD.** Painted, embedded and mapped in `ITEM_ICON` since #192 with **no `GEAR` row to hang on** - one of the two the #248 census reports. It is EPIC, `hands:2`, in the **MAIN** slot (the off-hand slot cannot say *both hands*, and `handsFree()` already empties the off hand for a two-handed main), 8-13 damage, **+30 armour, +12 dodge, `push:1`**. ⛑ **`armourValue` NEVER SUMMED A MAIN-HAND PIECE AND `unitFrom` NEVER READ A MAIN-HAND `dodge`**, so both numbers would have been printed by the sheet and ignored by the board - the promise-versus-payment split this repo has caught six times. **The brief predicted the first in capitals** (`art/ITEM_PACK_01_TEXT.md` §3, *"armour off a main-hand piece"*); the second was found by driving it. ⚠ The armour multiplier stays on the WORN row only: **a suit of plate fits badly on the wrong frame and a rock does not care whose arms hold it.** ⚡ `push:1` needed nothing, because #224 had already moved the shove onto a `push:` field on the WEAPON for the crossbow - and its note line is derived now (`main.range` separates *the bolt puts them a hex back* from *every hit shoves them a hex back*), which is the second reader that field has ever had. ⛑ **AND IT IS REACHABLE, WHICH WOULD OTHERWISE HAVE BEEN THE NEXT CENSUS FINDING.** `unique:true` means no random find can offer it, so an unwired row is dead content - the exact defect #245 and #247 each paid for by hand. It rides **THE SITTING STONE's bread door**, because that card's first line has had an ogre sitting on a rock since it was written and **the rock is the shield**. ⚠ **No fifth door**: style rule 4 caps a card at four and this one is at four, and a race-gated host would have been worse than unreachable because `raceDoorPick` shows the RATKIN door to a company holding both. ⚠ **The naive word counter reads 181 against a 175 ceiling and the card is fine**: two of its doors are `needRace` and only one is ever shown, so a company reads **106 (all human) / 129 (ogre) / 158 (ratkin)**. That is the over-count `.claude/rules/event-cards.md` documents for THE HOLD'S MEN, arriving on a second card. **⛔ THE PARTY CAP IS 11, AND THE WAGON IS PRICED AGAINST THE CURVE THAT REPLACED ITS OWN.** #238's remainder named `PARTYBASE` as the weak joint rather than the wagon (*a seat upgrade is only candy when the cap BINDS*) and four founders at 8 of 13 almost never reached it: **13 → 11**, seat ladder **11 / 12 / 14 / 16**. And `FRAME_COST` goes **6/12/24 → 5/10/18**, because #239 halved salvage above 3 and a run now holds **23-24 at the last fire where it held 42** - so 24 for the third frame was most of everything a run earns. ⚡ **The card's own sentence had the ladder TYPED IN** (*"Seats go 13, 14, 16, 18"*) and is derived off `FRAME_SEATS` now, so the cap change moved it without anybody going to look for it - which is #137's rule catching a lie in the same edit that would have created one. **⛔ AND A SHOP ROLLS ITS SLOT THE WAY A FIND ROLLS ITS SLOT.** #239's remainder said a rare rack leans to trinkets *"because most rare rows are trinkets"*, which is exactly what `rollFind` was built to stop - it rolls the SLOT off `FIND_SLOT` first and only then picks inside it, while `rollRack` was picking uniformly out of a pool whose shape is an accident of how many rows somebody authored. **Measured over 300 racks: main 41% · armour 25% · trinket 12% · bag 12% · off 11%.** ⛑ Still a preference and never a rule, which is `slotOrder`'s own note: an empty slot falls through rather than costing the shop a row, and the `noMain` gate survives it by skipping that slot in the walk instead of picking it and being refused. **Gates:** `LINT()` **0** · floor `[]` on both screens · clip the documented set · overlap `[]` · map 0/0/0 · no em dash · smoke 0 throws · the eight fights clean · `gt.py check` clean · `record.py check` clean · the census now reports `stoneshield` gone from the no-GEAR list, `kris` alone. | ✅ DONE |
| 8f.274 | **#249 - THE FOURTEEN-ASK BATCH: A POOL IS A SOCKET WITH THE FIGURE STANDING IN IT, THE MENU IS IN ONE CORNER, AND THE COMPANY SHEET GIVES 88px BACK FOR THE PERKS.** *(User, 2026-08-25, seven asks on the battle screen and seven on the inventory.)* **⛔ THE MOVEMENT ZONES: THE ASK NAMED A DIRECTION AND THE MEASUREMENT SAID THE OPPOSITE ONE.** (*"make more clear 1st and 2nd zone of movment (maybe 1st a bit darker)"*.) The wash is a teal laid over a dark green field, so its alpha buys LUMINANCE and nothing else. Sampled off `#bGround`'s own canvas under a field hex and composited src-over (`probes/wash.js`, kept as the instrument): bare ground **55** · far half .17 **72** · near half .24 **80**. **Eight points, which is the complaint.** A darker near wash walks TOWARDS the far one - `rgba(46,112,104,.46)` reads **74**, two points apart - and one step further, at `.55`, it reads **59** and the near half is DIMMER than the far one, i.e. the ground you can reach this action looks like ground you cannot. ⛑ **So the gap is spent on the near half alone: `.24` -> `.34`, measured 91, a 19-point step.** The far half is untouched, because #247 raised it from .11 to .17 on the grounds that *"at .11 the far wash is a hue nobody reported seeing"* and that is a floor, not a preference. **⛔ THE STUCK UNIT IS #247's OWN TRADE, REPORTED FROM THE OTHER SIDE.** (*"Stuck on my unit, when it is out of movement (automatically end turn)"*.) That entry found the UNDO offer *"was real and it lasted 140ms"* and fixed it by refusing to auto-end while an undo stood, writing down that *"holding the hand-over costs one press of END TURN"*. **That press is the report.** ⛑ It is a HOLD now and not a refusal: `UNDO_HOLD` 1800 base, which `paced()` makes **3.15s** at the shipped ×1.75 board clock (2600 was the first cut and ran 4.55s, long enough to feel like the hang it deletes). Three guards, each a real case: `B.undoT` is bumped by every new hold AND by `undoMove`, so a stale timer cannot end a turn that has moved on; `cur().id` catches a turn ended by hand; and `u.actions>0` is the undo itself. **Driven both ways: the turn passes to `foe0` after the window, and pressing ⟲ inside it puts the body back on its hex with its action and leaves it his turn five seconds later.** ⚠ **The window is drawn**, because a button that vanishes on its own is a control the player watched disappear: `#bUndo::after` drains over `paced(UNDO_HOLD)` written onto the element, so the bar and the timer are one number at every speed, and the RESTING state is empty rather than full (ui-scales §5's rule about a build that composites no frames). **⛔ THE THREE POOLS ARE SOCKETS NOW, AND THE HUES ARE UNTOUCHED.** (*"make the bars more in style of the game - now they partly look like somethign alien"*, and on the sheet *"bars health, armor and morale more in the style"*.) What read as a web widget was a flat fill with no frame, no depth and a hard black edge, on a build that draws every other box as a dim-gold hairline round a dark recess. So: a `--e1` hairline and an inset recess, and a lit crown fading to a shaded foot on the fill. ⛔ **The veil is a `::after` on the `<i>` and it has to be**: `setBar` and `drawInv` write `background:` INLINE (the armour steel, `hpCol`'s ramp, the rung's own colour), and an inline shorthand kills `background-image` and outranks the stylesheet. **The mood fill IS the rung and the health fill IS the ramp; repainting either would be a readout printing something other than what it reads.** ⚠ **The frame is drawn on `.sbt` and not on the trough**, because an outline or an inset shadow on `.sbb` paints in `.sbb`'s own layer and the fill paints over it - so the first cut had a hairline round the EMPTY half of every bar and nowhere else. **⛔ AND #247's REASON FOR THE 22px BAR IS WRONG, WHICH ONLY A SWEEP SAID.** (*"more space between that bars. And make them a little bit less high"* and *"write the curent point of that in the midle of that bar"*.) That entry read the floor as *"two FACES on one baseline whose combined line box is taller than either"*. Measured at every size from 11 to 17px, with the `/max` at `line-height:0`, at `line-height:1`, and **deleted entirely: the three sweeps are identical.** The `/max` never voted. What sets the floor is the DISPLAY face's own content area, ~1.27em whatever `line-height` says, which Chrome counts as scrollable overflow - **so a bar's height is a function of ONE number**, and the ladder is `11->15 · 12->16 · 13->17 · 14->18 · 15->21 · 16->22 · 17->23`. ⚠ **And the gate is `> 1`, which is why 22/17 ever passed**: ui-scales §5's counter ignores a single pixel, and 22 under a 17px numeral is 1 over and always was. Shipped **20/15 on the card (also 1) and 16/12 on the sheet (0)**, with the gap 3 -> `--p3`. ⛑ **The pitch is unchanged at 25, which is the point**: the three rows sit in the band they were measured into and only the proportion of bar to air moves, so nothing under the plaque shifts. ⛑ **The figure stands on the bar's own midline**, by a `1fr auto 1fr` grid rather than by centring a flex row - two of the three rows carry a WORD as well, so centring the pair would put `45/45` dead centre and `LIGHT 6/6` off to one side. The mood row has no figure (its value IS the word), so `:has()` moves that word into the middle column: one rule, asking about the row it is on. **⛔ THE MOOD MARK STOPS BEING A COLOUR EMOJI, WHICH IS B1 ANSWERED.** (*"for happy diffrent glyph - more in the style of other 2"*.) ⛨ and ♥ are cream line marks in the card's own type and the third row was an orange disc the font paints itself: #102's wrong-unit rule with the GLYPHS as the units. `MOOD_MARK` is `U+263A` plus the **text variation selector**, which is load-bearing - without it some stacks swap one emoji for another. ⛑ **And it is ONE mark**, which is the rest of *"the style of the other 2"*: ⛨ does not say which armour class and ♥ does not say how many hitpoints, and the rung was being said three times on one row (the face, the word in the bar, the colour of the fill). `LADDER[].ic` is untouched and still draws the five faces on the ladder hover, the road bar and the event chips. **⛔ ☰ MENU IS IN ONE CORNER ON ALL FIVE SCREENS THAT CARRY IT.** (*"in the battle menu in upper right corner - so it is in one space everywhere"*.) It was in three places and each had a good reason: #130 put it bottom left, #220 moved the MAP's copy top right and scoped that to the world screen because *"the battle's top-right corner is already spoken for"*, #247 reparented the battle's copy into the log's footer. **Three correct decisions, three positions, and a player who has learnt where the door is has to learn it twice more.** ⛑ The top right is the DEFAULT now and the world override is deleted - one rule instead of three - and what the two scoped notes were protecting is paid where it belongs: `#bZoom` steps to `right:94` (arithmetic: the button is `right:10` and 71 wide, so 10+71+13 of air) and `#iBar` takes the same **86px** reservation `#wBar` has had since #223. ⚠ **Measured on all five screens `MENU_ON` lists and not on the two that were reported**: `camp` and `sim` were already clear, the map clears `wWagon` by 5px, and the button reads x1199..1270 y8..32 and is the top element at its own centre on every one. ⚠ `? RULES` stays in the battle log's footer, which is the half of #247's argument that still holds. **⛔ THE ROSTER GOES 148 -> 80, AND THE CLASS MARK WAS NEVER THE WIDTH.** (*"Even less space for the portraits. what if we put class also at the bettom right of the portrait there - so that thing could be even smaller?"*.) #247 measured this column against the worst body it can carry and found the wall between 120 and 140; what set it was the WEAR CLOCK sitting in a text column beside the portrait, and moving the class mark out of that column buys about eighteen pixels. ⛑ **So the tile stops being a row.** The portrait goes on top with all four facts on its own corners - class bottom-left, level bottom-right, the unspent mark top-right - and the clock goes UNDERNEATH at the tile's full width, wrapping between chips instead of pushing the tile wider. **Clean tile 69px (69 before), worst body 97, six of those 582 inside a 678 window.** The race hue moves onto the tile as an inset shadow rather than a border, so it cannot argue with `.rmem.on`'s gold selection edge. ⚡ **AND THE UNSPENT MARK STOPS BEING A ★, WHICH ANSWERS B10 RATHER THAN SHARPENING IT.** That question is *"two ★ meet on one roster row"*; they were on two different LINES, and putting both on the picture would have put them 40px apart on one 56px face. `▲` is not a new glyph: it is what `.itl.spend` has printed after a stat row since #122 and what #244's caption is written in. **⛔ THE SIX GEAR SLOTS ARE TWO ROWS OF THREE AND THE FIGURE IS PARKED.** (*"Articaftcs lets do in 2-3 raws - now it too much free space (but with perks we will need a lot of that space)"*.) #140 put the slots in two fixed columns with the drawn body between them and the wires carrying the meaning, and the middle 200px WAS the picture. Two rows of three cost **108px against 196**, and the 88 is what the perk panel gets built in. `drawDoll` is untouched and still hands back its anchors, kept the way `proCard` was kept in #219. ⚠ **What is lost is the mark on the PART** - scars, a mutation and a condition were drawn on the figure - and every one of them is still on this screen as a `#iLedger` chip with its own hover; what has gone is where on the body. Raised in `OPEN_QUESTIONS.md`. ⚡ **The grid paid for the type**: three slots across a 594px column are 184 each against the old 150, so the name and the receipt each take the next `--fs` step (*"I feel we need bigger font for art stats"*) with nothing wrapping that did not wrap before. ⚡ **And `· both hands` comes off the main hand** (*"on the main hand don`t write 'both hand' - it is obvious from secondart"*): the OFF HAND slot beside it already reads `TAKEN` over `Both hands are on the boar spear`, so the fact was on the screen twice and the copy that survives is the one standing where the cost is PAID. It is still in the item's own hover. **⛔ THE WAGON DOOR WEARS THE CART THE MAP DRAWS** (*"The wagon replace by icon of wagon (as on global map menu)"*), filled from `CART_SVG` by `drawInv` so the two bars cannot drift - and 103px of button became 34, **which is what let ☰ MENU take this bar's corner without anything else moving.** **⛔ AND THE TWO MARKS IN THE SHEET'S HEADER ARE ONE BOX** (*"on this page the class same size as race"*). The race mark has been a painted 16px `.rart` since #211 and the class mark is a GLYPH, whose ink is a fraction of its em box and whose LINE BOX comes from whatever font the system fell back to: at `--fs3` the star was 12px of type against a 16px picture, and at `--fs7` its box measured 26 against the picture's 22. **A fixed 26x26 square settles it whatever is inside**, which is the only form of *same size* that survives the next glyph. ⚠ The honest fix is a PAINTED class mark and it does not exist yet. **⛔ 12px OF AIR BETWEEN THE BLOCKS** (*"10-15 px air between blcoks"*), six gaps, 36px, paid for by the gear grid - **and a caption is not a block**: SKILLS gives the gap back with `calc(var(--p5) * -1 + var(--p2))`, the pair ui-scales §3 already blesses. **⛑ AND THE MEASURING FOUND A SHIPPED DEFECT THAT IS NOT THIS ENTRY'S.** #244 appended the *+1 TO A STAT · PICK A ROW* caption into `#iStatTop`, which is a flex ROW, as a third CHILD - so the moment a stat point is banked the caption takes its own max-content width out of the middle of the sheet. Measured on `git show HEAD:`: **the value box goes to 0px wide on all four tells**, scrollWidth 51/96/50/50. **Every stat reading on the sheet disappears at exactly the moment the player is invited to raise one.** The caption and the rows share a column now and all four read full. ⚡ **It was found because the clip counter was driven in the `+point` state, which `gates.js` does not reach: a gate that only ever runs one state is a gate over one state.** **Gates:** `LINT()` **0** · floor `[]` on both screens · clip `[]` on the sheet in BOTH states and the documented three in the battle · `iChar` overlap `[]` · the sheet's worst body **38px of slack against 10 on HEAD** · map 0/0/0 · no em dash in `EVENTS` · the three scale greps 0 / `86px` / 0 · smoke 0 throws over 40 road cards, 19 camps, 59 gear hovers and every body sheet · the eight arena fights clean · `gt.py check` 3 scripts 0 problems. | ✅ DONE |
| 8f.275 | **#250 - THE PIT, THE FRONT DOOR SHUTS ONE OF ITS OWN ROWS, AND A MOOD FLOAT STOPS COUNTING.** *(User, 2026-08-26, three asks.)* **⛔ THE PIT IS A PLACE WITH ITS OWN RULE, WHICH IS #61's TEST FOR EARNING A WORD.** (*"I want to add a pit to the surfaces ... if you shove somebody into it, 10 damage ... the enemy needs 1 turn to get out ... you cannot melee an enemy out of or into a pit ... you can shoot at them and cast at them, but they cannot cast back"*.) `pit` is a WALKABLE kind like `ember` and unlike the camp's `fire`, and its five rules are one idea: **THE RIM IS A FLOOR YOU ARE STANDING UNDER.** A body shoved, hooked, dragged or thrown over the edge takes **`PIT_FALL` 10, through any armour**; a body that WALKS in takes nothing, because it climbed down and that is a decision rather than an accident - **the fall is what the shove BUYS, and it is the whole of what makes the hole a tool.** `moveBudget` is **1** while you are in one and `pitClimb` spends whatever the turn had left the moment you step over the rim, which is *"1 turn to get out"* without taking the choice away: you may stay down there and shoot instead. **⛑ AND EVERY REFUSAL IS STATED ONCE, IN `mayAim`**, which is #148's rule and the reason none of this needed a line in either AI brain: `handsOn(a) && acrossRim(u,t)` refuses a swing, a spear, a kick and a hook in both directions, and `a.arcane && inPit(u)` refuses the working. **Shooting is untouched in both directions**, which is his third clause. **⛑ THE RIM IS A DIFFERENCE AND NOT A PLACE, AND THAT IS THE ONE DESIGN CALL IN THE ENTRY.** Two bodies down in the same hole are on one ground and fight normally. His sentence is about the pit against the FIELD and does not consider pit-to-pit; a rule that refused it would need explaining every time two people fell in together, and **pits are allowed to touch (his words), so it is not a corner case** - measured over 40 boards, **6 of the 10 two-pit boards put them adjacent.** **⚠ A PIT DOES NOT HOLD AN OGRE**, which is arithmetic as much as fiction: `sizeOf>=2` is exempt, which matches #46's *"an ogre does not get moved"* (no `push` act in the game can put one in anyway) and means a two-hex body can never be half in and half out - the case `inPit` would otherwise have to answer by reading a tail. **⛔ WHERE THE HOLES COME FROM IS DERIVED TWICE AND AUTHORED ONCE.** `TERRAIN.rocky` carries `pits:2, pitOdds:.65` and **no other ground does**, because the ask named *"a rocky ravine and a lot of stones"*; `pitDue()` is the *"from the middle of the map onward"* half and is **anchored to the SHAPE of the map and never to a node id or a day count** - `armourWhere`'s rule and #160's, which #71 proved by moving the Muster Field and nearly firing the Thing in Armour on top of the Fen-Mother. `stepsToGoal()` counts roads backwards from the bells, so **`hold` is 20 out and the back half is 10 or fewer**, i.e. the Clan Wedding onward. **⛑ The dial and the gate were picked against each other and nothing had to be excluded by hand**: the three fights fought on `rocky` sit at 8, 4 and wherever `armourWhere` lands, and every fight in the front half is fought on `fen`, `forest`, `camp` or `swamp`. `digPits` is `lightEmbers`'s shape with `lightEmbers`'s three guards (never in a deployment band, never on a reserved hex, **the odds roll taken even when it fails** so a ridge with no hole consumes exactly one number) and one deliberate difference: **two pits may touch, where two ember patches may not.** Measured over 40 boards: **16 with none, 14 with one, 10 with two.** **⛑ AND THE ONE THING ONLY DRIVING IT FOUND: A FOE IN A PIT WAS NOT LIT AND ITS HIT ODDS WERE PRINTED OVER ITS HEAD ANYWAY.** `81%` on a swing `clickHex` would have refused. The ring comes off `offerAtk` (which asks `mayAim`, so it was already correct) and the FIGURE comes off a hand-copied branch inside `render` that repeats `mayAim`'s size gate and its oak - **#148's four-copies warning arriving for the fifth time.** The two clauses are repeated there with the note saying so; the honest fix is for that branch to ask `mayAim`, and it is not one line, because `mayAim` refuses a BLOCKED lane outright while that site deliberately draws one as refused-red. **⛑ `1 HEXES`.** Reachable since crippled-in-the-marsh and never once seen, because nothing put a body on a budget of one often enough. A pit does, on every climb. **⚠ AND THE PAINT WAS A PUDDLE UNTIL IT WAS PHOTOGRAPHED.** The hex layer washes teal over the ground canvas, so a blue-black mouth composites to exactly the colour `marsh` and `deep` wear. Warm black, a lit crescent for the far wall rather than a hairline (an outline reads as a pond), and a brighter spoil ring, which is the only part of a hole that is ABOVE the surface. **⛔ THE CAPTAIN SAYS IT BEFORE THE GROUND DOES ANYTHING**, which is what the LEARN tier is for (#64's split) and what he asked for in the same breath: `learn_pit` rides `B.hasPits` in `learnTurn`, beside `learn_bloom`, and the badge is `STATUS.inpit` - **computed like `chilled` and `nearring`, because it is a fact about the ground and not about the body.** **⛔ THE PRACTICE FIELD IS SHUT UNTIL THE ROAD HAS BEEN WALKED.** (*"disable 'practive fight' before person tried 'the road' (icon locked)"*.) The front door offered a menu of thirteen fights beside the button that starts the game, to somebody who has not seen one of them, which is the 2026-08-10 report (*"too many options/systems from the start"*) on the very first screen. **⛑ DERIVED FROM THREE FACTS THE SAVE ALREADY CARRIES AND NEVER A FOURTH FLAG** (`LEGACY.brawlDone`, `LEGACY.runs`, `hasSavedRun()`): a new `roadTaken` would have read FALSE for every player who has already played, i.e. it would have locked the screen for the ten people whose feedback this build is written from. `TEST.on` opens it, because it is also the developer's bench. **LOCKED AND NOT HIDDEN** - a missing row teaches nothing and comes back one day as a surprise. ⚠ **A `disabled` button shows no `title`**, so the reason is ON the row: measured 346px of text in 438 of inner width, 0 overflow, click does nothing, and it comes back the moment any of the three flags does. **⛔ AND A MOOD FLOAT STOPS COUNTING.** (*"Moral boost - one medium triangle. Or one big. All (now there are 1, 2, 3 triangles, and some circle under them. Simplify). One symbol slightly different sizes - and the direction is clear."*) The arc and the repeated glyph were both a NUMBER WEARING A PICTURE: the player had to read three shapes to learn one figure. One triangle says the direction and its size says how much. The three thresholds are the ones `moodFace` always used (22 / 12 / rest); what changed is that they pick a size. **Measured 10 / 13 / 17px, boxes equal to the type (`line-height:1`), all under `FX_ROW`'s 18** - `--fs7` at 20 was the first pick and is arithmetically closed, because a box over 17 eats the next line's row. `--fs1` is the floor and 10 sits on it. ⚠ `fx()`'s fourth argument takes a class name as well as `true` now, and every existing caller is untouched. **Measured, n=20 a side on the Steading-Line** (the fight that gets the most holes), the two arms differing only in `pitDue`: **12.05 rounds against 11.20, won 6/20 against 5/20, 4.55 down against 4.50.** So the hazard costs the AI about four fifths of a round of walking round it and changes nothing about who wins - **which is the right first-cut result, because only the player currently AIMS a shove at one.** **Gates:** `LINT()` **0**, and 8i proved by making all four of its branches fire and returning to silence · floor `[]` on both screens · clip the documented set · `iChar` overlap `[]` · map 0/0/0 · no em dash in `EVENTS` · the three scale greps 0 / `86px` / 0 · smoke 0 throws over 40 road cards, 19 camps and 59 gear hovers · the eight arena fights clean · **and the three rocky fights run again WITH holes in them, n=8 each, both brains at FULL: 0 guards, 0 errors** (`regress.js` cannot reach this - it runs from `hold`, where `pitDue` is false by design) · `gt.py check` 3 scripts 0 problems. | ✅ DONE |
| 8f.276 | **#251 - THE SEVEN SMALL FIXES: THE CONTRACT STOPS RECEIPTING ITS OWN SENTENCE, THE SHEET STOPS BEING THE SMALL VERSION OF THE BATTLE CARD, THE DOLL BECOMES A BODY AGAIN, AND THE UNDO WINDOW STOPS BEING A WAIT.** *(User, 2026-08-26, three numbered items over two screenshots.)* **⛔ THE CONTRACT CARD'S TWO CHIPS ARE THE RECEIPT RULE CATCHING ITSELF.** (*"Delete heads and crowns"*, both struck through in red.) `.claude/rules/event-cards.md` opens with *the prose says what happened, the chips say what it cost, and they must never say the same thing* - and on every other card in the game that split is real, because the prose has had its figures cut out of it. **This card is the one place where the two halves are the SAME event, read a second apart**: the paragraph directly above the chips is *"He puts a purse on the wood"* and *"It appears you are a company now"*, and the chips under it said **+88 CROWNS** and **+3 HEADS**. That is the second receipt this file spends its length deleting, arriving from the one direction nobody had checked - not a number typed twice, but a number standing beside the sentence that already is it. ⛑ **Nothing is lost, which is the test**: the purse is `CONTRACT_PURSE` and the door pays it, and the world bar the very next screen opens on carries the crowns and the headcount as live readouts. ⚠ **The three faces stay** - `.tvrwho` is not a receipt, it is three people with names, which is the card's last sentence as a picture. The divider went with the chips because a rule between one thing and nothing is furniture. ⚡ **And it paid the card 56 of its 99 overflowed pixels back**: the receipt strip measured 82.6px and now measures 28.9, so the flow scrolls **43 instead of 99** and the top-edge fade `#219` built for it fires over that much less. **⛑ AND THE ONE DOOR GAINS THE SHOE BESIDE ITS SCALES.** (*"put shoe on the go"*.) The label is two acts in one sentence - you take the purse AND you walk out of the tap-room - and it wore only the first. `['trade','leave']` is two VERBS and is legal by precedent this repo has already paid for: THE WARM SPRING's second door is `['take','leave']`, and it is the door that broke #218's first cut of the rail cascade. ⚠ **The rail does not move and that is derived, not lucky**: `leave` is the one intent with NO rail row (the quietest door has no colour), so the coin-yellow the door had as a `trade` door is what it keeps - measured `rgb(201,162,39)` both ways. **⛔ THE SHEET'S HEADER MARK LINE GOES, AND EVERY FACT ON IT IS ON THE TILE 130px TO ITS LEFT.** (*"Delete that smaller line near human face"*.) #246 put the level on the roster portrait's bottom-right corner and #249 put the class mark on its bottom-left; the race is the `.race` word on the same row AND the face in the picture; and the arc round the bust is `xpFrame`, the same reading `xpRing` was drawing in the header. ⚠ **One fact did not survive the move on its own and was handed over rather than lost**: the EXACT xp figures were on that chip's `title` and the roster's arc had no hover at all, so `xpHover` rides the roster's own level plate now. ⚠ **AND IT COSTS THE COLUMN NOTHING, WHICH IS WORTH KNOWING BEFORE SOMEBODY COMES HERE FOR PIXELS**: `#iHead` is **90px on every body in the game** because `#iField`'s portrait tile is what sets it, and the text column beside it measured 71 to 86.6. The 26px row came out of slack. ⚠ `SHEET_HEAD_MARKS=true` puts it back, which is #240's switch written the same way for the same reason - and the `#iChips`/`.ichip` stylesheet block is left standing for it. **⛔ THE THREE POOLS TAKE THE BATTLE CARD'S OWN TWO NUMBERS, AND THE WORST BODY PAYS NOTHING.** (*"design better this bar with health, more like in the battle"*.) #247 gave the sheet 16/`--fs3` against the card's 20/`--fs5` on the argument that *"the card is read mid-fight at arm's length and the sheet is read at a desk"*. Reasonable, and overruled: two surfaces draw the same three pools off one `.sbar` block, and the one the player STUDIES was the one drawn smallest. ⛑ **It could be granted because `#iStatTop` is a flex ROW and its height is the taller of two columns.** Measured over all 16 body x state cases: the pools stack to **127** against the four tells' **124** on a clean crew member, so +12 costs 12 out of 107px of slack - and to **141** the moment a level is banked, which is the state that owns the worst slack in the game. There the tells are still taller at 141 against 139, so **the worst case pays 0**. Worst slack **38 -> 29**, and every one of the 16 cases still `scroll 0`, `overlap []`. ⚠ **20/`--fs5` is the card's pair and `ui-scales.md` §3c records it as *one pixel over*, which the clip counter ignores by design.** Copying the card means copying that: 21 would be clean and would make two surfaces disagree about a shared block over a pixel nobody can see. **⛔ THE DOLL IS A BODY AGAIN, WITHOUT THE BODY.** (*"Make artifacts shown as on real body (armor in the middle a bit up) - hands on the sides. Two spots for trinket and bag centered under"*.) #249 parked the drawn figure and the six slots became a READING ORDER - *worn, then wielded, then carried* - which is a correct thing for a list to be and is not what a doll is. Now: the chest in the middle and raised, a hand either side of it, and what is merely carried centred underneath. **The arrangement IS the figure.** ⛔ **Two flex rows and not a grid, and the PET is the reason**: the Captain has a sixth slot and nobody else does, so the bottom row is two boxes on fifteen sheets and three on his - a grid would need its placement re-authored per count, where `justify-content:center` over a fixed basis is one rule that is right at both. The basis is the top row's own third, so the two rows line up (measured: trinket 192.7 and bag 380 inside 99..655, dead centre). ⛑ **`align-items:flex-end` is what makes the lift a lift**: with the row aligned to its foot, a bottom margin on the armour raises that box and nothing else, and the row grows by exactly the margin - **109 -> 118**. A negative top margin would have been free and would have hung the chest out of the panel it is drawn inside. ⚠ **The row a slot belongs in is DERIVED**, from a two-field table keyed on the slot, and `SLOTS` is not re-sorted: a seventh slot declares itself in one row and nowhere else. `order` rather than a DOM shuffle, because *armour is authored first and stands second* is a special case as an insertion rule and a number beside the slot's name as a flex order. **⛔ THE STASH READS IN TWO, AND THE ROW WAS NEVER THE WIDTH.** (*"items in the inventory in two raws - there is too much free space"*.) A stash row is a 40px painting beside three short lines, the longest of them a mono receipt like `COMMON · ARMOUR · stops 12`; #246 widened this pane to 606 for the PERK TREE, which genuinely wants it. So the widest thing in the list has been sitting in about half the box ever since. Rows go **586 -> 290.5** and stand two up. ⛔ **`#iStashBody.on` and never `#iStashBody` bare**, which is one of this file's oldest traps in a new coat: `.itabbody{display:none}` is 0-1-0 and an id is 1-0-0, so a rule without the class would have shown the stash on the PERKS tab as well, permanently, with nothing in the stylesheet looking wrong. ⚠ **`align-content:start` is not decoration either** - a grid's auto rows stretch to fill their container, and this container is a 639px scroller, so a stash holding one thing would have drawn that thing 639px tall. The caption, the empty line and the BREAK door span both columns; a door in one of two columns reads as a third item. **⛔ AND THE UNDO WINDOW: THE REPORT AS WRITTEN WAS NOT REPRODUCIBLE, AND DRIVING IT IS WHAT FOUND THE REAL FAULT.** (*"When undo - it is 'undo with timer' - but you can make a move with your next unit already"*.) **You cannot act with another body while the bar runs.** `cur()` is still the mover, `clickHex` refuses anybody else, and the order rail carries no click at all - measured on the brigand field at 0.06s and 0.96s (the mover is up), with a click on Two Teeth leaving it on its own hex with both actions, and at 3.56s the turn has passed and the button is gone. ⛔ **What was actually wrong is that the click was EATEN.** A player who has decided not to take the step back does what anybody does: clicks the next body. That click fell through every branch of `clickHex` and did nothing - so the board answered a deliberate act with silence and then made him watch 3.15 seconds of bar. **A control that swallows input while showing a countdown reads as a hang**, which is the same complaint #249 was answering and it fixed only the half where the player pressed END TURN. ⛑ **So the window is a MAXIMUM and never a pause**: `undoGo` does now what the timer was going to do anyway. ⛔ **The turn is still not rewound** - #247's paragraph holds and this does not go near it: the handover happens exactly once, at a moment the player chose, and `B.undo` is dead BEFORE `beginTurn` runs rather than after it. **The reading that would have kept ⟲ alive across the handover was put to the user and is not what he asked for** (*"stop making me wait"*). ⚠ **It is only ever the emptied body**: a body with actions left carries an undo with NO hold, and a click on the board is that body's own next move - `u.actions<=0` is what tells them apart, and it is the same test `autoEnd` opened the window on. ⚡ **AND IT CAUGHT A SHIPPED DEFECT NOBODY HAD REPORTED: THE DRAIN BAR OUTLIVED ITS OWN WINDOW.** `.draining` was added by `undoDrain` and removed only by `undoMove`, so it survived every handover on the hidden button - and the NEXT offer, which is the untimed kind a body with an action left puts up, opened wearing a countdown that had already run out. **A timer drawn on an offer that has no timer is #102's wrong-unit rule with a clock in it.** Stripped whenever the offer is not up. ⚑ **AND A MEASUREMENT THAT WAS WRONG TWICE, WHICH IS THE PART OF THIS ENTRY WORTH KEEPING.** The first floor reading on the opening screen came back with **20 elements under the 10px floor** and `#prDlg` carrying `scale(0.965)`. the file's *“+2 baked into every weapon row”* without editing a hundred dice rows - so STRENGTH reaches damage through the ladder and nowhere else; and `D.bonus` is 0, with the NERVE RUNG carrying what the MORALE stat used to (STEADY +5%, IT ROCKS +10%), because what a body is FEELING is a state and not a stat. **A spell is no longer dodged** at the one site that charged `dodgeOf(d)×0.35` against an arcane act. **⚡ AND THE PRICE, MEASURED RATHER THAN GUESSED**: `ARENA.match('prepared',f,15)` on seven fights, before and after. | 8f.284 | **#259 - THREE CLASS CARDS WERE ADVERTISING AN ACT THE ENGINE DOES NOT BUILD, AND THE ONE SCREEN THEY ARE PRINTED ON IS WHERE A CLASS IS CHOSEN.** *(User, 2026-08-27: "Double check - that stats and skills actually give what they promise".)* ⛑ **THE AUDIT CAME BACK CLEAN EVERYWHERE THE NUMBERS LIVE, AND THE LIE WAS IN THE SALES PITCH.** All 26 perks are read by the engine (no dead key); every build-time figure matches its own text to the digit, measured by differential (`ironjaw` +12 moraleMax, `stoneskin` +10 and `thickhide` +18 armour, `quickfoot` +1 speed, `conduit` spellPow 1 -> 1.2, `opportune` flankPow 15 -> 25, `eagle` +2 range through `perks.indexOf`); every strike-time perk has exactly one implementation line carrying the number its `d` field states; and `STATLADDER` is honest on all nine rungs (`thews` equals the ladder's own melee/bow figure, `D.hit` equals GIVEN+ladder, `D.dodge` likewise, **0 mismatches across rungs -4..+4**). ⛔ **WHAT WAS WRONG IS `CLASSES[k].sig`, THE ONLY PLAYER-FACING STRING DESCRIBING A BODY'S ACTS THAT IS NOT DERIVED FROM THEM.** The captain's card sold RALLY THE LINE and `unitFrom` pushes HOLD THE LINE; the archer's sold AIMED SHOT and the cutter's BACKSTAB, both of which their own code comments record as deleted in favour of CRIPPLING SHOT and DISTRACT. The acts were replaced twice and the pitch beside them never moved. ⛑ **AND IT IS CHECKED NOW (LINT 6h), BY THE ENGINE RATHER THAN BY A SECOND TABLE**: a real body of each class is built through `unitFrom()` and every act its card claims must be in the row. ⛔ **THE CLAIM IS DECLARED (`sigActs`) AND NEVER PARSED OUT OF THE PROSE** - the first cut read capitalised runs out of `sig` and was wrong inside the hour, because "HOLD THE LINE, AND COMMAND FROM LEVEL 4" yields the fragment "AND COMMAND FROM LEVEL", which is not an act and never was. A card may say more than its acts (the mage's two sell a rule, not a verb), so no regex can tell a promise from a sentence. ⚠ **AND THE FIRST ATTEMPT TO PROVE THE CHECK REPORTED ZERO THREE TIMES RUNNING, WHICH WAS THE MEASUREMENT AND NOT THE CHECK**: `LINT()` returns an OBJECT (`{findings,byCategory,items}`) and the probe read `.length` off it, so every run scored 0 - the exact shape of the trap this repo writes down about checks that have only ever agreed with you, arriving on the instrument instead. Proved properly by seeding each of the three real historical lies and watching 6h name it, then restoring to silence. ⚡ Two rulings landed with it: **SPEARWOMAN becomes SPEAR** (a class is a trade and the roster puts men, women and ogres in it; the word is 5 characters narrower than the one every box was measured around, so nothing can overflow, and only ONE of the 20 occurrences in the file was player-facing - the rest are comments about a PERSON, which stay), and **COMMAND moves from level 3 to level 4**, matching the skill-tree lab. | |
| 8f.285 | **#260 - THE FIRST THREE GENERAL SKILLS GO IN, AND ONE OF THEM ONLY WORKS BECAUSE IT WAS PUT AT THE RIGHT DOOR.** *(User, 2026-08-27, from the skill-tree lab: "Everchanging luck +1 to the worst or to best stat at the begining of the battle. Do to the main build", plus BREAK AWAY and "For shield mastery: replace naming and description: Doubles your shield bonus".)* ⛔ **EVERCHANGING LUCK IS ADDED INSIDE `effStats` AND NOWHERE ELSE, AND THAT IS THE WHOLE ENTRY.** The obvious site is `unitFrom`, where the other perk figures live - and it would have been WRONG in a way nothing on screen would have shown: `hpMax` comes from `bodyHp(p)`, which re-derives from the roster body, so a rung added at `unitFrom` moves to-hit, dodge, flank and spell power and leaves the hitpoints exactly where they were. Measured both ways: placed at `effStats`, a roll onto STRENGTH takes the body **66 -> 75 hitpoints**; placed at `unitFrom` it takes them 66 -> 66 while the sheet still says the stat went up. That is #259's promise-versus-payment split, one day later, and this time it was caught before it shipped rather than after. ⚑ **WHICH stat is rolled ONCE, by `rollLuck`, at the top of the fight** (`G.party.forEach(rollLuck)` before the bodies are built) and parked on the roster body: `effStats` is called many times a frame and a roll inside it would shimmer. It reads `statParts` and not `effStats`, or the stat it lifted last battle would look like this battle's best. **Measured over 200 rolls: 105 worst / 95 best, and never once a middle stat.** ⚑ **BREAK AWAY has ONE reader.** `disHexes(u)` is what the act pays, what the float shouts and what the MOVE card prints, because the hex count appears at three sites and three hand-typed 1s is exactly how a perk comes to promise what it does not pay. Measured: `moveBudget` after disengaging goes **5 -> 6**. ⚑ **SHIELDWORK doubles the SHIELD'S OWN figure**, not a flat number, so a buckler is worth twice a buckler and a round shield twice a round shield (+4 -> +8, +5 -> +10, +9 -> +18 measured) and the perk never becomes a reason to carry the cheaper board. ⛑ **AND ALL THREE ARE REACHABLE, WHICH IS NOT AUTOMATIC**: `perkPool` concatenates `CLASSES[cls].perks[tier]`, so a perk no class lists is a row nobody can ever buy - the dead-key shape #259 checked for. They are joined onto every class's tiers 2, 4 and 6 in one loop rather than typed into seven rows by hand. ⚠ **The patch script's first run reported two MISSes and wrote nothing, and the guard is why that was cheap**: the prototype is CRLF and the multi-line patterns were LF. `LINT()` 0 findings after. | |

It is the identical trap #259 wrote `if(!B)return false` into `wallBlocked` for, missed here one entry later, and found only by DRIVING the function: the page booted clean, `LINT()` read 0, and the crash sat one click away on a screen no probe had opened. ⚑ **The belongs-to mark is DERIVED, not a third vocabulary.** A class skill wears that class's OWN tool glyph through `cIcon`, a race skill would wear the race's own face through `RACE_ICON`, and only GENERAL authors a glyph (👥, verified unused in the file) - #102's one-thing-one-symbol rule, so the mark can never disagree with the icon the same class wears everywhere else. `kind` is a FIELD on the perk and not a guess from which tier it sits in, because a general skill is offered by every class and would be indistinguishable from a class one by position alone. ⚑ **DUELLIST reads its condition at two sites and they ask the same question** (`threatsAt(...)===1`), measured live: one adjacent foe puts *duellist, one on one +5* in the sheet's own dodge breakdown (13 -> 16, the rest eaten by the existing stack cap); a second adjacent foe makes the row vanish. ⚡ **Two saves were made defensive in passing**: `p.perks.map(k=>PERKS[k].n)` had no guard, so an existing save holding a retired key would have thrown on the roster line. It filters now. ⏳ **TIERS 6 AND 8 ARE EMPTY FOR EVERY CLASS** and that is a known, temporary state of the teardown: a body past level 5 banks perk points with nothing to buy until the tree's own skills land. It degrades honestly - `perksLeft` answers 0 and the sheet prints "Nothing to learn". | |
| 8f.287 | **#262 - THE SKILL TREE LANDS: TWENTY-EIGHT SKILLS IN THREE LANES, AND THE SHEET SHOWED TWO OF SEVENTEEN FOR AN HOUR.** *(User, 2026-08-27, with a screenshot of a three-row perk list: "no it is almost empty. Take skills logic from the file - there are class, race, and general skills - want to see them in new build".)* The tree in `tools/skilltree_lab.html` is the build's now: **11 class · 5 race · 12 general**, each row carrying `kind`, which is both what the card's mark is drawn from and what the pool gates on. ⚑ **ELEVEN OF THE TWENTY-EIGHT WERE RESTORED RATHER THAN WRITTEN, AND THAT IS #261's DECISION PAYING OFF.** That entry retired their rows but deliberately left the engine lines standing, so BRACED WALL, KILLING ZONE, SURE SHOT, EAGLE EYE, SHADOW-STEP, BUTCHER, BRAWN, THICK HIDE, STEADYING and WAR-LEADER came back as a row and a tier, on the mechanics that were already tested. `docs/archive/RETIRED_SKILLS.md` is what made that a lookup instead of an excavation. ⛔ **A RACE LANE IS STRUCTURALLY NEW.** `perkPool` had only ever asked about a CLASS, so a race skill was unreachable however many rows the table grew - the dead-row shape this file has now paid for three times. `RACEPERKS` and `GENPERKS` are the other two lanes. ⛔ **AND THE ENTRY'S OWN BUG IS THE ONE WORTH KEEPING: TEACHING THE POOL IS NOT TEACHING THE SHEET.** `perkPool` learned all three lanes and was measured correct (a level-9 ogre spearman is offered 17). The PERK CARD RENDERER never asked it: it walks `CLASSES[p.cls].perks` directly, so the sheet drew the class lane alone and that same body was SHOWN two cards. The pool was right and the screen was wrong, which is exactly the user's complaint one layer down. **`perksAt(p,l)` and `tiersFor(p)` are the one reader now** and `perkPool`, the card renderer and the class-tree hover all call them, so what a body is offered and what it is shown cannot come apart again. ⚠ `tiersFor` is the UNION of the three lanes' own keys rather than a literal 2/4/6/8, which is #174's rule about a tier list going stale one lane wider; `PERKTIERS` rose to 8 with it, or BERSERK would have been authored above the ceiling and silently unbuyable. ⛑ **EVERY SKILL WAS MEASURED, NOT ASSUMED.** Build-time by differential: COLOSSUS 66 -> 79 hp, FORTIFIED MIND 110 -> 130 morale, THICK HIDE 34 -> 52 armour, STRONG str 2 -> 3 AND hp 66 -> 75 (the `effStats` door from #260 doing its job), NINE LIVES `stands` false -> true, GOOD HAND range 5 -> 6 and EAGLE EYE 5 -> 7 stacking to 8, VERSATILE +5 with a dirk in the off hand and **+0 with a shield**, which is the promise read exactly. Damage by multiplier: base 1.25, TWO-HANDED 1.75 against armour, RAGE 1.47 below half and 1.25 above it, FRENZY 1.45 after a kill and 1.25 cold. **THICK MUSCLES was swept over all six facings**: a plain body takes +5/+15/+30 by arc and the thick-muscled one takes 0 from every one. ⚑ **It goes inside `arcOn` and not at its two call sites**, because `hitBreakdown` PRINTS the arc and `strike` PAYS it, and patching one of two is how a preview and a blow come to disagree. ⛑ **AND THEN SIX REAL FIGHTS WITH ALL TWENTY-EIGHT ON EVERY BODY**, which is the only thing that exercises the kill site, FEARSOME's wound hook and BERSERK's action refund on a real board: 6/6 wins at 6.5 rounds against a 6/6 at 7.0 baseline, no throw, `LINT()` 0. ⏳ **SEVEN LAB SKILLS ARE NOT HERE AND THE REASON IS THE SAME FOR ALL SEVEN**: ROTATION, TAUNT, MAGIC POTION, SECOND WIND, UNNOTICEABLE, TWO WEAPONS HANDLER and FAR HAND each need a new ACT or an AI targeting hook rather than a number on an existing line. Listing them without wiring them would be the broken promise #259 spent its whole audit deleting. | |
| 8f.288 | **#263 - THE OTHER SIDE CLIMBS THE SAME FOUR LADDERS, AND IT WAS ALREADY HALFWAY UP ONE NOBODY HAD CHOSEN.** *(User, 2026-08-28: "use this logic with stats and aply all 4 stats also for enemies. So they also get some of the bufs. We can imagine, that each enemy have influence of all this 4 stats and in the card in batlfield we will write this icon. Why I want to use it - in the future add bufs and debufs of it completly on the battlfield", then "if enemies stats and behavior not together - I think it is a good idea to groop it in main build", and then "but of course, enemy have thier own basic stats, that could be a bit lover then the party".)* ⛔ **EVERY FOE ALREADY CARRIED `st` AND IT WAS THE SAME FOUR NUMBERS FOR ALL 61 OF THEM** - `{str:-2,agi:-2,int:-1,mor:-2}`, typed into `build()`, with `int` the only overridable field and exactly one template overriding it. So the ladders were ALREADY reaching the enemy through every shared function - `thews` gave every body in the game a **x0.85 arm**, the mood `loss` and `climb` rungs, `D.arc` for a caster - and reaching them with a number nobody had chosen. `GIVEN`'s own note said the other half out loud: *"THE ENEMY DOES NOT MOVE WITH THEM"*. ☑ **A FOE'S RUNG IS `FOE_LEAN[race]` PLUS THE TEMPLATE'S OWN `lean:{}`**, which is the roster's *born + race + class + trait* said smaller, and the three people-races mirror `RACEMOD` exactly so a clan ratkin leans the way your ratkin do. A beast is quick, dim and unbothered; `unknown` leans nowhere. Then `hp`, `mskill`, `dodgeBase` and `moraleMax` read the rung at `build()`, after the trims and the champion multiplier, and the three capstones reach the unit off the same `holds()` `unitFrom` uses. ⛔ **AND THE GIVENS ARE NOT SHARED, WHICH IS THE USER'S THIRD NOTE AND IS NOW A MEASUREMENT RATHER THAN AN ACCIDENT.** Your people start from `GIVEN` and climb; an enemy starts from its own statblock and climbs the same rungs. Averaged over the **55 ordinary templates** (no captain, no boss, no `noTrim`): hitpoints **49.1 against 60 (-18%)**, to hit **53.3 against 70 (-16.7)**, nerve **62.7 against 90 (-27.3)**, and dodge **16.5 against 14**, the one column they lead - correct, because it is all a ratkin in rags has. That is before `TRIM` takes another 10%, `ARM` a quarter of the harness, and `HIT_EASE` tilts the roll a net +5 your way. ⛔ **There is deliberately no `FOE_GIVEN` constant**: one number behind 61 hand-tuned statblocks would be a second author for figures each of which is somebody's measurement, and the knob for the tilt already exists and is `HIT_EASE`. ⛑ **THE STATBLOCKS ARE ONE TABLE NOW (`FOE_T`), WHICH IS THE SECOND ASK.** It was fourteen tables in fourteen places: six had module-level names and eight were a `const T={}` inside their own plan function, so *what does the other side look like* could not be answered without opening eight functions and knowing which eight. **The move changed nothing and that is proved rather than claimed**: `probes/foeoracle263.js` builds every `FOE_BUILD[k]()` side on both builds and compares 19 fields a body - **0 drift on all 14 deterministic fights**. ⚠ `steading` drifts against ITSELF, because it rolls three of four ogres for its wall; that is the probe's own note now. ⛑ **THE CARD CARRIES THE FOUR MARKS**, `STAT_ICON` at 32px (never 24 - #230: a 3:4 resample smears a pixel painting), keyed on the band, which since #254 IS the rung, with `tell()`'s own word on the hover and **no number**, which is the MOOD row's rule two blocks up. ⛔ **`noFace` is refused its marks**: the Warden is a thing you cannot read, and four pictures saying how strong it is would answer the question the whole encounter is built on refusing. Driven, not read - `probes/statmark263.js` opens the real `inspect()` card over a foe, one of yours and the Warden: **4 / 4 / 0 marks, 32px drawn, inside the stage, 0 under the floor, 0 clipped**. ⛑ **AND THE SEAM THE WHOLE ASK WAS FOR IS ONE LINE.** `statRung(u,k)` reads `u.st` plus `u.stMod`, and `heldOf` goes through it - so the day a card or a working writes `stMod`, the weight of a blow, how fast a mood goes and comes back, what it takes to land a working and the four marks all follow with nothing added. ⚠ **The baked four do NOT follow it** and the comment says so: re-deriving a POOL mid-fight would have to decide what happens to the damage already out of it. ⚠ **`statRung` and not `rungOf`, and the collision was not theoretical**: `rungOf` is already the battle's MOOD ladder six thousand lines down, and the first cut threw *Identifier 'rungOf' has already been declared*. Two ladders, two meanings, one name is #102's wrong-unit bug waiting in a stack trace. ⚠ **THE BOSSES ARE AUTHORED AND ONE OF THEM COST FOUR DICE ROWS.** The Fen-Mother takes `st:{str:3,agi:0,int:-2,mor:4}` - and her rungs feed `thews` live, x0.85 to **x1.20**, so her dice come DOWN x0.71 (anvil 22-33 -> 16-23, lash 16-25 -> 11-18, gorge 30-44 -> 21-31, drag 24-36 -> 17-25) and she hits for exactly what she always hit for. **That is the point of authoring a boss on the ladder**: her numbers now say she is enormously strong and afraid of nothing, which is what the fight is about, and the damage is unchanged. The Warden is authored to the OLD default, so it is the one row in the re-stat that moves nothing at all. ⚡ **WHAT IT DID TO THE ROAD, `ARENA.match` n=15 a side against the same build without it.** The starting four: **brigand 80 -> 53%**, **pack 100 -> 80**, **slingline 40 -> 27**, tavern 93 -> 87, hold 20 -> 13. A prepared six: **circle 100 -> 53%**, **steading 53 -> 20**, snare 87 -> 80, mirehares 100 -> 93, and the eight early fights still 100 but slower and bloodier. ⛔ **AND THE ONE FIGHT `OPEN_QUESTIONS.md` O1 IS ABOUT DID NOT MOVE: THE SNARE IS STILL 93% FOR THE STARTING FOUR AT n=30** (28/30, 0.6 down). The reason is in the table: the ratkin lean gives +4 to hit and +20% on the swing and takes 5% of the hitpoints and 10 of the nerve, so on the one deck made almost entirely of ratkin it is close to a wash. **O1 still needs its own answer**, and this entry is not it. ⚠ **The re-stat made the long fights long enough to break the instrument**: `eyes.py` opens its CDP socket with `timeout=120` on the socket ITSELF, so any single eval over two minutes dies on `recv` whatever `--timeout` says, and four stacked matrix calls wedged the page. `matrix263.js` accumulates six fight-comps a call and says where it is. **Gates:** `LINT()` **0** · `gt.py check` 3 scripts 0 problems · the oracle 0 drift · `regress.js` 8/8 no ERR/FATAL/GUARD · `smoke.js` **0 throws** across 40 road cards, 19 camps, 59 gear hovers and every foe hover · floor and clip `[]` on the sheet and the documented set in battle · map 0/0/0 · no em dash. | |
| 8f.289 | **#264 - THE RACE LANES FILL OUT, THE LIST GOES TWO COLUMNS, AND GOOD HAND CANNOT BE WHAT WAS ASKED FOR.** *(User, 2026-08-28, on the shipped tree: "it should be definently more racial skills - now i see only 1", "You can write skills in 2 raws", and "Good Hand: this should work only for throwbles, +1 range and +50% damage to hitpoints from throwbles".)* ⚑ **SEVEN MORE RACE SKILLS AND NOT ONE LINE OF NEW ENGINE.** The lanes were 1/1/3 - a human or a ratkin was offered a SINGLE race skill in a whole career - and they are 4/4/4 now: human STUBBORN, SECOND WIND and PATIENT; ratkin SCURRY, PACK TACTICS and SLIP AWAY; ogre STONE HIDE. Every one of them is a key whose engine line has been standing since #261 retired the ROW and deliberately left the code, so a race lane three deep cost seven table rows. **The name is the race's and the key is the engine's**: `quickfoot` pays a hex of movement whatever it is called, and renaming the KEY would have thrown the working line away. ⛔ **AND THE ONE THING I ADDED THAT WAS NOT NEEDED IS THE FINDING.** `armourMax` ALREADY read `stoneskin` - that is an engine line, and #261 left every engine line alone - so wiring it again paid the perk TWICE: **+20 armour on a card that says 10, measured 34 -> 54 where 44 was wanted.** Only the differential caught it; nothing threw and `LINT()` read 0 with the bug in. ⚑ **The rule under it: restoring a retired perk means restoring its ROW, and nothing else. Check whether the engine line exists before wiring one**, because the half of the pair that was deleted is not the half you remember. ⚑ **THE LIST RUNS IN TWO COLUMNS** as a grid on the container rather than a rewrite of the card, so a card can never break across the fold the way `column-count` would let it, and the LEVEL headings span both. Measured: two distinct left edges at 45 and 333, `283.5px 283.5px`, headings at column 1. ⛔ **GOOD HAND WAS NOT CHANGED, AND THE REASON IS THAT THE GAME HAS NO THROWABLE.** `slot:'bag'` is a deliberately EMPTY promise - the code's own note says *"NOTHING IN GEAR{} CARRIES slot:'bag' YET, on purpose"* - and the only throw in the build is the ogre's own racial PICK UP AND THROW. Rewritten as asked, the perk would pay exactly nothing to anybody except an ogre, which is the dead-promise class #259's audit spent itself deleting. It is left as the working ranged perk and the decision is the user's: build throwables first, park GOOD HAND with the other seven act-shaped skills, or keep it ranged. ⛑ Verified: 35 skills, **11 class · 12 race · 12 general**, `LINT()` 0, every new key differential-measured (STONE HIDE +10 and +28 stacked with THICK HIDE, SCURRY +1 hex, PACK TACTICS +10 flank), and six fights with all 35 on every body at 6/6 and 5.0 rounds against a 5/6 at 6.5 baseline. | |
| 8f.290 | **#265 - THE OTHER SIDE BREAKS LESS EASILY, A CHAMPION IS FOUR LEVELS ON, AND THE SWEEP FOUND A FIGHT THAT HAD GONE OFF THE BOTTOM OF ITS OWN SCALE.** *(User, 2026-08-28: "i feel we need to add more morale for enemies, similar to us. So it is a little bit harder to break them", "And then run polishing sequence one more time - ensure things works properly", and "For a champion - it could be 4 the lvl - so 2 random perks from that pool and at least 1 stat up".)* ⛑ **THE NERVE IS ONE KNOB AND NOT 61 EDITS**, which is `HIT_EASE`'s own answer to the same shape of question: what a row AUTHORS is how steady that body is against the others on its side, and what the two SIDES are worth against each other is decided in one place. `FOE_NERVE` is **1.25**, and it is a MULTIPLIER rather than a flat add because the authored pools run **24 to 120** - a flat +16 is +67% on a wedding guest and +13% on a mirehare, and would quietly delete the one thing the wedding deck is tuned around. ⚡ **Measured, both sides built: your six average a 101.7 pool against the road's 63.6, which is -37.5%; at 1.25 the road reads 79.4, which is -21.9%** - closer, and still the lesser side, which is *"a little bit harder"* read exactly rather than *the same*. The ladder is FRACTIONAL, so a quarter more pool is a quarter more morale damage before BREAKING and nothing else about how they break has moved. ⛔ A `noTrim` boss is exempt: 460 already means *it does not react*. ⛑ **AND A CHAMPION IS NOW A BODY FOUR LEVELS ON, WHICH IS WHAT THE MULTIPLIERS WERE ALWAYS TRYING TO SAY.** #245's rule is *one of these, but better*, as one word on a plan row; the multipliers stay the BODY and the same word now also buys what four levels buy on your own roster - `levelUp` alternates a perk point and a stat point, so level 4 is two perks and two stat points, and the ask names two perks and *at least* one rung. **The perks are rolled from the body's own lanes** (race + general, which is `perksAt` minus the class half a foe does not have) and **the rung is DERIVED, not rolled**, because which stat is the one thing here that is not a coin toss: a shooter is better quicker, a caster cleverer, everything that closes and swings stronger. A rolled rung would put INTELLECT on a dog. ⛔ **AND THIRTEEN OF THE 28 PERKS WOULD HAVE PAID A CHAMPION NOTHING**, because they are read off `p.perks` inside `effStats`/`bodyHp`/`unitFrom` - the ROSTER path, which no foe takes. That is exactly the promise #259's audit was run to delete and #260 was caught by. ⛑ **So the answer was to PAY them rather than to ban them**: `foePerks()` is the foe's copy of those build-time lines (COLOSSUS, FORTIFIED MIND, STONE HIDE, SCURRY, PACK TACTICS, NINE LIVES, GOOD HAND and EVERCHANGING LUCK), which takes a human champion's pool from **three keys to five** and lets a champion come up tanky, lucky or long-armed instead of always the same. ⚠ **Three are deliberately still absent and the comment says why**: DISENGAGE would put a new ACT on a body neither AI brain has been taught to spend one on (#224's greyed-act trap), and SHIELDWORK and VERSATILE both read a piece of GEAR a foe has no `eq` to hold. ⛔ **AND THE POLISHING SWEEP EARNED ITS KEEP: `snarejoin` MEASURED 0/20 ON A FIGHT ITS OWN TABLE TUNES TO 40-53%.** Three builds, n=20 a side, same instrument: **30% before #263, 15% after it, 0% after #265**. The Hold host is nineteen coats of the two bodies that gained most from both changes, and #263's own matrix never covered it - `snarejoin` is not on the road list. ⛑ **The fix is the dial that function's own note already names** (*"one bill is worth twenty-seven points of win rate and a crossbow is worth nothing measurable"*), turned the other way for the first time: three bills out - the two the party-size clause used to add, and one from the wall - landing on the row that table already measured, **17 coats, 5 bills, 3 bows**. It reads **8/20, 40%**, which is #245's own figure. ⚠ **At n=15 the same build read 33% and the monkey-patched sweep read 55%**: this is the high-variance fight its note warns about (*"the first three passes here read as noise"*), so it is measured at 20 or it is not measured. ⚠ **The first cut of that sweep was WRONG and reported 57% where the fight reads 0**, because the reconstructed plan omitted `holdHost`'s `>=6` clause and measured a 15-body host against the real 19. **A tuning sweep that rebuilds its subject has to rebuild all of it.** ⚠ **The ranking against the honest finale cannot be restored here**: that note wants this road slightly easier than `snare`, and `snare` has drifted to ~80% on this instrument, which is O1 and not something this function can fix by losing bodies. **Gates, run twice over:** `LINT()` **0** · `gt.py check` 3/0 · **the oracle says only `morale` and `moraleMax` moved**, on every fight, with the Warden byte-identical and `snarejoin` correctly 19 -> 16 bodies · `regress.js` 8/8 no ERR/FATAL/GUARD · `smoke.js` **0 throws** · the card driven again: 4/4/0 marks, 0 under the floor, 0 clipped · floor and clip `[]` · map 0/0/0 · no em dash. ⚠ **The longer fights broke the instrument again** - fewer routs means more fights fought to the end - so the road matrix had to drop to a nine-row focused sweep at n=12; at that size every row but `snarejoin` is inside the noise. | |

## 8f.105 - the removed send block, kept whole for restore

*Here because 8f.105 removed a working feature to test a guess, and a guess that turns out wrong
must cost one paste to undo. This is the code exactly as it stood at 8f.104, in
`prototype/grimtoll_slice.html`, inside `drawNotes()`. Restoring it means putting all four pieces
back and deleting the `nSend` button and its handler.*

**1. The address, above `const POLL=[`:**

```js
const FEEDBACK_TO='dmytriyvihrov@gmail.com';
```

**2. The href, after `const L=own?letter():null;`:**

```js
/* built once so the length can be checked before the anchor is written */
const mailHref=(()=>{
  if(!L)return '';
  const full='mailto:'+FEEDBACK_TO+'?subject='+encodeURIComponent(L.subject)+
    '&body='+encodeURIComponent(L.body);
  if(full.length<=1900)return full;
  return 'mailto:'+FEEDBACK_TO+'?subject='+encodeURIComponent(L.subject)+
    '&body='+encodeURIComponent('RABBLEBOUND, playtest notes'+
      (JOURNAL.who?' from '+JOURNAL.who:'')+
      '\n\nIt was too long to put in the link. It is all on my clipboard. Pasting it here:\n\n');
})();
```

**3. The markup, where the `nSend` button now sits:**

```js
'<a class="popt sendit big" id="nMail" href="'+esc(mailHref)+'">'+
  '<b>✉ SEND IT TO ME</b><i>opens your mail addressed to '+esc(FEEDBACK_TO)+
  ', with the answers already written · the journal goes on your clipboard in the '+
  'same click, paste it under the line</i></a>'+
'<p style="margin-top:11px">No mail on this machine? Copy the box and send it to <b>'+
esc(FEEDBACK_TO)+'</b> any way you like. '+
'It is plain text — nothing in it but what is on this page and what you typed.</p>'+
```

**4. The handler, where the `nSend` handler now sits:**

```js
const mail=$('nMail');
if(mail)mail.onclick=()=>{
  try{ta.focus();ta.select();document.execCommand('copy');
    if(navigator.clipboard&&navigator.clipboard.writeText)
      navigator.clipboard.writeText(ta.value).catch(()=>{});}catch(e){}
  const note=mail.querySelector('i');
  if(note)note.textContent='the journal is on your clipboard. Paste it under the line.';
};
```

**And one line inside `letter()`**, whose tail read
`'\n\n----------\nThe full journal is on my clipboard. Pasting it below.\n\n'`
before the clipboard started carrying both halves at once.
